- `SP` = `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-tangyunpei-dev-OpenAlpaca/4b1ee495-f087-45a9-9fa8-4325993552c8/scratchpad/oct11` — today's scratch (`reports/`, `logs/`, `tools/`; `patches/` is empty — no group produced a patch today [M]; the compile check's edited copy is `SP/wt-afit/`). `SP10` = the same path ending `oct10`, yesterday's. Both are **temporary**; Appendix A names what to copy where.
- **The scope the owner set on 2026-10-11, and the three buckets every item falls into.** The owner removed CLAUDE.md's *Simplicity First* rule for this work — major modification of the codebase is in scope — and *Minimal Impact* now means "touch what the modernization needs and nothing unrelated". So: **work** = removing a deprecated usage, replacing an old API with its stable successor, or adopting a new feature that improves something concrete — scheduled as a commit with sites, effort, risk and a gate, the recommended default being "do it"; **DU** (owner decision) = only what CLAUDE.md's settled rule still reserves — a change that **adds an enforcement point** (a lint gate, a CI or bun gate, a required tool, a hook), **widens a component's job** (a new crate or dependency, a moved boundary), **reverses a row of an accepted design**, or **changes user-visible behaviour or an on-disk or wire format** — presented with options and a recommendation, the shipped code and configuration being the "no change" answer; **not to do** = the settled rules (memory search stays lexical; the approval hidden-character escape set and its tables; `test-home-guard`; the schema line; the MCP tool-governance axes), listed in §3.5 with the rule each would break. Volume alone is not a DU trigger; where a work item improves nothing concrete and its churn is large, this document says so and schedules it last, droppable.
- `plan-rev3 §N`, `P36`, `Q06`, `DU-17`, `E3`, `G-lint`, `G-CI`, `G-GUI`, `G-bundle` refer to `tasks/deps-upgrade/plan-rev3.md` at its current revision (rev 3.13 when rev 2.2 was written, rev 3.15 when rev 2.4 was). Rev 1 cited it at rev 3.2, and its line numbers have moved since: re-grep a `plan-rev3.md:N` before relying on it. The four groups' own numbering (OD-1…7, DU-A…D, RM-1/5/7/8, DW-1…9, W1…W20) is kept in the archived reports; §5 opens with the crosswalk.
- A `.` in a number is a decimal point; GiB is 2^30 bytes (the groups' `du -sk` ÷ 1,048,576); "paths" counts a hard-linked file once per name, as `cargo clean` does [D: §1.1]; a "triple" is a unique `(file, line, lint)` from `cargo clippy --message-format=json` whose primary span lies under `crates/` or `apps/`.

## 0. Summary

**Rev 2.0 note.** The owner's rule of 2026-10-12 (`CLAUDE.md:52`) re-answers the decisions whose taken recommendation was a compromise (§5, each entry's *Rev 2.0* paragraph; §6.2) and adds M58 (a–j) and M59–M84 (§6.1); where this summary says "last and droppable", "not scheduled" or "recommend no/none", the entry's rev 2.0 text decides.

**Rev 2.3 note.** The ZCode prior-art audit (`tasks/research/zcode-audit-2026-10-17.md`) reshapes M84 as a form (still Conditional), amends M14, M15, M60, M67 and M77, adds pointers to M07, M13, M19–M38 (M36), M39, M40, M44, M58, M63, M65, M66, M70, M74, M76, M78, M82 and M83, adds M85 (MCP images, audio and blobs kept as by-products; Conditional on the new owner question DU-R6) and M86 (the owner's taken D-I (revised): the plugin LLM provider deleted, before M58 (e)), records three sub-questions (DU-D3's interpreter pin, DU-R3's suppression ratchet, DU-R4 (i)'s sandbox clock) and M86's own question (a `plugin:` section left in `llm.toml`) and gives its thirty new fixes (N-LEAD, N-MCP, N-TOOL, N-SKILL, N-PLUG, N-DAEMON, N-STORE, N-GUI) their places in §6.3, counted in neither part. The programme's size (§6.4) becomes 104 commits, 93 under the answers taken; the decisions in §5 become 23.

**Rev 2.4 note.** The owner answered the review's D1–D3 on 2026-10-17, all (a): the cut at or after 2026-10-21T19:53Z (12:53 PDT), with Tauri's 2026-10-14 family; rustls 0.23.46 eligible at it, so D2's waiver never triggers; Rust 1.99.0 at P03 and 1.99.1 at P03b from 2026-10-23T04:47:44Z. The cut-day compatibility report (`reports/2026-10-17-cut-compatibility.md`) found every package of that set compatible on Rust 1.99.0 and Bun 1.4.3, with fixes to Part I's text; for this file it gives the versions the wave lands that Part III's entries are written over (§4.1 row 6, §4.3, M11, M80), the cut-day registry facts of Part III's new dependencies (M58, M66, M67, M69, M79, M82), the clock rule for a toolchain and P03b's second pin move, after which a second `cargo clean` is recommended and presented to the owner, not adopted (§6's conventions, M01's action, DU-M4, §6.2, §6.3 steps 1a and 2) and evidence for the still-pending D4 (§2.1 R-13, M36). No Part III commit is added or re-decided; Part I gains P03b (§6.4).

**The scope, as the owner set it.** On 2026-10-11 the owner asked for three things: (1) *embrace all the new APIs and new features* of the dependencies, (2) *stop using any deprecated API or feature* anywhere in the codebase, (3) stop the repository's `target/` directory from growing to 150–200 GB — that evening's `cargo clean` removed **530,884 files, 147.6 GiB**, about 292 KiB per path — and they removed CLAUDE.md's *Simplicity First* rule because this work needs major modification; *Minimal Impact* now means touch what the modernization needs and nothing unrelated. This document is therefore a **plan**, not a candidate list: **(a)** a complete deprecated-usage inventory with its removal scheduled as commits (§2, §6); **(b)** the new APIs and features to adopt, scheduled as commits with a recommended default of "do it" — the owner decides only the items CLAUDE.md's settled rule reserves (an enforcement point, a widened component, a reversed design row, a behaviour or format change; §5); **(c)** a measured diagnosis of the 147.6 GiB target directory with its mitigations, the dev-loop ones as DUs (§1, §5.1). Where the groups wrote "owner decision" out of caution rather than because a rule-5 trigger applies, this document reclassifies the item as work and says so; where they disagree, it says which evidence wins and why. A "no site" row means the grep found no site; a DU means the rule required one.

**The headline results.**

|---|---|---|
| How many deprecated APIs does the tree use? | **One in Rust**: `Atomic::<u64>::fetch_update` → `try_update` at `crates/openalpaca_core/src/session_log/mod.rs:519`, reported by rustc 1.99.0 only (`try_update` is 1.95+ [Docs: RELEASES.md 1.95.0, `SP/logs/releases-194-199.txt:257`, `:292-298`], so the fix lands after P03: **M04**). **The boundary of that "one"** (§2.4): every 1.99 clippy and check run today excluded the Tauri shell (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri` — 0 spans in `depr-clippy-199.json`; steps 12 and 16 of §1.2 ran `--exclude openalpaca_gui`) and compiled `tests/memory_eval.rs` (13,210 lines) as an empty crate root, its body being gated whole by `#![cfg(feature = "live-eval")]` (`:43`) — built today under 1.93.0 only (step 6); the shell's only 1.99.0 compiles are yesterday's, on the Tauri-2.12.3 and P04-held locks, 0 warnings and 0 clippy triples [M-oct10: `gui-D-check.log`, `gui-D-clippy.json`, `held-05-ggui-check-held.log`]. `deprecated_in_future` forced on: 0. Our own `#[deprecated]`: 0. `#[allow(deprecated)]`: 0. Let chains (1.88) have a real list: the 17 `collapsible_if` sites (M07). `#[async_trait]` → native `async fn` in traits is blocked for **14 of 15 traits** by `dyn` dispatch; the one convertible trait, `Connector` (5 of 211 sites), **compiled clean today** under the pinned 1.93.0 (M13). The React Compiler compiles the app (healthcheck 295/295) but the shipping oxc compiler **skips 13 locations** (15 diagnostics) the healthcheck does not report, costs **+18.2 % JS bytes** (+135,378 B over today's Vite-8-alone 742,230 B; the Babel host +12.1 %), and has no measured benefit here (DU-W1). **What this document asks the owner** — §5: **DU-M1…M6** (the target directory: the canonical invocation set, incremental off for agent builds, a sweep tool, toolchain hygiene as a tool, `build.build-dir`, the profile levers), **DU-D1…D4** (ESLint and a lint gate, knip as a gate, a Python floor, `erasableSyntaxOnly`; rev 1's DU-D5 — the widened P36 — is dissolved in rev 1.1: clippy hygiene is work, §2.5), **DU-R1…R5** (`dynosaur`, `file-lock` → std, pedantic lints at `warn`, the rmcp surfaces after P19 — its (v) the call path of protocol cancellation, M15, and its (vi) `Tool.output_schema` — tower-http's new layers), **DU-W1…W7** (the React Compiler, `<Activity>`, View Transitions, `useSuspenseQuery`, the scrollbar utilities, `bunfig.toml`'s `minimumReleaseAge`, Trusted Types) — **22 decisions** (23 since rev 2.3: DU-R6, M85's; and three sub-questions inside DU-D3, DU-R3 and DU-R4, §5). Everything else is scheduled work (§6). The recommended answers are: yes to DU-M1 (b), DU-M2 (a), DU-R2 and DU-W6; "no tool / no change" to DU-M3…M6, DU-D4, DU-R1, DU-R3, DU-R5, DU-W1…W5 and DU-W7; "later, decided together" for DU-D1 and DU-D2; "none, with one sentence" for DU-D3; and per item for DU-R4 ((v) yes, the raw path; (vi) defer); and, since rev 2.3, yes ((b)) to DU-R6, M85's, pending.

## 1. The target directory

**Source:** the target-attribution group's report (`SP/reports/target-attribution.md`) and its data (`SP/logs/attr-A-sizes.tsv`, `attr-B-sizes.tsv`, `attr-A-composition-full-after-13.txt`, `-after-15.txt`, `attr-B-composition-full.txt`, `levers-storage.tsv`, `levers-toy.tsv`, `churn-storage.tsv`, `toy-builddir.log`, `clean-gc-dryrun.log`, `toy-feature-unification.log`, `toy-backtrace-splitdebuginfo.log`, `cvi-cargo-invocations-instructions.txt`; tools `SP/tools/{measure,attr-driver,attr-extra,composition,levers,levers2,churn,toy-checks}.sh`, `analyze.py`, `compare.py`). Machine: Apple M5 Max, 18 cores, 128 GiB, APFS; `cargo 1.93.0 (083ac5135 2025-12-15)` (the pin), `1.98.1 (797e8a9bc 2026-08-05)`, `1.99.0 (5f94df478 2026-08-27)`; every call `nice -n 10 env CARGO_TERM_COLOR=never CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=8 CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<SP/target-attr-*> cargo +<toolchain> …`, one at a time, in the detached worktree `SP/wt-attr` of `7295f91b`; the worktree restored to `git status` clean and every `target-attr-*` directory (≈ 36 GiB at peak) deleted afterwards [M]. Wall times are this machine's under sibling agents' load and are indicative only.

### 1.1 What the owner's number can and cannot tell us

The owner's `cargo clean` removed **530,884 files, 147.6 GiB** — 147.6 × 2^30 / 530,884 ≈ 298.5 kB ≈ **292 KiB per path**. Both figures are per *path*: cargo 1.93.0's `CleanContext::rm_rf` adds 1 to `num_files_removed` and `meta.len()` to `total_bytes_removed` for every non-directory `walkdir` entry, and its own comment says it over-counts hard-linked files and under-counts block sizes [D: `rust-lang/cargo@083ac5135` `src/cargo/ops/cargo_clean.rs:455-521`, fetched to `SP/logs/fetch/rev2/cargo_clean-083ac5135.rs`] — so 530,884 is a count of names (a hard-linked object once per name, as the `paths` column below) and 147.6 GiB is an upper bound on the unique bytes. That directory is gone and cannot be decomposed. The experiment below reconstructs the drivers from the commands the repository documents (CLAUDE.md, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, the two eval READMEs, the three package scripts, the Tauri sidecar hook, `docs/`), on **one** lock state and **one** source tree. Three things it therefore cannot reproduce, each [NE] in §1.11: months of `Cargo.lock`, feature and source churn leaving stale variants behind (cargo removes nothing from `target/debug` on its own); an editor's rust-analyzer (step 10 runs its exact command, but no rust-analyzer is installed on this machine); and agent sessions building in the repository with toolchains, features or git worktrees not listed here. What the number's *shape* does say: 292 KiB per path sits between experiment A's 370 KiB per path after the 13 task steps (392 after the 11 documented steps) and B's 612 KiB after its 11 steps — consistent with a long-lived incremental target that has seen many edits, and inconsistent with "a few big binaries" (which reads in MB per path) [M: §1.4, §1.7].

### 1.2 Experiment A — the documented commands on 1.93.0, incremental on [M: `attr-A-sizes.tsv`, `attr-A-NN-*.log`]

Each row is the state of the target **after** the step; `GiB` is `du -sk` of the whole target (hard links counted once), `Δ` the step's growth, `paths` `find -type f | wc -l` (a hard-linked file once per name, as `cargo clean` counts), `stems` the distinct `<crate>-<16 hex>` metadata variants of `openalpaca_core` / `openalpaca_storage` / `tokio` in `deps/`, `exe` the executables in `deps/` (test binaries plus the two bin targets' hashed copies), `s-dirs` the incremental session directories. Steps 12, 13 and 16 carry `--locked` (1.99.0 accepts the lock unchanged [M-oct10]); steps 14–16 are the group's additions beyond the task's thirteen.

| step | command | wall | GiB | Δ GiB | paths | Δ paths | KiB/Δpath | stems core/stor/tokio | exe | exe GiB | s-dirs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `cargo build` (bare: the 8 default-members) | 37 s | 1.40 | +1.40 | 5,140 | +5,140 | 286 | 1/1/1 | 0 | 0 | 8 |
| 2 | `cargo build --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui` | 58 s | 3.60 | +2.20 | 10,866 | +5,726 | 403 | 2/2/1 | 2 | 0.18 | 18 |

| 4 | `cargo clippy --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets` | 36 s | 8.49 | +1.27 | 23,132 | +2,304 | 577 | 5/5/3 | 20 | 0.85 | 72 |

| 9 | `cargo test -p openalpacad --no-run` | 43 s | 12.57 | +1.70 | 33,610 | +3,449 | 516 | 7/8/4 | 26 | 1.29 | 94 |
| 10 | rust-analyzer's default: `cargo check --workspace --message-format=json-diagnostic-rendered-ansi --all-targets --keep-going` (includes `openalpaca_gui`) | 38 s | 13.89 | +1.33 | 35,488 | +1,878 | 741 | 9/10/5 | 26 | 1.29 | 127 |
| 11 | one blank line appended to `crates/openalpaca_core/src/lib.rs`, then step 3 again | **6 s** | **15.37** | +1.48 | **41,089** | +5,601 | 276 | 9/10/5 | 26 | 1.29 | 142 |
| 12 | `cargo +1.99.0 check --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --locked` | 42 s | 16.24 | +0.87 | 44,878 | +3,789 | 241 | 10/11/6 | 26 | 1.29 | 152 |
| 13 | `cargo +1.98.1 check --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --locked` | 56 s | 17.15 | +0.91 | 48,664 | +3,786 | 251 | 11/12/7 | 26 | 1.29 | 162 |
| 14 | `cargo build -p openalpacad` (what `prepare-sidecar.ts:60-61` runs on every `bun run tauri dev` [C]) | 32 s | 18.14 | +0.99 | 50,868 | +2,204 | 472 | 12/12/7 | 27 | 1.43 | 167 |
| 15 | `cargo build --release -p openalpaca -p openalpacad` (the three package scripts) | 161 s | 19.41 | +1.27 | 54,957 | +4,089 | 325 | 12/12/7 | 27 | 1.43 | 167 |
| 16 | `cargo +1.99.0 test --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --no-run --locked` (a *build* on a second toolchain) | 53 s | **24.27** | **+4.86** | **67,071** | **+12,114** | 420 | 13/13/8 | 45 | 2.29 | 194 |

Reading the table (each point measured in the stems, exe and s-dirs columns):

- **Steps 1 → 2: the bare command and the workspace command do not share a tower.** With `default-members` = the eight library crates (`Cargo.toml:17-26` [C]), `cargo build` unifies features over those eight; `--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui` adds the daemon and the CLI, whose features (the CLI's reqwest `multipart`, `apps/openalpaca/Cargo.toml:20` [C]; the daemon's provider and connector features) change the unified set, so `openalpaca_core` and `openalpaca_storage` go from 1 stem to 2 and every dependency whose features changed is compiled again.
- **Step 3: the test build is a third tower and the largest step** (+3.62 GiB, +9,962 paths). `cargo test` enables dev-dependency features — among them `openalpaca_storage/test-home-guard`, which every dependent crate's `[dev-dependencies]` turns on (`crates/openalpaca_core/Cargo.toml:61`, `apps/openalpacad/Cargo.toml:56` [C]) — so storage and everything above it compile a third time, 20 test executables (0.85 GiB) are linked and 26 incremental session directories appear. **The guard is the rule the project keeps** (CLAUDE.md, *Feature Flags*, *Gotchas*); this row is its measured cost, not an argument against it.
- **Step 4: clippy is not a cache hit on the build.** Check-mode units carry their own metadata (rmeta-only stems: core 3 → 5), so clippy adds +1.27 GiB and 28 session dirs; a plain `cargo check` afterwards rebuilds again (clippy-driver and rustc fingerprints differ), which step 10 partly shows.
- **Steps 6–9: each `-p` selection the documentation names is a new unification** (+0.84, +0.74, +0.41, +1.70 GiB). Agent sessions run many such selections; each distinct one costs a tower once per lock state.
- **Step 10** (rust-analyzer's default check) adds +1.33 GiB and 33 session dirs — if an editor ran it. No rust-analyzer is installed on this machine (§1.11), so for the owner it is probably not a driver.
- **Step 11: a one-line edit costs 1.48 GiB and 5,601 paths in 6 seconds.** rustc keeps the previous session beside the new one (two `s-*` per recompiled hash dir, never more — measured on 15 dirs and on six successive edits, §1.7), so every recompiled crate's incremental state exists twice until the next edit, and each new session links its codegen-unit objects into `deps/` under new names.
- **Steps 12, 13, 16: toolchain mixing.** Every artifact's hash includes the compiler, so a second toolchain shares nothing: +0.87 / +0.91 GiB for a `check`, **+4.86 GiB and +12,114 paths for one `test --no-run`** (19 more executables).
- **Step 14** is yet another unification (core 11 → 12): +0.99 GiB. **Step 15** adds a `target/release` of 1.33 GiB (deps 1.14) — the one profile with incremental off by default, so it is not duplicated per edit.

### 1.3 Experiment B — the same steps 1–11 with `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` [M: `attr-B-sizes.tsv`, `attr-B-composition-full.txt`]

| step | A GiB | B GiB | B − A | A paths | B paths | A wall | B wall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 build bare | 1.40 | 0.99 | −0.41 | 5,140 | 2,775 | 37 s | 39 s |
| 2 build ws | 3.60 | 2.45 | −1.15 | 10,866 | 4,712 | 58 s | 39 s |
| 3 test ws --no-run | 7.22 | 4.20 | −3.02 | 20,828 | 5,391 | 49 s | 40 s |
| 4 clippy | 8.49 | 4.45 | −4.04 | 23,132 | 7,575 | 36 s | 30 s |
| 5 check gui | 8.89 | 4.81 | −4.07 | 25,448 | 9,868 | 19 s | 14 s |
| 6 core live-eval | 9.72 | 5.23 | −4.50 | 27,848 | 10,104 | 21 s | 19 s |
| 7 core --lib | 10.46 | 5.49 | −4.98 | 28,894 | 10,149 | 30 s | 25 s |
| 8 storage | 10.87 | 5.69 | −5.18 | 30,161 | 10,415 | 10 s | 9 s |
| 9 daemon | 12.57 | 6.60 | −5.97 | 33,610 | 10,633 | 43 s | 31 s |
| 10 r-a check | 13.89 | 6.97 | −6.92 | 35,488 | 12,365 | 38 s | 28 s |
| 11 edit + test ws | **15.37** | **7.22** | **−8.15** | **41,089** | **12,365** | **6 s** | **30 s** |

- **−53 % bytes, −70 % paths** at the end of the documented set; B has 12,365 paths = 12,365 inodes (no hard links at all). The same rlib variants exist in B (`openalpaca_core` 5, `openalpaca_llm` 6, …): incremental changes the *size* of each variant, not the *number* of variants.
- **Time.** The one-shot cost is *lower* without incremental (steps 1–11: 347 s with, 304 s without; the CI set, steps 1–3: 144 s vs 118 s) because no dependency graph and query cache are written. The interactive cost is step 11: **6 s with incremental, 30 s without** for the workspace test build after a one-line edit of `openalpaca_core`; on `openalpaca_storage` alone 2 s vs 6 s (§1.7). An edit in B replaces files in place — paths unchanged at 12,365 — where A's edit added 5,601.
- `ci.yml:10` already sets `CARGO_INCREMENTAL: "0"` [C], and Cargo 1.99 does so by default whenever `CI` is set [Docs: Cargo Book CHANGELOG 1.99]; nothing about agent sessions or the owner's shell does.

### 1.4 Composition of A after the 13 task steps [M: `attr-A-composition-full-after-13.txt`]

- **Totals:** `du` 17.15 GiB; 48,664 paths over **31,407 inodes** (28,973 paths carry a link count > 1); unique bytes 16.89 GiB. `target/debug` only: `incremental` 10.6 GiB measured alone (8.8 GiB once the 1.9 GiB of objects also linked from `deps/` are charged to deps), `deps` 7.3 GiB, `build` (build-script OUT_DIRs) 0.56 GiB, the uplifted outputs (`openalpacad` 146 MB, `libopenalpaca_core.rlib` 98 MB, `examples/` 63 MB, …) 0.45 GiB, `.fingerprint` 42 MB.
- **By file type (unique inodes):** `.bin` 486 files **8.61 GiB** (incremental `dep-graph.bin`, `query-cache.bin`, work-product indexes); `.rlib` 782 files 2.31 GiB; extension-less executables 2.01 GiB; `.rmeta` 2,113 files 1.89 GiB; `.o` 12,930 inodes 1.54 GiB; `.dylib` (proc-macros) 141 files 0.48 GiB; `.d`, `.json`, `.timestamp`, `.lock` ≈ 8,000 names, ≈ 0.03 GiB.
- **Size histogram (unique inodes):** 12,165 files < 1 KB; 11,724 files 1–64 KB (0.19 GiB); 6,004 files 64 KB–1 MB (1.42 GiB); 1,294 files 1–16 MB (4.34 GiB); **220 files 16–256 MB (10.93 GiB)**. Two thirds of the bytes are in 220 files; three quarters of the inodes are under 64 KB.
- **The ten largest files** are `openalpaca_core` incremental caches and `openalpacad` test binaries: `dep-graph.bin` 260,404,130 / 260,371,835 / 255,999,148 B (two sessions of hash dir `15wavk551dsiv`, one of `3kef0cjqt3ezc`), `query-cache.bin` 203 / 193 / 192 MB, `deps/openalpacad-{f3b2…,d808…,ca15…}` 153.5 MB each, `target/debug/openalpacad` 153 MB.
- **Variants:** `openalpaca_core`'s 13 incremental hash directories range from 1.01 GiB (`15wavk551dsiv`, two sessions) through 588 and 551 MiB down to 144 MiB, and only the two largest (`15wavk551dsiv`, `3kef0cjqt3ezc`) hold a ≥ 255 MB `dep-graph.bin`. Incremental hash dirs per crate — `openalpaca_storage` 14, `openalpaca_llm` 13, `openalpaca_core` 13, `openalpaca_mcp` 12, `openalpacad` / `openalpaca_plugins` / `openalpaca_connectors` 11, `openalpaca_api` / `openalpaca_wake` 10, `openalpaca` 8, plus 3–4 for every integration-test target. `deps/` stems: `hashbrown` 17, `reqwest` 16, `getrandom` 16, `openalpaca_storage` 14, `toml_datetime` 13, `openalpaca_llm` 13, `openalpaca_core` 13, `tokio_tungstenite` 12, `rand_core` 12, `openalpaca_mcp` 12, `syn` 11, `serde_json` 11, `serde_core` 11, `nix` 11, `openalpacad` 11, `openalpaca_plugins` 11, `openalpaca_connectors` 11. Compiled vs checked per workspace crate: `openalpaca_core` 5 `.rlib` + 11 `.rmeta`, `openalpaca_llm` 6 + 12, `openalpaca_storage` 5 + 12, `openalpaca_mcp` 5 + 11.
- **Build scripts** are small: the largest OUT_DIRs are `openalpaca_gui` (tauri codegen) 31 MB ×2, `tauri-plugin-fs` 20 MB ×2, `tauri-plugin-dialog` 20 MB ×2, `tauri` 18 MB ×2, `aws-lc-sys` 6–14 MB ×6 (six feature/toolchain variants of one C library), `libsqlite3-sys` 5 MB ×2.
- After step 15: 19.41 GiB, 54,957 paths, 36,623 inodes; `target/release` 1.33 GiB / 4,089 paths, no incremental. After step 16: 24.27 GiB, 67,071 paths; 45 executables of 2.29 GiB [M: `-after-15.txt`, `attr-A-16-test-199-ws-norun.log`].

Shares of A's 24.27 GiB (16 steps) unless said otherwise [M: §1.2]:

| Driver | Measured | Share |
|---|---|---|
| Incremental compilation state | 8.15 GiB of the step-11 set (A 15.37 vs B 7.22) | **53 %** of the 11-step set; 62 % of the bytes after 13 steps |
| The five `-p` / feature selections the docs name (steps 6–9, 14) | 0.84 + 0.74 + 0.41 + 1.70 + 0.99 = 4.68 GiB | **19 %** |
| Other toolchains (steps 12, 13, 16) | 0.87 + 0.91 + 4.86 = 6.64 GiB | **27 %** |
| The two check modes (clippy, the rust-analyzer command; steps 4, 10) | 1.27 + 1.33 = 2.60 GiB | **11 %** |
| The release build (step 15) | 1.27 GiB | **5 %** |
| The bare-vs-workspace duplicate (step 1 → 2) | part of the +2.20 GiB | — (the genuinely new code — daemon, CLI — is part of it) |
| The shell check and the sidecar build in the main tower (steps 5, 14; step 14 is also counted in the `-p` row) | 0.40 GiB / +2,316 paths and 0.99 GiB / +2,204 paths | **6 %** — and already covered by plan-rev3's own rules: G-GUI (`plan-rev3.md:620`) prescribes a `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` of its own for `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui`, Q08 (`:576`) uses `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui` in CI, and Q02's G2 (`:571`) makes `target/gui` the sidecar build's default [C]. Followed, both steps leave the main tower — but `target/gui` is a second full tower **inside** `target/` (the workspace-minus-shell build alone is 2.73 GiB / 7,975 paths cold [M: `attr-C-sizes.tsv`]; the daemon alone was not measured cold), so `du -sh target` grows by it and M01's threshold expects it (M01 line (4); V4). |

What the repository tells people and agents to run [M: `cvi-cargo-invocations-instructions.txt` — instruction files only: CLAUDE.md, `tasks/lessons.md`, `.github/workflows/*.yml`, `scripts/**`, `docs/*.md`; the eval corpus JSON's "cargo nextest" strings are recorded chat transcripts and were excluded]: **31 file–invocation pairs collapsing to 13 distinct package/feature/profile selections** — bare (default-members); `--workspace` incl. the GUI; `--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui`; `-p openalpaca_core` tests with filters (×8); `-p openalpaca_core --features live-eval [--test memory_eval]` (×18 across the two eval READMEs); `-p openalpaca_storage` test; `-p openalpaca_llm` / `-p openalpaca_core` build; `-p openalpacad` build (CLAUDE.md and `prepare-sidecar.ts:60` on every `tauri dev`); `-p openalpacad` test (`-- managers::mcp`); `-p openalpaca` / `-p openalpacad` run; `--release -p openalpaca -p openalpacad` (the three package scripts); `--release -p openalpacad` and bare `cargo build --release`; `-p openalpaca_gui` check (×5) — plus two check modes on top of the dev/test builds and, for agent sessions, an unbounded number of `cargo test -p <crate> <filter>` selections, each new one priced by the table at 0.4–1.7 GiB per lock state.

### 1.6 The toolchain-mixing jump

`+0.87` GiB for `cargo +1.99.0 check`, `+0.91` for `cargo +1.98.1 check`, **`+4.86` GiB and `+12,114` paths for one `cargo +1.99.0 test --no-run`** of the workspace (19 more executables) — nothing is shared between toolchains [M: steps 12, 13, 16]. The repository pins 1.93.0 (`rust-toolchain.toml:2`, unchanged since the initial commit [M: `git log`]); `rustup default` is `stable` = 1.98.0 and plays no part inside the pinned repository, so only an explicit `cargo +<other>` mixes toolchains there [M]. Two consequences: (i) any session that checks another toolchain inside the repository's `target/` adds a whole tower; (ii) plan-rev3's P03 moves the pin to 1.99.0, and the first build after that re-keys everything once — **+15–24 GiB on top of the 1.93 set until a `cargo clean`** [NE: extrapolated from step 16, not measured]. M01's CLAUDE.md line covers (i); the one-time `cargo clean` after (ii) is an action attached to P03 (§6.1); DU-M4 asks only whether a tool should enforce the rule.

### 1.7 The profile levers [M: `levers-storage.tsv`, `levers-toy.tsv`, `levers-driver.log`, `churn-storage.tsv`, `toy-backtrace-splitdebuginfo.log`]

Each variant is `cargo +1.93.0 test -p openalpaca_storage --no-run` into a **fresh** target, the override passed with `--config` (no manifest edit), one change at a time; the repository's own `[profile.dev] debug = "line-tables-only"` and `[profile.dev.package."*"] debug = false` (`Cargo.toml:158-162` [C]) stay in force underneath.

| variant (storage test build, cold) | wall | target | Δ bytes | paths | Δ paths | `.o` in deps | s-dirs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| baseline (as shipped) | 15 s | 494 MiB | — | 1,795 | — | 512 (63.7 MiB) | 2 |
| `profile.dev.debug = 0` | 11 s | 446 MiB | **−10 %** | 1,283 | −29 % | 0 | 2 |
| `profile.dev.split-debuginfo = "off"` | 11 s | 494 MiB | **0 %** | 1,283 | −29 % | 0 | 2 |
| `profile.dev.split-debuginfo = "packed"` | 12 s | 526 MiB | +6 % | 1,286 | −28 % | 0 (dSYM bundles instead) | 2 |
| `profile.dev.strip = "debuginfo"` | 11 s | 492 MiB | −0.4 % | 1,795 | 0 | 512 | 2 |
| `profile.test.incremental = false` | 10 s | 288 MiB | **−42 %** | 794 | −56 % | 32 (60.9 MiB) | 0 |
| `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` (env) | 10 s | 288 MiB | −42 % (identical) | 794 | −56 % | 32 | 0 |
| `profile.dev.codegen-units = 16` | 11 s | 490 MiB | −1 % | 835 | −53 % | 32 | 2 |
| `profile.dev.codegen-units = 1` | 14 s | 479 MiB | −3 % | 775 | −57 % | 2 | 2 |
| `split-debuginfo = "off"` + `test.incremental = false` | 10 s | 227 MiB | **−54 %** | 762 | −58 % | 0 | 0 |

The toy crate (serde + serde_json + anyhow, one test) shows the same shape (52 / 43 / 48 / 59 / 51 / 47 / 47 / 53 / 49 / 43 MiB; 407 / 300 / 300 / 306 / 407 / 217 / 217 / 395 / 223 / 197 paths). A note on the logs: the first attempts at the three string-valued variants in `levers-driver.log` exited 101 on a TOML quoting error of the group's own script and never ran cargo; the `levers2` section of that log holds the real runs, and in `toy-backtrace-splitdebuginfo.log` only the `baseline`, `off-fixed`, `packed-fixed` and `off-fixed-debug0` sections are measurements [M].

**What the levers mean:**
- **Incremental is the only lever with a large byte effect** (−42 % on one crate's test build; −53 % on the whole documented set). Where it lives decides who pays the edit cost: `[profile.test] incremental = false` affects every `cargo test` (the owner's included), `[profile.dev]` every build, `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` whoever has it in the environment.
- **`split-debuginfo` decides the path inflation, not the bytes.** macOS's default `"unpacked"` keeps every codegen-unit object and links it into `deps/` (512 for storage: 256 per target × lib and test). `"off"` removes them (−29 % paths, 0 % bytes) **but a test backtrace then shows function names without `at ./src/lib.rs:N:M` lines** — measured on the toy with `RUST_BACKTRACE=1`: baseline and `"packed"` print the two `at ./src/lib.rs:8:…` frames, `"off"` prints none. That is precisely the file:line information `Cargo.toml:155-157`'s comment keeps `debug = "line-tables-only"` for ("readable backtraces") [C]. `"packed"` keeps the frames, has no objects in `deps/`, and costs +6 % bytes (one `.dSYM` per binary) and a `dsymutil` per link.
- **`debug = 0`** saves 10 % and removes the objects, at the price of no line tables anywhere.
- **`codegen-units = 16`** removes 94 % of the objects (−53 % paths) for −1 % bytes. Its edit-rebuild cost on `openalpaca_storage` is below the driver's 1-second resolution [M: `cgu-storage.tsv`, `cgu-{baseline,cgu16}-{0,1,2}.log`]: cold 8 s against the baseline's 9 s in the same session, two successive real edits ≤ 1 s each under both settings (a sub-second difference is not excluded), paths 835 → 908 → 940 against 1,795 → 2,828 → 3,340, `.o` names in `deps/` 32 → 64 → 96 against 512 → 1,024 → 1,536, bytes within 1 %. Its effect on `openalpaca_core`'s or the daemon's rebuild, where 16 coarser units could mean more code per recompiled unit, is [NE].
- **`strip = "debuginfo"`** only shrinks the test binary by 2 MiB.

**Six successive real edits of `openalpaca_storage`** (a new private function appended each time), test build, four settings [M: `churn-storage.tsv`]:

| setting | cold | after edit 1 | after edit 6 | paths cold → 6 | unique inodes | `.o` paths in deps cold → 6 | wall per edit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| baseline | 494 MiB | 678 MiB (2 sessions) | 673 MiB | 1,795 → **5,388** (+512 per edit) | 1,282 → 1,291 | 512 → **3,584** | 1–2 s |
| `split-debuginfo = "off"` | 494 MiB | 678 MiB | 673 MiB | 1,283 → 1,804 (flat after edit 1) | 1,282 → 1,291 | 0 → 0 | 1–2 s |
| `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` | 288 MiB | 314 MiB | 314 MiB | 794 → 794 | 794 | 32 → 32 | 6–7 s |
| both | 227 MiB | 227 MiB | 227 MiB | 762 → 762 | 762 | 0 → 0 | 6–7 s |

rustc keeps the previous session (two `s-*` per hash dir, never more — the toy ran six edits with the same result), so **bytes stop growing after the first edit for a fixed hash; the paths do not**: every session links its mostly-reused objects into `deps/` under a new name, 512 more names per edit over the same 1,291 inodes. Over months this is the mechanism that turns a 10–20 GiB target into a 500,000-path one. Objects whose session was deleted and that no newer session reuses stay in `deps/` as orphans (17 of them, 3.6 MB, after the toy's six edits; none yet in storage's six) — a slow byte leak on top [M].

### 1.8 Stability facts: what stable cargo can and cannot do [M: `toy-builddir.log`, `clean-gc-dryrun.log`, `toy-feature-unification.log`]

- **`build.build-dir` is stable on 1.93.0 and on 1.99.0**: `cargo --config 'build.build-dir="<dir>"' check` on a toy crate prints **no warning of any kind**, exit 0; `target/` keeps only `CACHEDIR.TAG` and the lock (4 KB) while `.rustc_info.json`, `debug/{.fingerprint,incremental,deps,build,examples}` land in the build-dir (26.6 MB on 1.93.0, 27.7 MB on 1.99.0, whose lock is named `.cargo-build-lock`). With `build`, the **final** artifacts (`toycrate`, `libtoycrate.rlib`, their `.d`) land in `target/debug/`; with `test --no-run` the **test executables land in `<build-dir>/debug/deps/`**. So a build-dir moves essentially all of the growth; `target/` keeps the uplifted binaries and rlibs (≈ 0.45 GiB in A). Stabilised in Cargo 1.91 (2025-10-30); templating `{workspace-root}`, `{cargo-cache-home}`, `{workspace-path-hash}` available [Docs: Cargo Book CHANGELOG, config → `build.build-dir`; `SP/logs/fetch/cargo-config.txt:432-443`]. Cargo 1.100 (2026-11-12) changes the build-dir's internal **layout** ("not a stable interface"); `-Z build-dir-new-layout` is refused on stable 1.99.0 [M]. `prepare-sidecar.ts:42-43` reads `target/<profile>/openalpacad` [C] and keeps working under a build-dir because final artifacts stay in `target/`; nothing in `scripts/` or `apps/openalpaca-gui/scripts/` reads `deps/` by hand [C: grep]. **What build-dir does not do:** it deletes nothing, shares nothing between invocations with different features, and does not stop the growth — it relocates it. One sharing effect is real: a fixed build-dir (no `{workspace-path-hash}`) used by several checkouts or worktrees of the same repository lets them reuse each other's artifacts (cargo's build lock serialises them); whether agent sessions build in separate worktrees is [NE].
- **`cargo clean gc` is refused on stable**: `cargo +1.93.0 clean gc --dry-run` and `cargo +1.99.0 clean gc --dry-run` both exit 101 ("the `cargo clean gc` command is unstable, and only available on the nightly channel of Cargo", tracking issue #12633); `-Z gc` is listed by `-Z help` on 1.93.0, 1.98.1 and 1.99.0 and refused on all three. It concerns the **global cache in `$CARGO_HOME`** (registry sources, `.crate` files, index, git checkouts), never `target/` [Docs: Cargo Book unstable → gc; `SP/logs/fetch/cargo-unstable.txt:1175-1212`]. `cargo clean --dry-run` works ("Summary N files, X total"); `-p <crate>`, `--profile`, `--release`, `--doc` are the only selective forms and none is age-based. **There is no cargo-native age- or size-based cleaning of `target/` on any installed toolchain.** The owner's global cache, `~/.cargo/registry`, is 2.1 GiB [M: `du`] — not where the problem is.
- **`-Z feature-unification` is refused on stable**: `--config 'resolver.feature-unification="workspace"'` on 1.93.0 and 1.99.0 prints `warning: ignoring resolver.feature-unification without -Zfeature-unification` and proceeds in the default `selected` mode. The stable remedies for the duplication the A table shows are the invocation set itself (always select the same packages → the same unification) or `default-members` (DU-M1). How much steps 1, 2 and 3 duplicate: each adds a complete tower of the workspace crates (stems 1 → 2 → 3) and of the dependencies whose features differ (distinct stems 1,711 → 4,062 → 8,855).
- **Cargo 1.99 disables incremental compilation by default when `CI` is set** [Docs: RELEASES.md 1.99; the rust-modernization group's reading] — nothing here runs under `CI` locally.

### 1.9 Third-party tools (read-only research; nothing installed) [Docs: the crates.io and GitHub API responses `SP/logs/fetch/crates-*.json`, `gh-cargo-sweep-*.json`; D: the fetched READMEs and sources; nothing was installed or run, so nothing here is [M]]

| Tool | Latest | Released | MSRV | What it does for `target/` | Standing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| cargo-sweep | 0.8.0 | 2025-10-11 | none declared (edition 2021) | Deletes files under `target/` selectively, by reading `target/debug/.fingerprint`: `--time N` removes artifacts whose fingerprint was last **accessed** (`metadata().accessed()`, atime) more than N days ago; `--installed` keeps only artifacts whose fingerprint names a currently installed rustc (it recomputes cargo's rustc-version hash, with the pre-1.85 SipHash form for old fingerprints); `--toolchains a,b`; `--maxsize 10GiB` deletes oldest-first until under the size; `--stamp`/`--file`; `--dry-run`, `--recursive` [D: `holmgr/cargo-sweep@master` `Cargo.toml:4-5`, `src/fingerprint.rs:19-30`, `:81-106`, `:112-118`] | **README on `master` carries an "unmaintained" banner** (commit 2026-05-26, after the 0.8.0 release; repository not archived) pointing at `cargo-clean-all` |
| cargo-clean-all | 0.6.5 | 2026-08-30 | none declared | Finds every `target/` under a path and removes **whole** target directories by age/size (`--keep-days`, `--keep-size`, `--dry-run`, `-i`); not selective inside one target | maintained |
| cargo-cache | 0.8.3 | 2022-09-11 | 1.57 | Reports and prunes `$CARGO_HOME`; its `local` subcommand only **reports** a project's `target/` | not a target-dir remedy |
| sccache | 0.18.0 | 2026-09-16 | 1.91.0 | A `RUSTC_WRAPPER` caching rustc outputs across target directories and workspaces (local disk cache, macOS `~/Library/Caches/Mozilla.sccache`, **default cap 10 GB**). Two caveats decide its value here: **incrementally compiled crates cannot be cached** (every workspace member under the default dev/test profiles) and crates that invoke the linker (bins, test binaries, proc-macros, dylibs) cannot be cached — so it speeds up rebuilding *dependencies* in a fresh target and does nothing for the incremental caches, test binaries and workspace rlibs that are ≈ 80 % of A. It does not shrink `target/`; it adds a second, capped cache [Docs: `mozilla/sccache@main` `docs/Rust.md`, `docs/Local.md:3-5`, `README.md:332-346`] | not recommended here; hit rate never measured [NE] |


### 1.10 The mitigations: one commit that needs no decision, and DU-M1…M6 (summary; the full options are §5.1)

A change to `Cargo.toml` or `.cargo/config.toml` is repository-wide policy that binds CI too; a file under `.claude/` binds Claude Code sessions in this repository (and `.claude/` is excluded whole by `.gitignore:139-140` — see DU-M2); a line in CLAUDE.md instructs people and agents and enforces nothing. Under the owner's rule the **instruction lines need no decision** and land now as **M01**; what the owner decides is the four things that change a build's behaviour or add a tool: the invocation set's defaults (DU-M1), incremental off (DU-M2), a sweep tool (DU-M3), toolchain hygiene as a tool (DU-M4), `build.build-dir` (DU-M5) and the profile (DU-M6).

| Item | Lever | Measured effect | Cost | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **M01** (no decision) | four CLAUDE.md lines in *Build & Development*: (1) build, test and clippy with `--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui`, `-p <crate>` only to run a filtered test (each distinct selection costs 0.4–1.7 GiB per lock state) — **this line is DU-M1 (c) as instruction; (b) changes the manifest**, and until DU-M1 is answered the Build block's bare `cargo build` line, which the `default-members` comment explains, stays; (2) no toolchain but the pin builds into the repository's `target/` — another toolchain is tried in a worktree with its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`; (3) `cargo clean` when `du -sh target` passes a threshold the owner picks (a cold rebuild of the CI set is 144 s, 118 s with incremental off), never by a hook, cron or agent rule; (4) the shell check uses `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui`, as plan-rev3's G-GUI and Q08 already say, and the sidecar build does once Q02 lands — a second tower inside `target/`, which the threshold counts | the `-p` towers (4.68 GiB, 19 % of A) and the other-toolchain towers (6.64 GiB, 27 %) stop accumulating in agent sessions that follow the rule; steps 5 and 14 (1.39 GiB) leave the main tower for `target/gui`; the threshold bounds the rest | none (instruction) | **land now** — it is the (a) answer of DU-M3 and DU-M4, and the (c) answer of DU-M1, written down; a different answer edits the line |
| **DU-M1** → M02 | `default-members` = every member except `apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri`, so a bare `cargo build`/`test`/`clippy` selects and unifies exactly what `--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui` does | one tower of **2.73 GiB / 7,975 paths** where steps 1 + 2 wrote **3.60 GiB / 10,866 paths** per lock state (−0.87 GiB, −2,891 paths) — **only when a session runs both forms**: under M01's line (1) a session never runs the bare form, so M02 then saves nothing more; what (b) adds beyond M01 is that a bare `cargo clippy` reports the project's "clippy 78" baseline set (CI's selection), where today's bare form lints the eight library crates only | **reverses `Cargo.toml:15-16`'s stated design**; a cold bare `cargo build` takes **22 s longer** (52 s vs 30 s, same session) and compiles the daemon and CLI; a bare `cargo test` then runs the daemon's tests too (≈ 5.5 min for the whole suite [M-oct10], process-spawning tests, the `managers::mcp` `sleep 3600` orphans) | **(b)** + M01's sentence, presented as the reversal it is; (c) the sentence alone if the design stands |
| **DU-M2** → M03 | incremental off for agent builds: (a) `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` in `.claude/settings.json`'s `env` (agents only; needs `.gitignore` to admit the file — §5.1) or in `settings.local.json` (this machine only, untracked); (b) `.cargo/config.toml` `[build] incremental = false` (everyone, every profile); (c) `Cargo.toml` `[profile.test] incremental = false` (every `cargo test`, the owner's included) | **−53 % bytes / −70 % paths** on the documented set; −42 % on one crate's test build; no path inflation on edits; one-shot cold builds faster (CI set 144 s → 118 s) | edit-rebuild **6 s → 30 s** (workspace test build after a core edit), 2 s → 6 s (storage alone); the `env` key is documented, the owner's installed build not checked [NE] | **(a)** now, with the 6 s → 30 s number stated; (c) if the owner accepts 30 s in their own loop |
| **DU-M3** | a sweep tool: (a) none — M01's threshold line; (b) owner-installed cargo-sweep 0.8.0 (`--maxsize`, `--time` (atime), `--installed`; README carries an "unmaintained" banner since 2026-05-26); (c) cargo-clean-all across `~/dev` (whole directories) | bounds accumulated staleness periodically either way | (b) adds an unmaintained tool; (c) deletes whole targets | **(a)** — the measured rebuild cost does not justify a tool; (b) only if the warm incremental state must be kept |
| **DU-M4** | toolchain hygiene as a tool: (a) M01's rule alone, plus **one `cargo clean` right after P03 moves the pin** (an action, not a commit); (b) the convention `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/<toolchain>` for any `cargo +<other>` (same bytes, separable); (c) `cargo sweep --installed` after each toolchain experiment | +0.87 / +0.91 GiB per extra-toolchain `check`, **+4.86 GiB / +12,114 paths per extra-toolchain `test --no-run`**, nothing shared; the pin bump will re-key everything once (by extrapolation +15–24 GiB until a clean — inferred) | a rule, no enforcement | **(a)** plus the one-time clean (rev 2.4: a second clean, right after P03b, recommended on the same reasoning and presented to the owner, not adopted — §5.1) |
| **DU-M5** | `build.build-dir` (stable on 1.93.0 and 1.99.0): (a) nothing; (b) `.cargo/config.toml` `[build] build-dir = "{cargo-cache-home}/build/{workspace-path-hash}"`; (c) a fixed shared build-dir across worktrees of this repository | relocates ≈ 100 % of the growth out of `target/` (which shrinks to ≈ 0.45 GiB of final artifacts) and deletes nothing; (c) deduplicates across worktrees, whose use is [NE] | out of sight of `du target`, not gone; Cargo 1.100 changes the layout on 2026-11-12; `prepare-sidecar.ts:42-43` keeps working (final artifacts stay in `target/`); CI's `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2` (`ci.yml:26` [C]) caches `./target`, so under (b) the build artifacts leave the cached paths unless the action is configured for the build-dir — whether `rust-cache` follows `build.build-dir` was not read [NE] | **(a)** today; revisit after 1.100 and if worktrees become routine |
| **DU-M6** | profile levers: (a) nothing; (b) `split-debuginfo = "packed"` (−28 % paths, +6 % bytes, frames kept, a `dsymutil` per link); (c) `"off"` (−29 % paths, 0 % bytes, **loses `at file:line` frames** — rejected); (d) `codegen-units = 16` (−53 % paths, −1 % bytes, per-edit path inflation +512 → ≈ +35, rebuild ≤ 1 s on storage, core [NE]); (e) `debug = 0` (−10 % bytes, no line tables) | path-count reduction mostly; bytes ≤ 10 % by any lever but incremental | each trades debuggability or link time for file names | **(a)** — the byte lever is DU-M2; (d) is the one cheap path lever and stays the owner's call because its effect on the largest crate is unmeasured |

Together, under the recommended answers, on one denominator — the 11-step documented set, 15.37 GiB: M03 addresses about 53 % (incremental, 8.15 GiB — the only figure measured with incremental off) and M02 the bare-vs-workspace duplicate, 0.87 GiB = 5.7 % of the same set (3.6 % of the 16-step 24.27 GiB) — **measured with incremental on, so not additive with M03** (B's step 2 shows the duplicate shrinking with the rest); the rules in M01 cover the 19 % and 27 % that selections and other toolchains add to the 16-step set, and line (4) moves steps 5 and 14 into `target/gui`, which the threshold still counts; the owner's own interactive loop is untouched by DU-M2 (a). The owner's literal ask was to stop the growth; nothing here reaches 100 % except the periodic `cargo clean` of M01's threshold line, because cargo itself never removes a stale variant (§1.8).

### 1.11 Not established [NE]

- The decomposition of the owner's 147.6 GiB / 530,884 paths (gone); how many lock states and toolchains it had seen; when it was last cleaned. `Cargo.lock` changed in 22 commits since 2026-08 (Aug 2, Sep 19, Oct 1; 27 since April, 55 in Feb–Apr) [M: `git log -- Cargo.lock`]; which of those states the target lived through without a clean is the number that turns 15–24 GiB into 147.6.
- Whether the owner runs rust-analyzer in this repository on another machine or editor: on this machine there is **no** rust-analyzer component on any installed toolchain, no rust extension in `~/.cursor/extensions` or `~/.antigravity/extensions`, no `rust-analyzer` key in either editor's `settings.json`, and `~/.cargo/bin/rust-analyzer` is a rustup proxy with nothing behind it [M]; step 10 (+1.33 GiB and 33 session dirs per run) is probably not a driver here.
- Agent sessions building in the repository with other toolchains, other feature sets or git worktrees (each worktree gets its own `target/` unless a shared build-dir is configured); neither is visible after the fact. Which agent runtimes besides Claude Code are used here, and whether they read `.claude/settings.json`'s `env` block at all (DU-M2 (a) binds only those that do).
- Whether the owner's installed Claude Code build matches the settings documentation fetched today (the `env` key itself is documented: `code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference#env`, `/docs/en/env-vars`, fetched to `SP/logs/fetch/rev2/` [Docs]) — verifiable in one session by reading `CARGO_INCREMENTAL` from a tool call after M03.
- `codegen-units = 16`'s effect on `openalpaca_core`'s and the daemon's incremental rebuild time (measured ≤ 1 s on `openalpaca_storage` only); `split-debuginfo = "packed"`'s `dsymutil` cost at the daemon's size (toy and storage only).
- sccache's hit rate on this workspace (never installed; by its documented caveats it cannot cover ≈ 80 % of A's bytes); cargo-sweep's behaviour on this target (not installed; its `--time` reads atime, whose update policy on this APFS volume was not checked).
- The contents of A's 63 MB `target/debug/examples/`.
- The size of the first build after the pin moves to 1.99.0 (+15–24 GiB by extrapolation from step 16, not measured).

## 2. Deprecated usage

**Source:** the deprecated-inventory group (`SP/reports/deprecated-inventory.md`, verification pass 02:22; logs `SP/logs/depr-*`; tools `SP/tools/depr-clippy.sh`, `depr-clippy-parse.py`, `depr-async-traits.sh`), the rust-modernization group's independent recount and second pass (`SP/tools/count_clippy_fixed.py`, `recount_indep.py`, `SP/logs/triples-*.txt`), the web-modernization group's greps where they overlap, and this author's recounts (§0). Its one cargo call ran `--locked` into `SP/target-depr` (1.5 GB, clippy and check artifacts only); its Bun calls ran in the detached worktree `SP/wt-js` with the scratch Bun 1.4.2; nothing was written in the repository [M]. Taxonomy: **deprecated** = marked by the compiler or the library (`#[deprecated]`, `@deprecated`, a runtime deprecation); **removed-next-major** = the library's own docs remove it in the next major; **superseded** = a stable replacement exists that the project already targets (edition 2024, Rust 1.93/1.99, React 19, Tailwind 4, Vite 7/8, TS 7); **not deprecated** rows were asked about and are kept for completeness. The last column names the commit (§6) or the decision (§5).

### 2.4 What the tooling could not see [NE]

- Tauri's runtime warnings about `tauri.conf.json` fields are printed by `tauri dev`/`tauri build`, which no group ran (by rule); the tauri-utils 2.9.3 / 2.10.1 schema read stands in for them.
- `shellcheck`-level findings for the four shell scripts (the tool is absent; the shell section is grep-only).
- `@typescript-eslint/no-deprecated` ran on TypeScript 6.0.3's compiler API, not 7.0.2's (typescript-eslint 8.71.1 refuses TS 7.0); a result could in principle differ on a type the two compilers resolve differently — judged unlikely for a 0 result over 350 files, strictly unmeasured. `type-coverage` cannot run on TS 7.0.2 at all (no `ts.createProgram`; it asks for `@typescript/typescript6` through an override; TS 7 ships no stable programmatic API until 7.1) [M: `web-17-knip-typecov.log`; Docs].
- A trait object built through a type alias or a macro would escape the `dyn` scan (simple and path-qualified forms, plus generic `T: Trait` bounds for the three names first thought `dyn`-free, were covered).
- Manual `Eq` impls against the 1.95 `assert_receiver_is_total_eq` deprecation were not checked; `if let` guard candidates: a shape scan found 26 arms opening with `if let`, the two read (`anthropic/response.rs:41`, `gateway/router/mod.rs:339`) are not guards — likely 0, the other 24 unread. (Trait upcasting and precise capturing were settled by the rust-modernization group's second pass: 30 `as &dyn` / `as Arc<dyn` / `as Box<dyn` casts, every one concrete → `dyn`, 0 upcasts; 0 `use<..>`, none wanted under edition 2024 [M].)
- Which dependency brings `proc-macro-error2 2.0.1` into the graph and whether the dependency wave drops it (`cargo tree -i` not run by either Rust group).
- Stabilisation versions of the std items involved were not cited from rust-src (not installed for 1.99.0); "stable on the 1.93 pin" is the measured fact — the tree compiles and already uses them.
- **The boundary of the Rust headline.** Every 1.99.0 clippy and check run today (`depr-clippy-199.json`; §1.2 steps 12 and 16) excluded `apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri`, and compiled `crates/openalpaca_core/tests/memory_eval.rs` as an empty crate root (`#![cfg(feature = "live-eval")]`, `:43`; 13,210 lines built today under 1.93.0 only, step 6 — `steer_routing_eval.rs`, 446 lines, is `#[ignore]`d but not feature-gated and was linted); the shell's 1.99.0 compiles are yesterday's, on two locks that are not HEAD's (R-1). M04's gate and V2 add `cargo +1.99.0 clippy -p openalpaca_core --features live-eval --all-targets --locked` and the shell's `cargo +1.99.0 clippy -p openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked` in its own target dir.
- **Kinds of deprecation nobody checked** [NE]: (i) npm-registry-deprecated packages in `bun.lock` — the npm analogue of `serde_yaml 0.9.34+deprecated`; "0 `deprecat` lines in 45 `SP10/logs/gui-*.log`" is evidence only if Bun 1.4.2 prints the registry's deprecation flag at install, which nobody established (`bun.lock` itself carries no such field — 0 `"deprecated"` strings [M] — so the lock cannot answer it); a one-off registry check (`npm view <name>@<version> deprecated`, or `bun pm view`, over the lock's 153 packages) closes it if the owner wants the npm side closed — not a gate (Appendix B #20); (ii) `scripts/prepare-sidecar.ts`, `vite.config.ts` and `vitest.setup.ts` were in the worktree ESLint config's `ignores` (and its `files` was `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}`; `SP/wt-js/apps/openalpaca-gui/eslint.config.js` [M]), so `@typescript-eslint/no-deprecated` never saw them — three files, readable by hand in a minute, unread by the tool; (iii) the project's **own** reserved or retired keys fall outside the inventory's definition (deprecated = marked by a compiler or a library): `log_retention_days = 0` at `config/daemon.toml:164` (reserved and inert, R85/T12 [C]) and `docs/Skill_Template_Reference.md`'s `execution.skill_defaults.*` citations (`:420`, `:421`, `:573`, `:631`, `:978`, `:1053` [C] — the section exists; only C8's per-tool deny key under it is gone) are intentional and documented, not deprecations. Checked clean by the completeness pass and recorded here [M: `SP/reports/completeness.md`]: GitHub workflow commands, `egrep`/`fgrep`/backticks in the shell scripts, Pester v4 assertion syntax, `@types/dompurify`, `index.html`'s meta tags.

### 2.5 The tooling questions, as owner decisions (summary; the full options are §5.2)

### 2.6 The counting correction to plan-rev3 (a proposed rev 3.3 entry; `plan-rev3.md` is not edited here)

**What happened.** `SP10/tools/count_clippy.py` reads cargo's JSON and skips a span when `f.startswith("/") or "registry" in f or ".cargo" in f`. The middle test, meant for `~/.cargo/registry`, also matches the **relative** path `crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/registry/…` — 33 `clippy::useless_vec` in `tests.rs` (lines 1376–1910) and `clippy::collapsible_if` at `mod.rs:1574` — so every count rev 3 built on it is 34 short. Two groups found it independently and the correction is measured three ways [M]: (i) `SP/tools/depr-clippy-parse.py` over yesterday's `a02`/`a07` JSON (primary span under `crates/`/`apps/`, nothing else dropped) → **101 / 78**; (ii) `SP/tools/count_clippy_fixed.py` (yesterday's script with the one condition removed) over `a02`, `a07`, `c01` → **101 / 78 / 100**; (iii) today's in-place `cargo +1.99.0 clippy --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked -- -W clippy::pedantic` filtered to the 27 lint names that fire in `a02` → **101**, set-identical to `a02`'s corrected set (`SP/logs/r01-clippy-pedantic-199.json`). The **+23** delta is identical under both methods (`comm` of the sorted triple lists: 23 new, 0 gone; `SP/logs/triples-new-194-199.txt`): `useless_borrows_in_formatting` ×6 (`apps/openalpaca/src/commands/config_tui.rs:1113`, `:1154`, `:1559`, `:1561`, `:1563`; `crates/openalpaca_core/src/runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:647`), `result_large_err` ×5 (`apps/openalpacad/src/routes/extensions.rs:684`; `routes/memories.rs:534`, `:542`, `:553`; `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:663` — a sixth, `routes/tasks.rs:638`, fires on 1.93 too), `drain_collect` ×2 (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs:950`, `:959`), `single_element_loop` ×2 (`orchestrator/query_handler/simple_query_handler.rs:130`; `orchestrator/skill/invocation.rs:278`), `unnecessary_sort_by` ×2 (`session_log/record.rs:425`; `session_log/sweep.rs:330`), `useless_format` ×1 (`apps/openalpaca/src/commands/memory/tests.rs:1493`), `for_kv_map` (`commands/ai_config.rs:270`), `question_mark` (`orchestrator/intent/mod.rs:148`), `collapsible_match` (`orchestrator/skill/output/mod.rs:45`), `unneeded_wildcard_pattern` (`apps/openalpacad/src/event_bridge.rs:37` — R79's deliberate `created_by: _`, kept with an `#[allow]` as P36 says), `deprecated` (`session_log/mod.rs:519`).

## 3. New APIs and features — Rust

**Source:** the rust-modernization group (`SP/reports/rust-modernization.md`, rev 2; `SP/logs/afit-map.txt`, `afit-sites.json`, `releases-194-199.txt`, `r01-clippy-pedantic-199.*`, `r02-tree-i-*.log`, `rm-connector-afit-check-193.log`, `rmcp-3.5.1-CHANGELOG.md`; tools `SP/tools/afit_map.py`, `count_clippy_fixed.py`, `recount_indep.py`), with the deprecated-inventory group's greps where they overlap. Its cargo calls (`clippy … -W clippy::pedantic`, `cargo tree -i`, and one `cargo +1.93.0 check -p openalpaca_connectors --all-features --locked` on an edited copy of the tree at `SP/wt-afit`) ran into `SP/target-rustmod` (2.1 GB) and wrote nothing in the repository (`git status --porcelain` afterwards: ` M CLAUDE.md`, `?? tasks/deps-upgrade/` only [M: `r03-git-status-after.txt`]). Rust-src is not installed for 1.99.0, so std signatures are cited from `RELEASES.md`, the installed 1.93.0 rustdoc and the published docs, not from source.

**The headline:** of the language and std items Rust 1.94–1.99 stabilized, **all but two replace nothing this code does by hand** (`Atomic::try_update`, 1 site; `String::from_utf8_lossy_owned`, 2 production sites; every other candidate count is 0); the items named from 1.80–1.93 are **mostly already adopted**; the largest named item — `async-trait` → native `async fn` in traits — is **blocked for 14 of 15 traits by the language itself** (they are `dyn`-dispatched, and AFIT traits are not dyn-compatible), and the one trait it is not blocked for compiled clean today. What is genuinely new and applicable is about a dozen sites plus three passes (the widened P36, `thiserror`, `#[expect]`), one dependency swap with a real fact behind it (`file-lock` has no Windows implementation), and three MCP-bridge gaps rmcp already serves.

### 3.1 Ranked table — work to schedule, and the decisions

Ranked by benefit per effort with the risk stated. **work** = scheduled (§6), the gate named; **DU-n** = an owner decision (§5), with the rule-5 trigger it hits. "After P03" = needs the 1.99 pin; "after P19" = needs rmcp 3.5.0.

| 11 | `std::fs::File::{lock, try_lock}` (1.89) in place of the `file-lock` crate | new-api / an unmaintained-looking dependency (2.1.11 of 2024-02-17, a `cc` build script) | `file_lock::FileLock::lock(path, blocking, opts)` at `storage/discovery/mod.rs:187-205` (the daemon's singleton lock, `block` chosen by the caller), `core/config_io.rs:241-256`, `llm/keys/key_encryption/mod.rs:299-315`; workspace `Cargo.toml:99`, the three crate manifests (`core:33`, `storage:15`, `llm:33`) | ≈ 30 lines + 4 manifest lines; a `struct … (File)` guard whose drop closes the file; `try_lock` → `Result<(), TryLockError>` [Docs: the installed 1.93 rustdoc] | **removes the one unconditional Unix-only dependency** — `file-lock`'s `src/file_lock.c` is `fcntl(F_SETLK\|F_SETLKW)` with no `#ifdef`, its `Cargo.toml` has no target table, its README says "POSIX advisory record locks" [D], while every `libc` use here is behind `cfg(unix)` (218 `cfg(unix)` branches [M]) — so one Windows build blocker goes; its C shim and its `cc` build script leave (the `cc` crate itself stays via libsqlite3-sys at least [NE: `cargo tree -i cc` not run]); rustyline 18 made the same swap (#909 [Docs]) | **medium, and a primitive change on Unix**: `fcntl` record locks → `flock` (Linux: "no interaction between the types of lock placed by flock() and fcntl(2)"; "does not lock files over NFS" [Docs: `linux.die.net/man/2/flock`]; macOS's page silent on the interaction [NE]). Consequences: (i) **a mixed-build window** — an old-build daemon (`fcntl`) and a new-build CLI/daemon (`flock`) on the same `openalpacad.lock` both "hold" it, so `claim_store`'s "the lock alone decides" and a second daemon's refusal are blind across that line; it is the "keep the builds apart" doctrine already in `docs/Installation_Manual.md`, and closes once both binaries are the new build; (ii) a latent same-process hazard goes away (under `fcntl` a probe's close from inside the holder's process would release the holder's lock process-wide [Docs: fcntl(2)]; no production path probes from a holder today); (iii) `O_CLOEXEC` keeps the lock out of spawned plugins and MCP servers; (iv) the non-blocking refusal's text changes (`TryLockError::WouldBlock` where `file-lock` gave `os error 35/11`), printed by `main.rs:118`'s log line and `claim_store`'s context — pinned by no test [M: grep]; (v) rev 2.2 (D-06): two writers of one config file in the same process now exclude each other — closing a latent lost-update race `fcntl` leaves open — and a path that takes a lock it already holds now blocks (M17's entry) | **DU-R2** (recommended yes) → **M17**, after P03 · the CLI's cross-process `LockHolder` test (`apps/openalpaca/src/test_util.rs:160-245`), `cargo test -p openalpaca_storage -p openalpaca_core -p openalpaca_llm`, a manual second-daemon start refused (isolated store, sandboxed), `cargo tree -i file-lock` empty; the `LockFileEx` leg only if Windows is ever built | [C] [D: `file-lock-2.1.11/{src/file_lock.c,Cargo.toml,README.md:3-6}`] [Docs] [M: `r02-tree-i-file-lock.log`] |
