Cargo-side, for the record [Docs: RELEASES.md 1.97, 1.99]: 1.97 stabilized `build.warnings` (a config-file replacement for `-Dwarnings` — the hook for DU-R3 if a warnings gate is ever taken) and `resolver.lockfile-path`; 1.99 adds the built-in `debug` profile, lets an edition-2024 member override an inherited dependency's `default-features` (plan-rev3 P23's option) and disables incremental compilation by default under `CI`.

### 3.2 `#[async_trait]` → native `async fn` in traits: the per-trait decision table [M: `SP/tools/afit_map.py`, `SP/logs/afit-map.txt`; C]

Ground truth: **211** `#[async_trait]` / `#[async_trait::async_trait]` attributes in **91** files (core 165, daemon 15, llm 10, plugins 6, connectors 5, mcp 4, api 4, wake 2), decorating **15 trait definitions and 196 impls**; 67 `use async_trait::async_trait;` lines and 50 fully-qualified attributes; `async-trait` is a dependency of 8 crates (every one but storage). No trait uses `#[async_trait(?Send)]`; every one has `Send + Sync` as a supertrait; the daemon is a tokio multi-thread runtime, so every future a trait method returns has to be `Send`. The rule that decides the table [Docs: the Rust 1.75 announcement, `blog.rust-lang.org/2023/12/21/async-fn-rpit-in-traits/`: keep `#[async_trait]` if "You want dynamic dispatch"; `async-trait` 0.1.92 `README.md:9-11` [D]: dyn dispatch of traits with async functions is not supported by the stabilization]. `trait-variant` only generates a `Send`-bounded twin and says nothing about `dyn`; `dynosaur` would generate a `DynTrait` struct that boxes at every dynamic call [Docs: both READMEs] — a new proc-macro dependency plus a renamed type at every `dyn` site, which is a widening. The prod/test split below is by file name and type name (`Mock*`/`Stub*`/`Recording*`, `tests.rs`, `tests/`), not by `#[cfg(test)]`.

| Trait | Defined [C] | Impls (prod / test) | `dyn` use | Default `async fn` bodies | Option |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BuiltInTool` | `core/src/tools/registry/mod.rs:318` | 66 (34 / 32) | 20 sites, 5 files (`ToolBackend::BuiltIn(Arc<dyn …>)`) | 3 | **keep `async-trait`** |
| `LlmProvider` | `llm/src/lib.rs:63` | 57 (6 / 51) | 16 sites, 10 files (the router holds `Arc<dyn LlmProvider>`) | 4 | keep |
| `MessageHandler` | `core/src/gateway/router/mod.rs:194` | 22 (1 / 21) | 3 sites (`Gateway.handler: Arc<dyn MessageHandler>`) | 1 | keep |
| `PluginSkillExecutor` | `api/src/plugin_traits.rs:39` | 9 (1 / 8) | 3 sites, **all path-qualified** (`Arc<dyn openalpaca_api::plugin_traits::…>` at `orchestrator/skill/invocation.rs:1224`, `skill/catalog/mod.rs:33`, `:725`) — a simple-name scan had read it as dyn-free | 0 | keep |
| `PluginToolExecutor` | `api/src/plugin_traits.rs:8` | 7 (1 / 6) | 1 site, path-qualified (`tools/registry/mod.rs:301`) | 0 | keep |
| `PluginAgentExecutor` | `api/src/plugin_traits.rs:66` | 6 (1 / 5) | 8 sites, 2 files | 0 | keep |
| `ConnectorSendProvider` | `core/src/orchestrator/mod.rs:85` | 6 (1 / 5) | 14 sites, 6 files | 1 | keep |
| `ContextSource` | `core/src/prompt_ctx/sources/mod.rs:31` | 5 (4 / 1) | 6 sites, 2 files | 0 | keep |
| `ToolCallbackExecutor` | `api/src/plugin_traits.rs:31` | 1 (1 / 0) | 10 sites, 6 files (`&dyn ToolCallbackExecutor` is a parameter of `PluginSkillExecutor::invoke`) | 0 | keep |
| `Summarizer` | `core/src/context_budget/compaction.rs:11` | 4 (2 / 2) | 4 sites (`&dyn Summarizer` parameters, `compaction.rs:54`, `:183`, `graduated.rs:35`, `:43`) | 0 | keep; a generic parameter instead of `&dyn` is a redesign of the compaction API |
| `ExtensionSupervisor` | `core/src/tools/extensions/mod.rs:423` | 2 (2 / 0) | 1 site (`fn supervisor(&self, kind) -> &dyn ExtensionSupervisor`, `managers/extensions.rs:114`) | 0 | keep; an enum over the two supervisors would free it (ADR-030 territory) |
| `EventWatcher` | `wake/src/watcher/mod.rs:11` | 1 (1 / 0) | 1 site (`Vec<Box<dyn EventWatcher>>`, `wake/manager/mod.rs:19`) | 0 | keep (or an enum; one impl today) |
| `Transport` (ours, not rmcp's) | `mcp/src/transport/mod.rs:23` | 3 (2 / 1 mock) | 1 site (`Box<dyn Transport>` from `build_transport`, `client.rs:619`) | 0 | keep; `TransportKind` is already an enum, so an enum-dispatched `connect` (≈30 lines) would free it |
| `PersonaHandler` (private) | `core/src/tools/builtins/update_persona/mod.rs:33` | 3 (3 / 0) | 1 site (`let handler: &dyn PersonaHandler = match target {…}`, `:81`) | 0 | keep; an enum over soul/user/identity (≈20 lines) would free it |
| **`Connector`** | `connectors/src/lib.rs:42` | 4 (4 / 0) | **0** sites, 0 generic bounds; each connector is spawned as its concrete type (`startup.rs:130` `spawn_telegram(connector: TelegramConnector)`) | 0 | **plain AFIT — compiled clean today**: `fn run(&self) -> impl Future<Output = Result<(), ConnectorError>> + Send` and `shutdown` likewise, the impls keeping their `async fn` bodies; on a copy of the tree (`SP/wt-afit`, 10 changed lines) `cargo +1.93.0 check -p openalpaca_connectors --all-features --locked` exit 0, 0 warnings, 26.15 s, 291 crates checked [M: `rm-connector-afit-check-193.log`] — the lib target only (no `--all-targets`: its tests and `examples/telegram.rs` not built); the plugins impl read, not compiled |

The `dyn` counts above are the deprecated-inventory group's (`rg -P 'dyn\s+(\w+::)*<Name>\b'`, tests included); the rust-modernization group's one-grep recount reads `BuiltInTool` 19, `LlmProvider` 12, `MessageHandler` 3, `Summarizer` 4, `Transport` 1, `PersonaHandler` 1, `EventWatcher` 1, `ExtensionSupervisor` 1, **`Connector` 0** [M: both groups]; this author's `rg -n 'dyn\s+(\w+::)*Connector\b'` over `crates/` and `apps/` finds 0 [M]. The difference is the grep form; the verdict per trait is the same.

**So: 1 of 15 traits (5 of 211 sites) leaves `async-trait` under the language's own rule — scheduled as work, M13**; three more (`Transport`, `PersonaHandler`, `EventWatcher`) could after a small enum redesign each (≈20–30 lines; no modernization gain on its own — scheduled as **optional work, M55 (a–c)**, last and droppable, so the owner sees the choice); the remaining eleven are `dyn`-dispatched by design (tool backends, providers, plugin executors, the gateway handler) and stay unless `dynosaur` is adopted (DU-R1). The dependency cannot leave any crate's manifest but `openalpaca_connectors` — and not even there while `impl Connector for PluginConnector` (`plugins/bridge/connector_bridge.rs:49`) needs the attribute, which is why M13 changes the plugins crate in the same commit. The whole-codebase number the owner asked to size: **5 of 211 sites removable; 0 further at low risk**; a `dynosaur` route would touch ≈89 `dyn` sites and add a dependency. M13 is independent of the rmcp bump (the task's example "async-trait after rmcp" has no dependency behind it: P19 touches `openalpaca_mcp` and `openalpaca_core`, not these traits) and is reversible by the connector-bridge wiring (CLAUDE.md: that bridge is "not yet wired into `ConnectorManager`"), which is where a `dyn Connector` would come back. Pedantic clippy's `large_futures` ×3 and `unused_async` ×10 are the only async-shape findings the measurement produced; neither depends on the attribute.

**Rev 2.0 — DU-R1 re-answered (b).** Under the owner's rule the boxed proc macro beside native AFIT is the older form, and dynosaur is the named way out of the language's blocker (no `dyn`-compatible async method through 1.99.0 [Docs: RELEASES.md]). So: `Connector` stays plain AFIT (M13); `Transport`, `PersonaHandler`, `EventWatcher` and `ExtensionSupervisor` become enums (M55 (a–d), before the series); the other ten — `PluginToolExecutor`, `ToolCallbackExecutor`, `PluginSkillExecutor`, `PluginAgentExecutor`, `LlmProvider`, `BuiltInTool`, `MessageHandler`, `ConnectorSendProvider`, `ContextSource`, `Summarizer` — are declared `fn m(&self, …) -> impl Future<Output = T> + Send` with `#[dynosaur::dynosaur(pub DynX = dyn(box) X)]`, every `dyn X` becoming `DynX<'_>` / `Arc<DynX<'static>>` (M58 (a–j), crate order api → llm → core); `async-trait` then leaves all 8 manifests (M59). dynosaur 0.3.1 (2026-07-03) accepts only the explicit `= dyn(box)` spelling and refuses a `where Self: Sized` method or an associated const [D: `dynosaur_derive` 0.3.1 `lib.rs:50-65`, `:346-352`, `:358`]; the erased type keeps the supertraits, so `DynX` stays `Send + Sync` [D: `:340-347`]. Budget for higher-ranked "`Send` is not general enough" errors in `BuiltInTool` (66 impls) and `LlmProvider` (57), which `async-trait`'s early boxing hid. Drop dynosaur for plain `dyn` when Rust stabilizes `dyn`-compatible async methods.

### 3.3 What folds into plan-rev3's P36, and what does not

P36 (`style: address clippy lints new in Rust 1.94–1.99, and the one deprecation`, Optional under DU-17 b/c) is the home of every default-warn item; this document corrects its **gate** (§2.6), **widens its scope** to the 66 mechanical items and the 17 let-chain sites (commit M07 — work under the owner's scope statement; rev 1's DU-D5 is dissolved, §2.5) and makes the one deprecation (M04) Required under ask (2) whatever DU-17 says. Classification of the **101** corrected 1.99.0 triples [M: rust-modernization §6; Appendix A of the deprecated-inventory report lists every `file:line`]:

| Class | Lints (count) | Of which new in 1.94–1.99 |
|---|---|---|
| **Mechanical — 66** (clippy's own suggestion; no semantic change; review by diff) | `useless_vec` 33 (all `tools/registry/tests.rs`), `useless_borrows_in_formatting` 6, `redundant_field_names` 5 (tests), `unnecessary_get_then_check` 4 (tests), `unnecessary_sort_by` 2, `single_element_loop` 2, `drain_collect` 2 (`plugins/manager.rs:950`, `:959` → `mem::take`), `useless_format` 2, `question_mark` 1, `manual_contains` 1 (test), `redundant_closure` 1, `needless_borrow` 1, `needless_borrows_for_generic_args` 1 (test), `clone_on_copy` 1 (test; the AES hunk of P11 removes it), `for_kv_map` 1, `redundant_guards` 1, `collapsible_match` 1, `deprecated` 1 | **17** (`useless_borrows_in_formatting` 6, `unnecessary_sort_by` 2, `single_element_loop` 2, `drain_collect` 2, `useless_format` 1, `question_mark` 1, `for_kv_map` 1, `collapsible_match` 1, `deprecated` 1) |
| **Judgement — 25** | `collapsible_if` 17 (let chains, §3.1 row 4), `field_reassign_with_default` 4 (tests), `derivable_impls` 2 (`agent/template/mod.rs:31`, `prompt_ctx/lifecycle.rs:16` — derive only if the manual `Default` is field-for-field the derive), `items_after_test_module` 1 (`commands/tasks.rs:559`), `new_without_default` 1 (`secret_store/mod.rs:174`) | 0 |
| **Allow with reason — 10** | `result_large_err` 6 (`routes/extensions.rs:684`, `routes/memories.rs:534`, `:542`, `:553`, `routes/tasks.rs:638` return `Result<_, Response>` like the 14 existing allows in `routes/`; `openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:663` against rmcp's `ClientInitializeError` — box it or allow it, P36's own either/or), `too_many_arguments` 2 (`routes/tasks.rs:936`, `telegram/handler.rs:29`; 62 existing allows), `unneeded_wildcard_pattern` 1 (`event_bridge.rs:37` — R79's explicit `created_by: _`, keep with `#[allow]`), `large_enum_variant` 1 (`compose/mod.rs:112`; boxing changes a public enum) | **6** (`result_large_err` 5, `unneeded_wildcard_pattern` 1) |

Sizing: P36 as written (the +23 only) is **17 mechanical one-liners + 6 allows**, well under an hour, and leaves the count at **78 under 1.99** — exactly the 1.93 baseline under the corrected counter. Taking every mechanical item is 66 edits (33 of them in one test file) and lands at 35; adding the 17 let-chain sites (clippy's own suggestion under edition 2024) lands at **18** — the 10 allows and the 8 remaining judgement items (`field_reassign_with_default` ×4, `derivable_impls` ×2, `items_after_test_module`, `new_without_default`), each a one-site decision the committer makes with a sentence. **That is M07's scope and gate: 101 → 18 on HEAD, 100 → 17 on the re-cut.** **Never a blanket `clippy --fix`** (P36's own text; it broke the build in September [M-sep per plan-rev3]). Three mechanical items need a second look despite the label: `drain_collect` → `mem::take` changes nothing only if the drained vector is not read again in the same expression; `unnecessary_sort_by` → `sort_by_key` must keep stability and the key's borrow; the `useless_vec` edits in `registry/tests.rs` must keep the arrays' types where a `Vec` was passed by value.

### 3.4 Dependency features not used yet, by crate [D; C]

- **rmcp 0.16 → 3.5.0** (P19; the wave already compiles against it [M-oct10]). What 3.5 serves that the bridge (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/`, 2,279 lines; `crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/mcp/`, 1,142 lines) does not use [D: `rmcp-3.5.0/src/handler/client.rs`, `service/client.rs`, `service/client/cache.rs`, `model.rs`; `SP/logs/rmcp-3.5.1-CHANGELOG.md`]: structured tool output (`CallToolResult.structured_content`, `Tool.output_schema` (`rmcp-3.5.0/src/model/tool.rs:30` [D]) — `bridge.rs:42` reads only `input_schema` and `ToolDefinition` has no output-schema slot: carrying it to the model widens the tool-definition shape every provider adapter reads — **DU-R4 (vi)**, recommend defer); resource links (`RawContent::ResourceLink`, `bridge.rs:115`); protocol cancellation (`Peer::send_cancellable_request` → `RequestHandle::{await_response, cancel}`, `service.rs:542`, `:555`, `:666`, `:853`; rmcp's own per-request timeout already sends `notifications/cancelled`, `:570`); progress notifications and a progress-aware request timeout (`ClientHandler::on_progress`; rmcp 1.7.0 #858 — `with_cancel_and_timeout` applies a fixed `request_timeout`, so a long call reporting progress would no longer time out at the fixed bound: a change to the timeout contract and a new log/UI surface); elicitation (`ClientHandler::create_elicitation`, default `Decline` — answering it is a new approval surface through the `ConfirmationBroker`, the same family as pending **T35**); tasks (SEP-2663; `client.rs:548` sets `task: None`; no surface in the loop or the Work pane); the client response cache (SEP-2549, `ClientCacheConfig::default()`: `enabled: true`, `max_entries: 512`, **`serve_stale_on_error: true`** — on by default per `Peer`; plan-rev3 DU-5/DU-25 already decided "the response cache (default)"; the one semantic to record in P19's body is that a stale cached response is served when a request errors); the modern client lifecycle (`ClientLifecycleMode::{Initialize, Discover, Auto}`; `serve_with_conn` uses `serve` = `Initialize`); OAuth (out of scope). The structured-output and resource-link rows are work (M14); protocol cancellation is **DU-R4 (v)** → M15 after P19 (its call path is the decision; §5.3); `output_schema` is **DU-R4 (vi)**; the progress-timeout, elicitation, tasks and lifecycle rows are **DU-R4 (i)–(iv)**; the cache semantic is recorded in P19's body, as is what remains of the `input_required`/MRTR check (Appendix B #6: how a server reacts to the Decline the wrapper feeds back).
  - **Rev 2.0:** DU-R4 re-answered — (i) the progress-reset timeout and (v) (b) the MRTR loop in M15; (iv) `Auto` in plan-rev3's P19 (rev 3.9); (vi) `output_schema` surfaced (M14) and validated (M82); (ii) elicitation and (iii) tasks are owner questions (M84, M83).
- **axum 0.8.9** (locked, latest): path syntax already `{param}` (4 of `router.rs`'s 111 routes); new and unused since 0.8.0 — `OptionalFromRequest` for `Json`/`Multipart`/`Extension`, `NoContent`, `Router::reset_fallback`, `method_not_allowed_fallback`, `WebSocketUpgrade::{selected_protocol, requested_protocols}`, binary SSE data, `ResponseAxumBodyLayer` [D: `axum-0.8.9/CHANGELOG.md`]. Nothing replaces a hand-written shape found; `NoContent` — one production site (`apps/openalpacad/src/routes/sessions.rs:828`), whose `IntoResponse` is exactly `StatusCode::NO_CONTENT.into_response()` [D: `axum-0.8.9/src/response/mod.rs:77-83`] — is not an older form: no change (rev 2.0).
- **tower-http 0.6.11 → 0.7.1** (P25): `CsrfLayer` (the `Sec-Fetch-Site` scheme) and `RequestBodyDeadlineLayer` / `ResponseBodyDeadlineLayer` — §3.1 row 21, **DU-R5**; the 0.7 breaking change (compression wildcard handling) touches nothing used [D].
- **tokio 1.49 → 1.53.1** (locked; 1.53.2 newest): `LocalRuntime` (1.51), `worker_index`, runtime names, `is_rt_shutdown_err` (1.50), `TcpStream::set_zero_linger`, `enable_eager_driver_handoff` (1.52), the task-schedule-latency metric and `File: From<OwnedFd>` (1.53) — none addresses a site [D: `tokio-1.53.2/CHANGELOG.md`]. Of the older primitives: `JoinSet` 0 uses (§3.1 row 15, M40), `CancellationToken::run_until_cancelled` (tokio-util 0.7.12; future-first after an up-front check since 0.7.16) for 28 hand-written two-arm races (M60, rev 2.0), `task_local!` 0 and no pattern wanting one, `select!` 40, `CancellationToken` 102 sites, the sync primitives all in use; the daemon's `tokio` features are the minimum the code uses, `full` nowhere.
- **reqwest 0.13.4** (locked; 0.13.5 newest): the 0.13 migration (`rustls` default, `rustls-tls` → `rustls`, `query`/`form` opt-in) is done — `Cargo.toml:81-85` reads `default-features = false, features = ["json", "rustls", "stream"]` [C]. Unused: `Error::is_dns()` (0.13.5) with `is_timeout`/`is_connect`/`is_request`/`is_body` for a **typed** classification at the providers' error paths (`openai/mod.rs:184`, `:232`, `:314`; `anthropic/mod.rs:126`, `:175`, `:254`; `ollama.rs:129`, `:196`, `:315`), which today flatten every `reqwest::Error` to a string the transient classifier cannot read — **M57, Required, a `fix:`** (rev 2.0; rev 1.1 called it a wording improvement at a mis-cited `lib.rs:88`); `ClientBuilder::http1_max_headers` — no need. The lock still carries reqwest 0.12.28 beside 0.13.4 and tokio-tungstenite 0.28.0 beside 0.29.0 (other crates' dependencies) until teloxide/twilight/axum move [M: `Cargo.lock`].
- **rusqlite 0.38 → 0.40.2** (P06): `TryFrom for Value` (0.39.0, #1819), unix-timestamp helpers, VTab constructors (unused), `UtcDateTime`, `From` for `FromSqlError`, bundled SQLite 3.53.2 (0.40.1, the tainted-SAVEPOINT fix #1854), MSRV 1.88 [Docs: rusqlite releases]. Our usage (`params!`, `execute`, `prepare`, `query_row`, `exists`, `OptionalExtension`, `types::Value`) needs nothing new.
- **rustyline 17.0.2 → 18.0.1** (P27): the `Prompt` trait for styling, `NO_COLOR` honoured, minimal repaint, "Replace fd-lock with std File::lock" (#909); 18.0.1 fixes `page_completions` [Docs]. Our surface is `Editor::<ReplHelper, FileHistory>::with_config`, `Config::builder`, the five helper traits (`apps/openalpaca/src/repl/`).
- **tokio-tungstenite 0.28 → 0.30** (P26): both changelog entries read only "Update `tungstenite`"; the tungstenite 0.29/0.30 changes were **not read** [NE]. Our use is two CLI clients (`commands/tail.rs:11`, `commands/confirmations.rs:42`) and one daemon test.
- **thiserror 2.0.20**: §3.1 row 13 (M19–M38). **clap 4.6.6 → 4.6.7**: `#[command(defer = …)]` (M61, rev 2.0), `Command::get_overridden_usage`; 4.6.0 raised the MSRV to 1.85; no deprecation in the range [Docs]. **teloxide 0.17.0, twilight 0.17.1**: at the latest; nothing new. **tracing 0.1.44**: 0 `#[instrument]`, 12 `info_span!` + `.instrument(..)` — a style choice. **tauri 2.11.5** (Rust side): five `#[tauri::command]`s, `Builder::default().plugin(tauri_plugin_dialog::init())`, `async_runtime::spawn_blocking` ×2; the shell's unused `directories` declaration (`src-tauri/Cargo.toml:24`) is M18's manifest line; rev 2.0 adds the shell's Tauri adoptions — the CSP's `ipc:` source and hardening, an app manifest for the five commands, `removeUnusedCommands`, the download, new-window and navigation handlers, `preventOverflow`, `opener::reveal` (M72–M79) — and plan-rev3's Q11–Q17.

### 3.5 Things not to do, and the rule each would break

1. **Memory search semantics.** No "modern" tokenizer, no new `str` API and no reordering inside `crates/openalpaca_storage/src/memory_index/` (`query.rs`'s `normalize`, `is_unsegmented`, `is_prefix_term`, `split_scripts`, the chunker, `authority_rank`, `record_for`) without bumping `SCHEMA_VERSION` (6) and running design 09 §6.5's retrieval proxy on parent and change — CLAUDE.md Gotchas *The memory index layout is design 09 §6.5 (LQ17)*, and MD-1 (lexical by design; never an embedder). The pedantic `case_sensitive_file_extension_comparisons` at `dirstat.rs:30` is inside this boundary. And CLAUDE.md's read-first rule for memory code binds every commit that touches `memory_files`, `memory_index`, `memory::service` or `memory::learning`, however behaviour-identical by reading: read 03 §7a/§7c and the 09 revision-log entry of the slice concerned first — M05's `cut_bytes` (`memory/service/mod.rs:2444`, called by `in_chat.rs:606`, `:699`, `:701`, `:749` for `memory_confirm`'s preview, an approval surface under `tasks/approval-hidden-chars.md` R2, and by `mod.rs:1453`, `:1529`) and M21–M24 are inside that rule, and their gates name the reading and the `in_chat` and hidden-chars suites.
2. **The approval escape set.** `openalpaca_core::utils::text::{escape_hidden, escape_hidden_line, escape_hidden_within, hidden_notice, cut_notice}` and `apps/openalpaca-gui/src/lib/hidden-chars.json` are one table pinned from both sides; no rewrite onto `char::escape_*` or a Unicode-class predicate — CLAUDE.md Gotchas *An approval surface shows third-party text escaped*, `tasks/approval-hidden-chars.md` A1–A32. An `uninlined_format_args` pass (M45–M52) must not touch those format strings' text.
3. **The schema.** No migration for a cleanup; `001`–`004` are not edited; a new one is `005_<name>.sql` registering 5 — SQ-1. Nothing in this document needs one.
4. **`strict_mode_compatible`** (`openalpaca_llm/src/providers/openai_tools.rs:106`): the allowlist is the rule, not a lint to simplify; any tool-schema restructuring keeps `every_builtin_goes_to_openai_with_strict_only_where_strict_mode_accepts_it` green — Gotchas, residue R8.
5. **The five-part `file_write` result** (`file_ops.rs:491`, `save_as_artifact`; pinned by `a_by_product_result_is_pinned_whole_in_five_parts`) and every other refusal sentence a test pins: a thiserror derive (M19–M38) or a `derivable_impls` fix that alters one byte of a `Display` is a behaviour change — Gotchas *`file_write` … written for the model that reads it*.
6. **`test-home-guard` and the home-store guards** are not a cost to remove (§1.2 step 3 is their measured price, not an argument); any test a modernization adds that resolves, registers or lists projects holds its crate's guard, and no test reads `~/.openalpaca` — Gotchas, Feature Flags.
7. **`event_bridge.rs:37`** `created_by: _` stays — R79, P36's text: under `#[allow(clippy::unneeded_wildcard_pattern)]` from P36 (M07), under `#[expect(clippy::unneeded_wildcard_pattern, reason = "R79")]` from M39 (rev 2.0). Rev 1's "stays with `#[allow]`" is superseded by M39's rev 2.0 bullet; the pattern itself stays.
8. **No blanket `cargo clippy --fix`** — P36 (it broke the build in September).
9. **Enforcement points, widenings and contract changes are decisions, not modernizations**: `CsrfLayer`, `RequestBodyDeadlineLayer`, answering MCP elicitation, a progress-reset timeout, `dynosaur`, a new lint at `warn`, a new CI or bun gate — each hits one of rule 5's triggers, so each is a DU in §5 and none rides in on a modernization commit — CLAUDE.md *Settled rules*, last bullet. (`#[expect]` and a `JoinSet` whose abort-on-drop is stated are work: M39, M40. Superseded in part: rev 1.1 scoped `#[expect]` to the allows that hold under every documented selection; M39's rev 2.0 bullet converts every allow, with `cfg_attr` where a lint is selection-dependent.) **Rev 2.0:** the owner's rule of 2026-10-12 re-answers several of them (§5) — `dynosaur` (DU-R1 (b)), a lint table and a warnings gate (DU-R3), the progress-reset timeout (DU-R4 (i)), the JS gates (DU-D1, DU-D2) — and §5's entries are their presentation; answering MCP elicitation stays an owner question with T35, and `CsrfLayer`/`RequestBodyDeadlineLayer` stay out (DU-R5: no older form to migrate from; a bearer token on loopback, no ambient credential for the `Sec-Fetch-Site` scheme to defend).
10. **AES/digest formats**: nothing under `key_encryption/` or the SHA-256 naming beyond the commits plan-rev3 schedules for them (P05's fixture, P10's sha2, P11's aes-gcm). The byte-format fixture landed as P05, `afb1da60` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/key_encryption/tests.rs:252`, `a_secret_written_by_the_aes_gcm_0_10_build_still_decrypts`); rev 1's "still unwritten" is superseded (rev 2.2, F-15).
11. **`event_log`**: no retention sweeper rides in on a "cleanup" — W6.
12. **`Atomic::update`** (rev 2.0; rev 1.1 named `try_update`): the infallible form 1.95 stabilized — `let prev = self.next_spill.update(Ordering::Relaxed, Ordering::Relaxed, |current| current.max(floor) + 1); let number = prev.max(floor);` — both `Ordering::Relaxed` arguments kept, the sequence-number contract (`session_log/mod.rs:502-513`) unchanged.
13. **`async-trait` removal by force** (hand-written `Pin<Box<dyn Future + Send + '_>>` returns on the 14 dyn traits) is what the macro already generates, with more lines and no gain; `dynosaur` is the only principled route and it is DU-R1. **Rev 2.0:** DU-R1 is re-answered (b) — dynosaur for the ten open-set traits, enums for the four closed sets, `async-trait` gone (M55, M58, M59).
14. **No source change rides into Part I's commits** beyond what plan-rev3 schedules: Part III's source commits follow P38 (§6.3), so every Part I gate anchor (G-lint's counts, the fmt hunk counts, P31/P32/P33's CSS hash and dist bytes) stays as measured; the exceptions are M04/M07, which *are* P36. *Rev 2.3:* plan-rev3 rev 3.14's §5.4 schedules the ZCode audit's N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3 and N-MCP-4 inside Part I, at Phase G's head before P19, and lets a pre-cut fix not ready by the cut land in the wave before the commit its place names (§6.3 steps 1a and 2); those are plan-rev3's commits, measured by its gates, not Part III source riding into Part I.
15. **The singleton lock's rule** ("the process, then the lock, never the port", `daemon_lifecycle.rs`) is unchanged by M17: the primitive may change, the arbiter does not; a swap that made the probe read the port, or that dropped the CLI's cross-process `LockHolder` test (`apps/openalpaca/src/test_util.rs:160-245`), would reverse an accepted design.
16. **No `cargo clean`, sweep or `target/` deletion by an agent, a hook or a cron unasked** — DU-M3's one fixed point; the rule for agents was and stays "never build in the repository" while a session is attributing growth, and M01's threshold line is an instruction to a person.
17. **ZCode's trust defaults** (rev 2.3; the ZCode audit's §6). Where a commit here borrows a mechanism from ZCode (`/Users/tangyunpei/dev/AlpacaCode/ZCode`, Apache-2.0, read as reference — every borrowing is a design written fresh, never a code port, the audit's §1), it does not borrow the default beside it: M84 attributes no elicitation by server name and answers none when no call is in flight, and keeps no answer; M77 hands no `file:` URL to the operating system (ST-18's Open-by-id stays the only way to open a local file); no default pre-push hook rides in with M64–M67 (the full suite takes about 5.5 min; an opt-in format hook is an owner option, the audit's Z7-17, not scheduled); M15 lets no server's progress extend the sandbox's clock unless the owner answers DU-R4 (i)'s sub-question yes. The audit's §6 rows — a model on any memory path (MD-1), read-only shell auto-allow (ST-14), unconfined tool paths (R31/R32), a run-wide allow (R43), no cost budget (N4), plugins enabled on install (ADR-030), no CSP — are reversals for the owner, never modernizations; items 1, 2 and 9 above and CLAUDE.md's *Settled rules* already hold most of them.

---

## 4. New APIs and features — GUI and toolchain

**Source:** the web-modernization group (`SP/reports/web-modernization.md`, second pass ≈ 09:20Z; logs `SP/logs/web-00…21-*`; four `git archive` exports of `7295f91b` under `SP/web-export*`, the scratch Bun 1.4.2 with `BUN_INSTALL_CACHE_DIR=SP/bun-cache`, Node v24.15.0; no cargo call, no daemon, nothing written in the repository or under `~/.bun` by that group [M]), with the deprecated-inventory group's ESLint, tsc, knip and grep results where they overlap. Every wall time was taken under sibling agents' load (5–24) and is indicative only; the comparisons that matter were taken minutes apart on the same machine. **One rule-1 finding, not this group's**: `~/.bun/install/cache` received 4,869 files in two batches (08:18:35Z and 08:24:02Z) from the deprecated-inventory group's `bun add` and `bun x` in `SP/wt-js`, run without `BUN_INSTALL_CACHE_DIR`; nothing under `~/.bun/bin` changed, and one manifest stamped 01:22:46 local has no logged writer [M: web-modernization's header]. Nobody cleaned it (that would be a write there too); the owner may.

**The headline:** most of what React 19.2/19.3, TanStack Query 5, zustand 5, Tailwind 4.1–4.3, Vite 8, Vitest 5, TypeScript 7 and Bun 1.4 added has **no site** in `apps/openalpaca-gui` or sits on a design row the owner already decided (G1, P2, F6, D-G). The work is short — one rename, six dead files, one measured test-pool switch, a typed `skipToken` rewrite, thirteen Rules-of-React sites — and the rest is an owner decision with a "no" or "not now" recommendation, except `bunfig.toml`'s `minimumReleaseAge`, which is plan-rev3's own 7-day rule as a tool.

| 20 | Actions / `useActionState` / `useOptimistic` / `use` / `useTransition` / metadata hoisting / Fragment refs / `useId` / Tauri 2.12's new JS APIs / marked 18.1's options / DOMPurify `SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES` or `USE_PROFILES` | new-api | 0 sites (one synchronous `<form>`, `SessionSidebar.tsx:203`; the one `<title>` is inside an `<svg>`, which React does not hoist; the app's `@tauri-apps/api` surface is `invoke` ×2 files and `getCurrentWindow().isDecorated()`) | — | — | none (`USE_PROFILES: { html: true }` would drop SVG/MathML from rendered markdown — a behaviour change with no need shown; `SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES` the README advises against in production) | — | — | **no site** | [C] [Docs] |

### 4.2 The React Compiler, with the healthcheck numbers [M: `web-02…04-healthcheck*.log`, `web-09…15-*`, `web-19-oxc-apptest-verbose.log`]

`react-compiler-healthcheck` 1.0.0 reports **"Successfully compiled 295 out of 295 components"** on `src/**/!(*.test).{ts,tsx}` (6/6 on the test files, 301/301 on the whole glob; `--src ./src` reports "0 out of 0" — a directory is not a glob), "StrictMode usage found", "no usage of incompatible libraries". **That figure is optimistic.** The shipping compiler on its two Vite hosts (both need P32 + P33: `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.0.0 dropped Babel and Vite ≤ 7; today's `@vitejs/plugin-react-swc` has no compiler path):

| Stack (exports of `7295f91b`) | check | test | build (wall; Vite's "built in") | JS bytes in `dist/assets` | compiled / skipped |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HEAD: Vite 7.3.6 / Vitest 4.1.11 / plugin-react-swc 4.3.3 | 0.36–0.39 s | 9.27–10.58 s (Duration 9.51 s) | 1.21–1.60 s; 1.06 s | 740,645 (19 chunks) [M-oct10] | — |
| Vite 8.3.4 / Vitest 5.0.3 (P32), 0 config edits | 0.31–0.63 s | 8.71–10.45 s (9.61 s) | **0.58–0.68 s; 0.24 s** | **742,230 (18 chunks)** [M: `web-07-gates-v8-build-1.log` 742.15 kB; `SP/web-export-v8/…/dist/assets` summed] — rev 1 wrote 775,895 here, yesterday's `gui-C`, which is P31 + P32 [M-oct10] | — |
| + plugin-react 6.1.2 + compiler, **Babel host** (`@rolldown/plugin-babel` 0.2.4 + `reactCompilerPreset()`, `babel-plugin-react-compiler` 1.0.0 — the documented path) | 0.35–0.64 s | **2 of 1,551 fail**: `App.test.tsx`'s first lazy `ChatView` `findByRole("heading", {name: "Chat"})` times out at 1,077–1,323 ms (3/3 alone) against 383 ms uncompiled — a transform-latency artefact; **1,551/1,551 in 14.03 s** with `configure({ asyncUtilTimeout: 5000 })` | 3.05–3.54 s; 2.85 s (a second parse of every file) | 832,166 (**+89,936 B, +12.1 %** over P32) | no diagnostics printed (the preset's logger is off) — the skip set is **[NE]** |
| + plugin-react 6.1.2 + compiler, **native oxc host** (`oxc-transform-react` 0.153.0, `react({ compiler: { reportDiagnostics: true } })`, "experimental" in the plugin's 6.1.0 changelog) | 0.41–0.74 s | 1,551/1,551, 13.1–13.5 s (first lazy load 433 ms) | 0.68–0.96 s; 0.32 s | **877,608 (+135,378 B, +18.2 % over P32's 742,230)** | **15 diagnostics at 13 locations in 7 files, every one a component or hook the compiler skipped**: 11 × `react-compiler(Refs)` "Cannot access refs during render" (`useChat.ts:222`, `:224`; `useDaemonEvents.ts:36`; `useChatSession.ts:458`, `:722`, `:727`, `:979`, `:1026`, `:1335`; `useComposerAttachments.ts:77`), 2 × `PreserveManualMemo` ("Existing memoization could not be preserved"), 2 × `Todo` (unsupported syntax: a computed-key destructure, a member-expression default parameter) |

Reading: both hosts compile the app and the suite passes under both; the healthcheck's 295/295 is **not** the compiler's verdict — 13 locations are skipped, 11 of them the latest-ref pattern the lint names too; the Babel host hides that (no logger), the oxc host prints it. The costs are measured (JS **+135,378 B / +18.2 %** over P32 on the oxc host and **+89,936 B / +12.1 %** on the Babel host, against today's Vite-8-alone export of 742,230 B — rev 1 computed "+13 % / +101,713 B" against yesterday's `gui-C`, 775,895 B, which is P31's bumps plus Vite 8 [M-oct10] and remains plan-rev3 P32's gate anchor for the wave, where P32 lands after P31; the two stacks differ by P31's ≈ +33 kB; build +0.1–0.3 s oxc / +2.4 s Babel; test wall +3–4 s under oxc and two `findByRole` budgets broken under Babel). The benefit is **[NE]**: no render-performance baseline or complaint exists, the docs say to leave the 62 existing `useMemo`/`useCallback` sites in place and let the compiler cover *new* code, and plan-rev3's G4 (streaming render) is the one place a render-cost question is on record — measure-first under its §7.5 protocol, not a compiler flag. Hence rev 1.1's DU-W1 recommendation (a). **Rev 2.0:** the owner's rule re-answers it (b) — §5.4 and M69 — and adds a fact the run above did not record: `@rolldown/plugin-babel` 0.2.4 has a required peer `@babel/core ^7.29.0 || ^8.0.0-rc.1`, Bun installs a required peer at its highest version (8.0.7, 2026-10-07), and `babel-plugin-react-compiler` 1.0.0 errors on `AssignmentPattern` (react#36868) and silently skips components with destructured parameter defaults (react#37406) under Babel 8 — ≈ 53 such components and hooks in 44 files here [M: the React verifier]. The Babel-host row's figures (+89,936 B, the two `findByRole` budgets, "no diagnostics printed") are therefore [NE] as compiler evidence until re-measured on `@babel/core` 7.29.7 with the logger on (M69's gate). The 13 sites themselves split [C]: **genuine `useEffectEvent` candidates** — `useDaemonEvents.ts:35` (the hook's own comment: "the handler is held in a ref, so an inline arrow does not re-subscribe" — exactly the API's purpose), `useChat.ts:221-224` (`acceptsRef`, `streamIdRef`, read inside the effect at `:240-256`), and the effect-side reads in `useChatSession.ts:755-766` / `:913-921`; **ref kept, the assignment moved into an effect** — `useChatSession.ts:1335` (`respondRef`, read by `approve`/`deny`, which are handed on to `useGlobalKeys` and the confirmation store: the docs forbid passing an Effect Event on), `useComposerAttachments.ts:77` (read inside a handler, not an effect), and the handler-side reads of `laneKeyRef`/`confirmationMetaRef`; **two `useMemo` ref reads re-homed** (`:979` reads `agents.current` at `:994`; `:1026`). The three run-once effects that only initialise a ref (`ConnectionSection.tsx:308`, `ProjectsCard.tsx:210`, `:302`) were not flagged.

### 4.3 Per library: what is new, and what has a site

**Rev 2.4 — the versions the wave lands at D1 (a)'s cut** [the compatibility report §2.2, §2.4]. The readings below were made on the versions each names; Part I takes patch releases over several of them, and Part III's entries are written over these: react and react-dom **19.3.1** (an RSC transport fix only; the app uses no RSC), @tanstack/react-query **5.104.2** (no release notes exist for its tag), dompurify **3.4.17** (`IN_PLACE` fixes), marked **18.1.1** (parser edge cases), vitest **5.0.4** (`fsModuleCache` now on by default; it writes `<repo root>/node_modules/.vitest-cache`, which `.gitignore`'s `node_modules/` covers, and a `.vitest-secret-token` under `$HOME/Library/Application Support/vitest` — the report's rev 2, §8.3), @testing-library/user-event **14.6.8** (a null input value read as empty; C4), with vite 8.3.4, @vitejs/plugin-react 6.1.2, jsdom 30.1.2, prettier 3.9.10 and typescript 7.0.2 as named below; Bun **1.4.3** (P42); and the Tauri JS pair, `@tauri-apps/api` **2.12.4**, `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` **2.8.3** and `@tauri-apps/cli` **2.12.3**. C4 found the notes of its JS literals — react and react-dom, @tanstack/react-query, dompurify, marked, vitest and user-event — to show bug fixes only, react-query 5.104.2 having no notes to read (the report's §7); the Tauri pair's 2.12.4 fixes a panic in the IPC and asset protocol handlers on an empty URI path (the review's MF-1); Bun 1.4.3's own notes were not read (the report's §7). By what was read, no row below gains or loses a site; each entry's [Docs] reading is re-read at the version taken when its commit lands (MF-1).

- **React 19.2 → 19.3** [Docs: the 19.2 (2025-10-01) and 19.3 (2026-09-09) posts; C]. Inventory at HEAD (non-test): 40 effect sites in 18 files (38 `useEffect`, 2 `useLayoutEffect`), 62 manual-memo sites (23 `useMemo`, 39 `useCallback`), `useState` 115 lines, `useRef` 47, `useSyncExternalStore` 2 (`hooks/useConnection.ts:50`, `:58`), `lazy(` 6, one `<Suspense>` (`App.tsx:90`), `<StrictMode>` (`main.tsx:24`); **zero** `forwardRef`, `memo(`, `use(`, `useTransition`/`startTransition`/`useDeferredValue`, `useActionState`/`useOptimistic`/`useFormStatus`, `useEffectEvent`, `<Activity`, `ViewTransition`, `useId`, `createPortal`, `flushSync`, document-metadata elements, `hydrateRoot`/`renderTo*`. Sites: `useEffectEvent` → row 5 (M43); `<Activity>` → DU-W2; View Transitions → DU-W3; Actions, `use`, `useOptimistic` → no site (server state is TanStack: 41 `useMutation`, 50 `useQuery`, 4 `useInfiniteQuery`, 4 `useQueries`; the one optimistic write, `useTogglePin` at `hooks/useArtifacts.ts:220-235`, is already done in the cache; the rename `<form>` is synchronous); Fragment refs, Trusted Types pass-through (DU-W7), `onFullscreenChange`, `maskType`, `fetchPriority`, `useId`'s `_r_` prefix, Suspense SSR batching and Web Streams (client-rendered app), Performance Tracks (a profiler, usable from a Chrome tab against `bun run dev` when plan-rev3's G4 wants a frontend profile) → no site. 19.3's behaviour changes against this code: Transitions render independently (0 transitions), StrictMode double-invokes effects during *hydration* (0 `hydrateRoot`), `innerHTML` not re-set when unchanged (the two `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` sites get fewer DOM writes with the same content), `resize` events batched to the next frame (`ChatView.tsx:183-199`'s `fit` sees one batched update per frame — same outcome, later by at most a frame), `submit` events carry `submitter` (the rename form ignores it); main chunk +30,035 B [M-oct10: gui B2]. P31 moves it.
- **@tanstack/react-query 5.102.8 → 5.104.1** [Docs: query-core CHANGELOG]: 5.103.0 exported manager types, a shared refetch-interval resolver, a partial-key filter fix (our keys end in objects, not `undefined`), a hydration cleanup; 5.103.1 structural sharing with own-`constructor` objects; 5.103.2 a cache-removal fix; 5.104.0 "Build projects with Vite 8" — nothing in the range touches an API this app calls; P31 is a plain bump. The 4.0 engine is in use through `@tailwindcss/vite` 4.3.3; `starting:`/`@starting-style` and container queries have 0 sites (`components/shell/pane-fit.ts`'s collapse-only state cannot be a container query); no `tailwind.config.*` or `safelist` ever existed (the GUI started on `tailwindcss ^4.1.18`, `f533ed9e`). Rev 2.0 (M09 widened): `.sc`'s `@layer utilities` definition becomes `@utility sc { … }` (v4's replacement for v3's custom-utility form [Docs: tailwindcss.com upgrade guide]), the two `oaP` animations `--animate-*` tokens with their `@keyframes` in `@theme`, and the 17 `duration-[Nms]` values `duration-N`, DESIGN_SPEC §1.7–1.9's code updated with them; the 146 `[&_…]` variants are first-class v4 syntax and stay (no `@utility prose-compact`). Lightning CSS under Vite 8 (P32): the minified CSS differs from esbuild's by 147 diff lines [M-oct10: `gui-css-B2-vs-C.diff`]; rendering in the three webviews [NE], carried by E5.
- **Vite 7.3.6 → 8.3.4** [Docs: vite.dev/blog/announcing-vite8, /guide/migration; C]: Rolldown replaces esbuild + Rollup, Oxc minifies, Lightning CSS minifies CSS, `build.rollupOptions` → `rolldownOptions` (deprecated alias), `esbuild` → `oxc`, the browser floor Safari 16.0 → 16.4 (our explicit `target: "es2022"`, `vite.config.ts:34`), `build.commonjsOptions` a no-op, the object-form `manualChunks` removed, Node 20.19+/22.12+. `vite.config.ts` (`:11-43`) uses none of the renamed options → **nothing to adopt and nothing to rename**; the environment API and `experimental.enableNativePlugin` are not stable and nothing here authors a plugin. New: `vitest doctor` (row 3), `fsModuleCache` (a per-developer on-disk cache, not for CI's clean checkout; would need `.vitest/` in `.gitignore`, which has 0 mention of it — *rev 2.4: 5.0.4 writes it at `<repo root>/node_modules/.vitest-cache`, which `.gitignore:18`'s `node_modules/` already covers; the compatibility report's rev 2, §8.3*), `sharedViteServer`, `vi.when` (rev 2.0: the argument-dispatching `mockImplementation`s that remain after plan-rev3's Q07 move to it, in Q07), `--project -p` (one project, one config), browser mode / Trace View / strict locators (0 browser tests), type testing (0 `*.test-d.ts`), the reporters (the gate uses stdout), `vitest --changed` (a dev-loop flag). `vitest.setup.ts` needs nothing.

Against the two tsconfigs: `baseUrl` already absent with the comment "TypeScript 7 removed it" (`tsconfig.json:29-30`), `types` explicit in both, `rootDir` moot under `noEmit`, `moduleResolution: bundler` — **nothing to change**. `target`/`lib`: the code's newest built-ins are `.at()` (ES2022, 6 sites), `findLast` (ES2023, `views/chat/transcript-model.ts:429`), `Object.hasOwn` (ES2022, `views/settings/key-rows.ts:27`); no ES2024/2025 built-in; the real output floor is Vite's `build.target: "es2022"` (the three webviews) — raising `lib` to ES2024 would admit built-ins the webview floor may lack: no change until the floor is declared (rev 2.0: plan-rev3's Q18 sets `target`/`lib` from the owner's macOS floor; M68 drops the empty `DOM.Iterable`) (a raised config compiles clean, 0 diagnostics [M: `depr-tsc.log`], so it is a choice, not a fix). `erasableSyntaxOnly` → DU-D4. `bun run check` (two `tsc --noEmit` under 7.0.2) is 0.31–0.74 s; `--checkers` is moot at that size.

Mapped: `minimumReleaseAge` — a **Bun 1.3** feature (2025-10-10), honoured by the owner's 1.3.13 [Docs: the 1.3 release post, fetched today], not one of 1.4's — → DU-W6 (M44); `packageManager` and `bun audit` in CI are P01 and P34; the v2 lock is DU-13 (a); `bun test` instead of Vitest — no (RTL + jsdom with 60 `vi.mock`, fake timers, `@tailwindcss/vite` and `css: true` — Bun's runner has no jsdom environment or Vite plugin pipeline); `bun check` (1.4.3) — `tsc` 7 already answers in 0.3 s; `bun outdated --filter`, `bun pm diff`, `bun why` — review conveniences, not gates.

### 4.4 Two things to confirm with the GUI owner, not deprecations

- The three `outline-none` sites (`components/overlays/CommandPalette.tsx:178`, `components/ui/Resizer.tsx:125`, `components/chat/Composer.tsx:452`) are v4-native and correct as written, but in Tailwind 4 `outline-none` really sets `outline-style: none`, where v3's `outline-none` (now `outline-hidden`) kept an invisible outline that still shows in forced-colors mode [Docs: the v4 upgrade guide] — an accessibility intent to confirm (Appendix B), not a rename.
- `isolate: false` and `vmForks` are not usable with this suite as written (`WorkPane.tsx:101`, `persona.ts:29`); if either is ever wanted, those two sites change first.
