## 5. Owner decisions

Each row below is a decision because it hits one of rule 5's triggers — an **enforcement point** (a lint, CI or bun gate, a required tool, a hook), a **widened component** (a new crate or dependency, a moved boundary), a **reversed row of an accepted design**, or a change to **user-visible behaviour or an on-disk or wire format**. (Rev 1 also listed DU-D5, the widened P36, "because it restates a plan-rev3 decision"; that is none of the four triggers, and rev 1.1 dissolved it — §2.5, §5.2.) Nothing here is adopted; the shipped code and configuration are the "no change" answer. When taken, each is recorded as an *Owner decisions taken* row in `tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §0. The eleventh subsection, *Owner decisions taken — `chore/toolchain-and-deps`, 2026-10-11*, already exists (`tasks/api-fix-plan.md:250` at HEAD), so plan-rev3's P38 and this file's M54 **update** it — P38 with Part I's answers and the 2026-10-12 re-answers, M54 with Part III's (rev 2.2, SF-18; rev 2.1 said P38 "adds" it) and as a closed row in `tasks/pending-decisions.md` (rev bumped), with the commit that applies it.

**Crosswalk from the groups' numbering.** target-attribution OD-1 → **DU-M1**, OD-2 → **DU-M2**, OD-3 → **DU-M3**, OD-4 → **DU-M4**, OD-5 → **DU-M5**, OD-6 → **DU-M6**, OD-7 (placement) → folded into §6; deprecated-inventory DU-A → **DU-D1**, DU-C → **DU-D3**, DU-D (boxed futures for the fourteen `dyn` traits) → **DU-R1** (with rust-modernization's RM-1); rust-modernization RM-1 → **DU-R1**, RM-5 → **DU-R3**, RM-7 → **DU-R4**, RM-8 → **DU-R5**, its "veto point" on `file-lock` → **DU-R2**; web-modernization DW-1 → **DU-W1**, DW-2 → **M43** (work; rev 1 made it a condition on DU-D1/DU-W1, withdrawn in rev 1.1), DW-3 → **DU-W2**, DW-4 → **DU-W3**, DW-5 → **DU-W4**, DW-6 → **DU-W5**, DW-7 → **DU-W6**, DW-8 → **DU-W7**, DW-9 → **DU-D2**, W16 → **DU-D4**; rev 1's **DU-D5** (P36's scope and DU-17's split) is dissolved in rev 1.1 — P36's widening is work (M07), DU-17's gate half is DU-R3. **22 decisions.** **Rev 2.3:** the ZCode audit adds **DU-R6** (MCP images, audio and blobs kept as by-products, M85) — **23 decisions** — and three sub-questions inside existing rows, each pending with a recommendation: DU-D3's interpreter pin (the audit's A-36), DU-R3's suppression ratchet (A-37, Z7-10) and DU-R4 (i)'s sandbox clock (Z5-06 (c)). Its other owner decisions (the audit's B-01…B-27: Always-allow scope, T35's answer, skills' confirmation lists, extension children's environment, connector approvals, migration 005's usage and telemetry rows, a LICENSE and notices, the `tooling` CI job, `trustedDependencies`, the dependency-edge check, …) are not Part III commits and are recorded in `tasks/pending-decisions.md` (rev 5); where one would amend a commit here (B-23 at DU-D3 and M63, B-24 at M44, B-20/B-21's migration 005 at §6.4's last paragraph), that entry names it as pending.

**Rev 2.0 — the owner's rule of 2026-10-12** (`CLAUDE.md:52`): "don't compromise when migrating to new apis and new functionalities for both rust and tauri and react". On 2026-10-11 the owner took every recommendation below; where a recommendation was a compromise — a "not now", a "no change" where the new form exists, a partial adoption — its entry now ends with a **Rev 2.0** paragraph giving the new answer and why. That paragraph is the presentation CLAUDE.md's *Settled rules* last bullet asks for (several re-answers add an enforcement point or widen a component); the re-answers are taken with this revision and recorded as *Owner decisions taken* rows by M54. Where a settled product decision conflicts and no form keeps it as written, the entry says **owner question** and gives a recommendation instead.

### 5.1 The target directory — DU-M1…M6

**DU-M1 — the canonical invocation set and `default-members`.** *Trigger:* reverses `Cargo.toml:15-16`'s documented row ("Bare `cargo build` / `cargo test` covers the library crates only; the apps … build when named with -p or --workspace"). *Facts [M: §1.2 steps 1–2; §1.10]:* the bare command and the CI command build separate towers (core/storage stems 1 → 2); one tower of 2.73 GiB / 7,975 paths replaces 3.60 GiB / 10,866 paths per lock state; a cold bare `cargo build` would take 22 s longer (52 s vs 30 s in the same session) and compile the daemon and CLI; a bare `cargo test` would run the daemon's tests (≈ 5.5 min for the whole suite, process-spawning, the `managers::mcp` `sleep 3600` orphans). *Options:* **(a)** no change; **(b)** `default-members` = every member except `apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri` (M02), the comment rewritten; **(c)** keep `default-members`, rely on M01's CLAUDE.md sentence alone. *Recommendation:* **(b) + M01's sentence**, presented as the reversal it is. *Under each answer:* (b) → M02 lands, the bare build joins CI's selection among the selections M39 checks (the `-p` and `live-eval` selections remain), and a bare `cargo clippy` reports the project's "clippy 78" baseline set; (a)/(c) → M02 is dropped, the bare-vs-workspace duplicate stays — −0.87 GiB per lock state not taken, **only in a session that runs both forms** (under M01's line (1) none does, so the measured saving is a convenience, not a growth driver) — and M39 keeps the bare build as a selection of its own.

**DU-M2 — incremental compilation off for agent and one-shot builds.** *Trigger:* changes every agent session's edit-rebuild time (a dev-loop behaviour change), and (b)/(c) bind the owner's own builds. *Facts [M: §1.3, §1.7]:* −53 % bytes / −70 % paths on the documented set, −42 % on one crate's test build, no path inflation on edits, one-shot cold builds faster (CI set 144 s → 118 s); edit-rebuild 6 s → 30 s (workspace test build after a core edit), 2 s → 6 s (storage alone); CI already sets `CARGO_INCREMENTAL: "0"` (`ci.yml:10` [C]) and Cargo 1.99 does so under `CI` by default [Docs]; the `env` key of Claude Code's settings is documented — "Set environment variables for every session and for the subprocesses Claude Code starts from it", scope any file, project values applied after the workspace is trusted, overriding a shell export, `CARGO_INCREMENTAL` not on the list project settings cannot set [Docs: `code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference#env`, `/docs/en/env-vars`, fetched to `SP/logs/fetch/rev2/`]; whether the owner's installed build matches that page, and whether other agent runtimes read the file, is [NE]. *Options:* **(a)** `.claude/settings.json` → `"env": {"CARGO_INCREMENTAL": "0"}` for Claude Code sessions in this repository — agents only, the owner's shell untouched (M03). Two forms: **(a-i)** the tracked file — `.gitignore:139` (`.claude/`) excludes the whole directory, and Git cannot re-include a file whose parent directory is excluded [Docs: gitignore(5), PATTERN FORMAT; not re-fetched today], so `:139` goes, `:140` (`.claude/*`) stays and `!.claude/settings.json` is added after it; **(a-ii)** `.claude/settings.local.json` on this machine only (untracked; the file exists, 35 B [M]), no commit. **(b)** `.cargo/config.toml` → `[build] incremental = false` — everyone, every profile. **(c)** `Cargo.toml` → `[profile.test] incremental = false` — every `cargo test`, `cargo build` keeps incremental. **(d)** nothing. *Recommendation:* **(a-i)** now, with the 6 s → 30 s number stated; (c) if the owner finds 30 s acceptable in their own loop. *Under each answer:* (a) → M03 as written; (b)/(c) → M03 becomes a one-line `.cargo/config.toml` or `Cargo.toml` commit and the owner's own loop pays the 30 s; (d) → the 53 % stays.

**DU-M3 — a sweep tool.** *Trigger:* a required tool. *Facts [M: §1.8, §1.9]:* nothing on stable cargo prunes `target/` (`cargo clean gc` and `-Z gc` refused on 1.93.0, 1.98.1 and 1.99.0; `cargo clean --dry-run`, `-p`, `--profile` are the only selective forms); a cold rebuild of the CI set is 144 s (118 s with incremental off), of the whole documented set 5–6 min; cargo-sweep 0.8.0 (2025-10-11) deletes by fingerprint atime (`--time`), by installed toolchain (`--installed`) or by size (`--maxsize`) and its README has carried an "unmaintained" banner since 2026-05-26 [D; M: `fetch/gh-cargo-sweep-*.json`]; cargo-clean-all 0.6.5 removes whole targets by age/size; sccache cannot cache incrementally compiled crates or linked binaries (≈ 80 % of A's bytes) and does not shrink `target/`. *Options:* **(a)** no tool — M01's line: `cargo clean` when `du -sh target` passes a threshold the owner picks (e.g. 30 GB); **(b)** owner-installed cargo-sweep (`cargo install cargo-sweep --locked`; `cargo sweep --maxsize 30GiB`, `--time 14`, `--installed`); **(c)** cargo-clean-all across `~/dev`. **Never** a hook, cron or agent rule that deletes `target/` unasked. *Recommendation:* **(a)**; (b) only if the warm incremental state must be kept. *Under each answer:* (a) → M01 as written; (b)/(c) → M01's line names the tool and its invocation.

**DU-M4 — toolchain hygiene as a tool.** *Trigger:* (b)/(c) add a convention or a tool; (a) is instruction. *Facts [M: §1.6]:* +0.87 / +0.91 GiB per extra-toolchain `check`, **+4.86 GiB and +12,114 paths per extra-toolchain `test --no-run`**, nothing shared; the pin bump (P03) will re-key everything once (+15–24 GiB by extrapolation until a clean — inferred, not measured). *Options:* **(a)** M01's rule — no toolchain but the pin builds into the repository's `target/`; another toolchain is tried in a worktree with its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (what every group did this week) — plus **one `cargo clean` right after P03 moves the pin** (an action attached to P03, §6.1); **(b)** the convention `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/<toolchain>` for any `cargo +<other>` (same bytes, separable and deletable); **(c)** `cargo sweep --installed` after each toolchain experiment (needs DU-M3 (b)). *Recommendation:* **(a)** plus the one-time clean. *Under each answer:* (b) → M01's line changes wording; (c) → with DU-M3 (b). *Rev 2.4:* under D3 (a) the pin moves twice — 1.99.0 at P03, 1.99.1 at P03b. The owner's (a) is one clean, right after P03; a second, right after P03b, is **recommended** on (a)'s own reasoning (M01's line (2): a second toolchain's tower in `target/`, here 1.99.0's left beside 1.99.1's) and **presented to the owner, not adopted**: the owner approved one clean, and CLAUDE.md's build hygiene lets no agent rule delete `target/` unasked. On a yes it runs as M01's action says; without one, P03b's `Verified:` line records `du -sh target` and CLAUDE.md's threshold clean stands.

**DU-M5 — `build.build-dir`.** *Trigger:* a repository-wide build-layout change. *Facts [M: §1.8]:* stable on 1.93.0 and 1.99.0 (no warning; `target/` keeps `CACHEDIR.TAG` and the lock; test executables land in `<build-dir>/debug/deps/`; final artifacts stay in `target/<profile>/`, so `prepare-sidecar.ts:42-43` keeps working); stabilised in Cargo 1.91; Cargo 1.100 (2026-11-12) changes the build-dir's internal layout ("not a stable interface"); it deletes nothing and shares nothing between feature sets; a fixed shared dir deduplicates across worktrees of this repository, whose use is [NE]. *Options:* **(a)** nothing; **(b)** `.cargo/config.toml` → `[build] build-dir = "{cargo-cache-home}/build/{workspace-path-hash}"` (the growth moves to `~/.cargo/build/<hash>/`, out of sight of `du target`, not gone); **(c)** a fixed shared build-dir for all worktrees. *Recommendation:* **(a)** today; reconsider after 1.100 has shipped and if worktrees become routine. *Under each answer:* (b)/(c) → a one-line `.cargo/config.toml` commit, Q02 (which reads cargo's JSON) unaffected, anything reading `target/debug/deps` by hand affected (nothing in `scripts/` does [C: grep]).

**DU-M5, rev 2.0: (b).** `build.build-dir` has been stable since Cargo 1.91; "today (a); reconsider after 1.100" was a deferral. The real blocker the plan did not see: tauri-build finds its target dir by walking `OUT_DIR` up to `build` and copies the staged sidecar there [D: `tauri-build-2.7.2/src/lib.rs:292-301`, its TODO citing cargo#5457], and `tauri dev`/`tauri build` run cargo from `src-tauri/`, which would inherit a root build-dir — the dev app would not find its daemon. Way out: `apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/.cargo/config.toml` sets `build-dir` equal to its `target-dir` (`../../../target/gui`), and a root `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` adds `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=target/gui`. `Swatinem/rust-cache` handles `target/` only [D: rust-cache `6323deb1` `src/config.ts:142-150`, `:274-276` — answers Appendix B #21], so CI names the build-dir in `cache-directories`. M63. **Rev 2.2 (MF-5) — what the answer also catches.** With a `build-dir` configured, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` moves only the final artifacts; the intermediates still go to `{cargo-cache-home}/build/{workspace-path-hash}`, one `~/.cargo/build/<hash>/` per workspace path, which deleting a scratch export never removes — and `git archive` carries the root config into every export. So M63 pairs `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` with every `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` the programme's recipes set and checks that `$CARGO_HOME/build` gains nothing; V4 measures the target-dir and the build-dir apart. The answer, (b), is unchanged.

**DU-M6 — the profile levers.** *Trigger:* `Cargo.toml` profile changes bind everyone and trade debuggability. *Facts [M: §1.7]:* `split-debuginfo = "packed"` −28 % paths, +6 % bytes, the `at file:line` backtrace frames kept, a `dsymutil` per link; `"off"` −29 % paths, 0 % bytes, **the frames lost** (measured on the toy with `RUST_BACKTRACE=1`) — which is what `Cargo.toml:155-157`'s comment keeps `debug = "line-tables-only"` for; `codegen-units = 16` −53 % paths, −1 % bytes, per-edit path inflation +512 → +32…73 on storage, rebuild ≤ 1 s on storage, `openalpaca_core` [NE]; `debug = 0` −10 % bytes, no line tables; `strip = "debuginfo"` −0.4 %. *Options:* **(a)** nothing; **(b)** `"packed"`; **(c)** `"off"` — rejected unless the owner gives up readable backtraces; **(d)** `codegen-units = 16`; **(e)** `debug = 0`. *Recommendation:* **(a)** — the byte lever is DU-M2; (d) is the one cheap path lever and stays the owner's call because its effect on the largest crate is unmeasured. *Under each answer:* a one- or two-line `[profile.dev]` commit; (d) measured on `openalpaca_core` first (one edit-rebuild timed each way in a worktree).

### 5.2 Deprecated usage and tooling — DU-D1…D5

**DU-D1 — ESLint in the repository and a `bun run lint` gate.** *Trigger:* a dependency set and a gate. *Facts [M: `depr-eslint-*`]:* eslint 10.12.0 + typescript-eslint 8.71.1 + eslint-plugin-react-hooks 7.1.1 with a flat config of `@typescript-eslint/no-deprecated` (type-aware, `projectService`) + `recommended-latest` (`rules-of-hooks`, `exhaustive-deps`, and the Compiler-era rules `static-components`, `use-memo`, `void-use-memo`, `preserve-manual-memoization`, `incompatible-library`, `immutability`, `globals`, `refs`, `set-state-in-effect`, `error-boundaries`, `purity`, `set-state-in-render`, `unsupported-syntax`, `config`, `gating`): **350 files, 17 messages** — `no-deprecated` 0, `rules-of-hooks` 0, `refs` 11, `set-state-in-effect` 3, `exhaustive-deps` 3 (the sites of §4.1 row 5). typescript-eslint **refuses TS 7.0.2** ("does not support TS 7.0"; issue 10940 tracks ≥ 7.1), so the run used `typescript@6.0.3` installed in the worktree only. *Options:* **(a)** no; **(b)** one-off worktree runs at each wave, not committed; **(c)** commit it — needs a second `typescript@6` devDependency for the linter alone, or waiting for typescript-eslint's TS 7 support. *Recommendation:* **(b)** until typescript-eslint supports TS 7.x, then revisit (c); decide together with DU-D2 and DU-R3 as **one warnings-gate decision** across both languages. *Under each answer:* (c) → the lint becomes a gate and M43 (scheduled regardless) keeps it clean, `package.json` gains the devDependencies and a `lint` script, `ci.yml`'s `gui` job a fifth step; (b) → the §2.2 run is repeated by hand at each wave; (a) → nothing.

**DU-D1, rev 2.0: (c′) oxlint + oxlint-tsgolint.** The one-off run on `typescript@6.0.3` beside the shipped TS 7 was the older form beside the new one, and its blocker (typescript-eslint on TS 7, issue 10940) has a TS-7-native way around it: oxlint-tsgolint is "built directly on TypeScript 7" and stable, and implements `no-deprecated` [Docs: tsgolint README; oxc type-aware guide "TypeScript 7.0+ is required"]; oxlint carries `react/rules-of-hooks` and `react/exhaustive-deps` [D: oxc `crates/oxc_linter/src/rules/react/`]. The Compiler-era rules need oxlint's JS plugins, "currently in alpha" — the named blocker; M69's skip check, with the compiler's `validate*` flags on, covers them meanwhile. No ESLint, no second `typescript@6`. M66.

**DU-D1, rev 2.3 — ZCode's evidence, the answer unchanged.** The audit's answer was unverified (†, its §5.4); read here: ZCode's root `.oxlintrc.json` keeps oxlint's default categories (`"plugins": null`, `"categories": {}`) and adds one rule, `max-lines` (ZCode:.oxlintrc.json:3-7), with no type-aware linting (neither manifest names `oxlint-tsgolint`), and its root and CLI manifests declare different oxlint ranges (`^1.57.0`, ZCode:package.json:99; `^1.62.0`, ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/package.json:47). M66 stands as answered, with one oxlint version in the repository, named in one manifest.

**DU-D2 — knip as a fifth bun gate.** *Trigger:* a gate. *Facts [M: `web-18-knip-full.log`; `depr-knip.json`]:* knip 6.41.0 without a config: 6 unused files (M10), 1 unlisted binary (`rustc` spawned by `prepare-sidecar.ts`), 47 unused exports, 35 unused exported types (the deprecated-inventory run, from another cwd, read 0 files and 78 exports/types — §0); `tsc`'s `noUnusedLocals` catches unused locals, never unused exports or files. *Options:* **(a)** none; **(b)** knip in CI with a `knip.json` after a one-time triage of the 47 + 35 exports (some may be kept deliberately as a module's public shape). *Recommendation:* triage once by hand (M10 now); decide the gate with DU-D1 — two JS gates in one go or none. *Under each answer:* (b) → a `knip.json`, a `knip` devDependency, a CI step, and the triage's deletions as one hygiene commit.

**DU-D2, rev 2.0: (b).** knip 6.41.0 needs no TypeScript peer and already runs clean of files after M10; the gate waited only on DU-D1's blocked half. M67 triages the 47 + 35 exports and adds the CI step.

**DU-D2, rev 2.3 — ZCode's evidence, the answer unchanged.** Verified here (the audit's answer was †): ZCode runs knip as a script (`"knip": "knip"`, ZCode:package.json:61) — whether its CI runs it is not established, the export carrying no CI configuration — and marks a deliberate export with a JSDoc `@lintignore` tag, which `knip.json`'s `"tags": ["-lintignore"]` excludes (ZCode:knip.json:3-5), the reason written in the same comment (ZCode:packages/ui/src/hooks/useTaskSessionFilePath.ts:5-8). M67 takes the inline tag (the audit's A-38, Z7-16); the gate is not reopened.

**DU-D3 — a declared Python floor for `scripts/*.py`.** *Trigger:* a policy (a required interpreter). *Facts [M: `depr-python.log`]:* `/usr/bin/python3` = 3.9.6 is what `python3` resolves to (`which -a` finds only it; `~/.local/bin/python3.13` and `python3.14` exist off that name); `scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check`, `scripts/tests/test_gen_api_docs.py` (17/17) and `scripts/eval/memory/compare.py -h` run clean on 3.9.6 and on 3.14.4 with `-I -X dev -W error::DeprecationWarning -W error::PendingDeprecationWarning`; the first two carry `from __future__ import annotations`; no 3.10+-only idiom by grep; CI runs no Python step; 3.9's end-of-life (2025-10) is general knowledge, not re-fetched [NE]. *Options:* **(a)** none; **(b)** document "3.10+" and drop the two `__future__` imports when 3.9 is gone; **(c)** `requires-python` in a `pyproject.toml`. *Recommendation:* **(a)**, with one sentence in `scripts/tests/` or the manual naming the two interpreters verified. *Under each answer:* (b)/(c) → a docs or a one-file commit.

**DU-D3, rev 2.3 — a sub-question, pending (the ZCode audit's A-36, which follows its A-12).** If the owner takes the audit's `tooling` CI job (A-12; B-23 in `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.3, rev 5, pending) — `gen_api_docs.py --check`, its tests and the gate tools' tests on ubuntu, no network — CI runs Python for the first time, and the interpreter it pins is then the declared floor: (a)'s sentence names that version and `ci.yml` pins it. ZCode's evidence: its runtime pins sit in one file, `mise.toml` (node 24.14.0, pnpm 10.33.2; ZCode:mise.toml:1-3), while `.nvmrc` floats at `24` (ZCode:.nvmrc:1); that its CI checks the pins is doc-only (the audit's §1: no CI configuration in the export). *Recommendation:* under A-12, yes — CI's pinned interpreter is the floor; without A-12, (a) as answered. Not adopted; nothing lands before the owner answers A-12.

**DU-D4 — `erasableSyntaxOnly: true`.** *Trigger:* a gate tightening. *Facts [M]:* 0 `enum`, 0 `namespace`, 0 constructor parameter properties in app code; three parameter properties in test files, which `tsconfig.json` includes. *Options:* **(a)** no; **(b)** yes, with the three test sites rewritten in the same commit. *Recommendation:* **(a)** — no need shown. *Under (b):* a one-line tsconfig change plus three test edits; `bun run check` the gate.

**DU-D4, rev 2.0: (b).** "No need shown" is "not now" for a stable TypeScript option the tree already satisfies outside three test parameter properties. M68 (with `DOM.Iterable` dropped and the superseded array idioms).

**DU-D5 — dissolved in rev 1.1 (kept so the id does not dangle).** Rev 1 asked the owner to confirm P36 widened to the 66 mechanical items and the 17 let-chain sites, "because it restates plan-rev3's DU-17". It is work, not a decision: the widening adds no gate, widens nothing, reverses no taken row (DU-17 is pending — "carried forward" — not taken) and changes no behaviour, and the owner's scope statement already made lint-site fixes work; DU-17's gate half is **DU-R3**. M07 therefore lands as **Recommended** with the scope rev 1 recommended as (b) — the 66 mechanical items, the 17 let-chain sites, the 10 allows with a reason each, the 8 remaining judgement items decided with a sentence — and its entry in §6.1 records the narrower scope (P36's +23 alone, gate 101 → 78 / 100 → 77) and the wider one (the 8 judgement items fixed too, gate → 10) as what the committer may do instead, with a sentence why. The facts stand [M: §3.3]: 101 triples on 1.99.0 = 66 mechanical (17 new in 1.94–1.99) + 25 judgement (17 of them `collapsible_if` = let chains) + 10 allow-with-reason (6 new); P36 as written is 17 one-liners + 6 allows landing at 78, the 1.93 baseline; a blanket `clippy --fix` broke the build in September [M-sep per plan-rev3]. The one deprecation (M04) is Required under ask (2) regardless.

### 5.3 Rust adoptions — DU-R1…R6 (DU-R6 since rev 2.3)

**DU-R1 — `dynosaur` for the 14 `dyn`-dispatched async traits.** *Trigger:* widening — a new proc-macro dependency and a `DynX` type at ≈ 89 `dyn` sites. *Facts [M: §3.2]:* `BuiltInTool` (66 impls), `LlmProvider` (57), `MessageHandler` (22), `PluginSkillExecutor` (9), `PluginToolExecutor` (7), `PluginAgentExecutor` (6), `ConnectorSendProvider` (6), `ContextSource` (5), `Summarizer` (4), `Transport` (3), `PersonaHandler` (3), `ExtensionSupervisor` (2), `EventWatcher` (1), `ToolCallbackExecutor` (1) are all used as `dyn Trait`; native AFIT traits are not dyn-compatible (E0038); `async-trait` 0.1.92 is current and not deprecated; hand-written `Pin<Box<dyn Future + Send>>` methods are what the macro already generates. *Options:* **(a)** keep `async-trait` for the dyn traits; **(b)** `dynosaur` for all 14; **(c)** for the four small ones (`Transport`, `PersonaHandler`, `EventWatcher`, `ExtensionSupervisor`) only. *Recommendation:* **(a)** — nothing is deprecated and (b)/(c) widen fourteen trait shapes for no behaviour gain; the `Connector` conversion (M13) is work and needs no decision. *Under (b)/(c):* a dependency commit plus one commit per trait, each gated by the crate's tests and clippy 0 new.

**DU-R1, rev 2.0: (b), with enums for the closed sets.** "Nothing is deprecated" does not answer the owner's rule: the boxed proc macro on 206 of 211 attributes beside native AFIT on `Connector` is an older form beside the new one, and the language's blocker (no `dyn`-compatible async method through 1.99.0) has a named way out, dynosaur 0.3.1. The ten open-set traits take dynosaur (M58 (a–j)); `Transport`, `PersonaHandler`, `EventWatcher` and `ExtensionSupervisor` take enums first (M55 (a–d)) so they never need it; `async-trait` leaves every manifest (M59). The widening (a proc-macro dependency, a `DynX` type at ≈ 89 `dyn` sites) is presented here; dynosaur goes when Rust stabilizes `dyn` async methods. §3.2's rev 2.0 paragraph has the source facts. **Rev 2.2 (D-12, D-09):** the size was understated. A `DynX` is built only through its constructors (`new_arc`, `from_arc`, `from_ref`, …), so every construction site changes as well as the ≈ 89 `dyn` uses, and `LlmProvider` has five default `async fn` bodies, not four (ten in all). And `async-trait` stays in the lock on Linux: zbus 5.19.0 depends on it on the prep lock, through keyring-core's Secret Service store (P12) and, after M79, opener's `reveal`. The aim is therefore that no workspace member depends on it (M59), with zbus named as the remaining parent.

**DU-R2 — `std::fs::File::{lock, try_lock}` in place of the `file-lock` crate (M17).** *Trigger:* a primitive change on the singleton lock's path (the second leg of "the process, then the lock, never the port", an accepted design) and a window a person can meet (a mixed-build store); the rule itself is unchanged. *Facts:* §3.1 row 11 — `file-lock` 2.1.11 is `fcntl` record locks in a C shim with no Windows branch [D]; std's `lock` is `flock` on Unix and `LockFileEx` on Windows [Docs: the installed 1.93 rustdoc]; the mixed-build window (an old-build daemon and a new-build CLI or daemon on one `openalpacad.lock` both "hold" it) is the "keep the builds apart" doctrine already in `docs/Installation_Manual.md` and closes once both binaries are the new build; a latent same-process unlock hazard goes away; the non-blocking refusal's text changes and no test pins it; macOS's `flock`/`fcntl` interaction [NE]; rustyline 18 made the same swap. *Options:* **(a)** take M17 after P03; **(b)** keep `file-lock` (Windows stays blocked there; the C shim stays). *Recommendation:* **(a)**, with the gates of row 11 and the commit body recording the primitive change and the window. *Under (b):* M17 is dropped and §3.1 row 11 joins plan-rev3 §3.1's holds with the trigger "a Windows build".

**DU-R3 — pedantic lints at `warn` in `[workspace.lints]`, or a warnings gate.** *Trigger:* enforcement (= DU-17's gate half). *Facts [M: `r01-clippy-pedantic-199.count.txt`]:* pedantic fires 5,046 times (noise: `uninlined_format_args` 645, `doc_markdown` 610, `must_use_candidate` 602, `missing_errors_doc` 562, …); nine lints carry signal — `unused_async` 10 (`commands/config.rs:93`, `commands/gui.rs:19`, `hot_reload.rs:352`, `:588`, `telegram/connector.rs:70`, `settings_service.rs:753`, `credential_discovery/mod.rs:271`, `:310`, `orchestrator/bootstrap.rs:28`, `wake/scheduler/mod.rs:131`), `large_futures` 3 (`lead_agent/tools.rs:736`, `plugin_agent.rs:470`, `:576`), `option_option` 18 (`routes/memories.rs:225-268`, `:1114-1115`, `memory_tools.rs:139`, `:767-789` — the "absent vs null" wire shape: allow per site, the API is frozen), `unchecked_time_subtraction` 1, `duration_suboptimal_units` 4, `ref_option` 9, `match_wild_err_arm` 1, `manual_let_else` 55 (M08), `map_unwrap_or` 213 (M06's gate); **never `case_sensitive_file_extension_comparisons`** (12: the production sites are file-kind routing whose extension sets R21 and `store::kind_for_file_name` pin by test, `dirstat.rs:30` inside the memory-index boundary); 1.97's `build.warnings` is the config-file form of `-D warnings`. *Options:* **(a)** none; **(b)** the five small-count lints at `warn` (`unused_async`, `large_futures`, `unchecked_time_subtraction`, `duration_suboptimal_units`, `match_wild_err_arm`); **(c)** also `manual_let_else`, `ref_option`, `option_option`, `map_unwrap_or` with per-site allows; **(d)** a warnings gate (`build.warnings = "deny"` or `-D warnings` in CI) once M07 lands. *Recommendation:* **(a)** per DU-17 (a); (b) is the smallest useful gate if DU-17 reopens; fixing a lint's sites without the gate is ordinary work (M06, M08 do). *Under (b)/(c)/(d):* a `[workspace.lints.clippy]` table or a `.cargo/config.toml` line, and the sites fixed or allowed first so the gate is green when it lands.

**DU-R3, rev 2.0: (b) + (d).** Cargo's lint table (1.74) and the stabilized `build.warnings` (1.97, "replacing `-Dwarnings`" [Docs: RELEASES.md 1.97.0]) are the new forms of the ad-hoc "clippy 78, the baseline". M65 puts the signal lints at `warn` in `[workspace.lints]` with their sites fixed (`unused_async`, `large_futures`, `unchecked_time_subtraction`, `duration_suboptimal_units`, `match_wild_err_arm`, `ref_option`, `manual_let_else`, `manual_is_variant_and`; never `case_sensitive_file_extension_comparisons`, never `option_option` — the frozen wire shape), sets `CARGO_BUILD_WARNINGS=deny` in CI and adds `cargo fmt --all -- --check` after M64's one formatting pass. An enforcement point, presented here; CLAUDE.md's baseline line becomes "0".

**DU-R3, rev 2.3 — a sub-question, pending: a ratchet on lint suppressions (the ZCode audit's A-37, Z7-10).** M39 turns all 135 `#[allow]`s into `#[expect(…, reason = "…")]`, but nothing in M65's table keeps a new bare `#[allow]` out, so M39's state can regress one attribute at a time. *Options:* **(a)** no change — M65 as answered; **(b)** add `clippy::allow_attributes` and `clippy::allow_attributes_without_reason` at `warn` to M65's `[workspace.lints.clippy]`, denied in CI through `CARGO_BUILD_WARNINGS` like every other warning — a widening of the taken (b) + (d), hence presented. The `cfg_attr(<where it fires>, expect(…))` form for a lint that fires on one platform or selection only is already M39's (its rev 2.0 and rev 2.2 bullets); running the gate on macOS as well as ubuntu is plan-rev3's A-23 (a `rust-macos` job, DU-31 under DU-33, pending), not this sub-question. ZCode's evidence (the audit's answer was †; read here): its architecture checker counts every inline `oxlint-disable`/`eslint-disable` line as a violation (ZCode:scripts/architecture/index.mjs:165-178), an exception needs an entry with a reason in the policy file (ZCode:architecture-policy.yaml:187-195), and the baseline moves only through an explicit script (`architecture:baseline:update`, ZCode:package.json:75); that its CI runs the check is doc-only. *Recommendation:* **(b)**, landing in M65 if answered before M65, else as a one-line follow-up to it; whether `allow_attributes_without_reason` also reads `#[expect]` at the pinned toolchain is checked in that commit [NE]. Not adopted.

**DU-R4 — rmcp 3.5's opt-in surfaces, after P19.** *Trigger:* per item — (i) `PeerRequestOptions.reset_timeout_on_progress` (+ `max_total_timeout`) **changes the timeout contract** (user-visible: a long call that reports progress no longer stops at `request_timeout`; it defaults to `false`, so P19 alone changes no timeout [D: `rmcp-3.5.0/src/service.rs:772-779`]); (ii) answering `ClientHandler::create_elicitation` (default `Decline`, `handler/client.rs:179`) is **a new approval surface** through the `ConfirmationBroker` — the T35 family; (iii) tasks (SEP-2663; `client.rs:548` sets `task: None`) have **no surface** in the loop or the Work pane; (iv) `ClientLifecycleMode::{Discover, Auto}` is **a handshake wire change** (`serve_with_conn` uses `Initialize`). *Recommendation:* (i) defer; (ii) keep `Decline` and decide with T35; (iii) defer; (iv) keep `Initialize`. *Also for P19's body, a check, not a decision:* 3.5's `Peer::call_tool` is `call_tool_with_mrtr_max_rounds` (`service/client.rs:1966-1980`) and drives a server's `input_required` answers through the local input handler (`InputRequiredRoundsExceeded`) — how that surfaces with elicitation declined is [NE] and is read before P19 lands (Appendix B); and the client response cache (`ClientCacheConfig::default()`: `enabled: true`, `max_entries: 512`, **`serve_stale_on_error: true`**) is on by default per `Peer` (DU-5/DU-25 decided "the response cache (default)"; the one semantic to record is that a stale cached response is served when a request errors).

**(v) protocol-level cancellation — M15's call path.** *Trigger:* a wire-behaviour change: the daemon starts emitting `notifications/cancelled` to MCP servers (new outbound protocol messages), and the path `call_tool` takes decides how a server's `input_required` answer surfaces. *Facts [D: `rmcp-3.5.0/src/service/client.rs:1379-1400`, `:1966-2004`, `:2098-2130`, `model/mrtr.rs:53`; C: `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:558`, `:585-593`]:* 3.5's `Peer::call_tool` is `call_tool_with_mrtr_max_rounds(params, DEFAULT_MRTR_MAX_ROUNDS = 10)`: a loop over `call_tool_once` — a plain `send_request`, no cancel handle, no per-call timeout of its own — that answers each `InputRequiredResult` through the local handler (`prepare_input_required_retry`, private: an `InputRequest::Elicitation` goes to `create_elicitation`, whose default `Decline` is serialized and **sent back to the server as the input response**; `CreateMessage` and `ListRoots` likewise) and gives up after 10 rounds with `InputRequiredRoundsExceeded`; today our `call_tool` wraps that in `tokio::time::timeout` and, on our `CancellationToken`, drops the future and tells the server nothing (the "later refinement" comment). `send_cancellable_request(ClientRequest::CallToolRequest(..), PeerRequestOptions::with_timeout(..))` returns a `RequestHandle` (`await_response` / `cancel(reason)`), carries rmcp's own timeout — which sends `notifications/cancelled` ("request timeout") by itself — and answers no `input_required` round: its `ServerResult` may be an `InputRequiredResult`. *Options:* **(a)** the raw path — `send_cancellable_request`, `cancel(reason)` when our token fires, an `InputRequiredResult` reported to the model as a tool error naming that the server asked for input this client does not provide (no Decline round trip; one request, cancellable end to end); **(b)** the raw path plus a re-implementation of the MRTR loop on top of it (the Decline fed back, ≤ 10 rounds, each round cancellable) — ≈ 60 more lines copying a private helper; **(c)** keep the wrapper and no protocol cancellation (the local bail-out stays). *Recommendation:* **(a)** — elicitation stays `Decline` under (ii), so the only behaviour (b) preserves over (a) is a server that finishes after being told "Decline", which P19's real-server round trip will show or not; the error text is the one model-visible consequence and is written in M15. *Under each answer:* (a) or (b) → M15 after P19 as written or with the loop; (c) → M15 dropped and the comment at `client.rs:585-593` stays. What remains of Appendix B #6 is the server's side: how a server reacts to a Decline [NE].

**(vi) `Tool.output_schema`** (`rmcp-3.5.0/src/model/tool.rs:30` [D]). *Trigger:* a widening — carrying it to the model needs an output-schema slot on `ToolDefinition`, the shape every provider adapter and `strict_mode_compatible` read (§3.5 item 4). *Recommendation:* **defer** until a server in use ships one; no commit.

**DU-R4, rev 2.0 — per item.** **(i) yes**: `PeerRequestOptions { timeout: Some(request_timeout), reset_timeout_on_progress: true, max_total_timeout: Some(bound) }` on M15's request — rmcp's handle assigns the progress token itself [D: `rmcp-3.5.1/src/service.rs:772-806`] — with a new `config/mcp.toml` key `max_total_timeout_secs` beside `request_timeout_secs` (`config/mcp.toml:11`), proposed default 300 (10 × 30), and `on_progress` logged at debug (no new event variant); a user-visible timeout-contract change, presented here. **(ii) an owner question with T35** — answering `create_elicitation` through the `ConfirmationBroker` (escaped text, a person's Approve, an unattended turn refused at once) and advertising the capability (a wire change) is a new approval surface; recommended yes, M84. **(iii) an owner question** — MCP tasks need a Work-pane surface that does not exist; recommended a design first, M83. **(iv) `Auto`** — `ClientLifecycleMode::Auto { preferred_versions: [LATEST], legacy_version: Some(LATEST_WITH_INITIALIZE) }` in plan-rev3's P19 (its rev 3.9, DU-25 (p4)); a legacy streamable-HTTP server answering `server/discover` with a 4xx falls back too [D: `rmcp-3.5.1/src/transport/streamable_http_client.rs:375-406`]; the named cost is 10 s per (re)connect for a server that ignores the request. **(v) (b)** — the raw cancellable path **with** the ≤ 10-round MRTR loop, each round cancellable (the handler's answer is `Decline` until (ii) is yes); (a) refused a protocol feature rmcp serves. **(vi) surface + validate** — `output_schema` carried on the registered tool and served by `GET /v1/tools` (M14), never in a provider tool definition (`strict_mode_compatible` stays the rule), and `structured_content` validated with `jsonschema` at the newest release eligible when M82 lands (rev 2.0 named 0.58.6, eligible since 2026-10-13T14:51Z; 0.59.0, a breaking 0.x minor, was published 2026-10-16T20:30:14Z and is eligible from 2026-10-23T20:30:14Z [M: crates.io API, 2026-10-17]; rev 2.2, SF-9). A new dependency, presented here; M82.

**DU-R4, rev 2.3 — what the ZCode audit changes (A-31, A-32, A-40; Z1-12, Z5-06, Z5-07).** **(ii)** keeps its status — an owner question with T35, `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.3's (c)-3 — but its recommended **shape** changes, so the recommendation recorded there changes with it: rev 2.0's Approve/Deny card cannot return the `content` a schema with fields asks for (`ElicitResult.content`, `rmcp-3.5.1/src/model.rs:3910-3923`), so M84 is now a form — form mode only, one typed input per primitive field, attributed exactly through M15's loop or, for a request sent mid-call, to the one call in flight, the clocks held within the broker's bound while a person answers, declined on unattended turns and connector lanes, nothing persisted (M84's entry). ZCode's elicitation is built in that shape, but keys its in-flight map by server name (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:1104, :1125, :1133), which M84 does not borrow. **(i)** keeps its answer (the progress-reset timeout, M15) and gains two fixes and one sub-question. The fixes, work: M15 hands every MCP call a cancellation token that the sandbox fires at its deadline and on a run's cancel — today the registry passes none (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/registry/mod.rs:852`) and the sandbox ends a call by dropping its future (`security/sandbox/mod.rs:542-547`), and rmcp's `RequestHandle` has no `Drop` impl (`rmcp-3.5.1/src/service.rs:542-550`), so neither a sandbox cut nor `/cancel` tells the server anything — and M15 clamps `max_total_timeout` below the call's effective `max_tool_runtime_secs` (60 s for an agent, 300 s for the lead), since the proposed 300 would otherwise reach only the lead. **The sub-question, pending (Z5-06 (c)):** may a server's progress extend the sandbox's clock, as ZCode's does — its one per-call clock (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/mcp/index.ts:149, :226-229) is reset by every progress notification (`resetTimeoutOnProgress: true`, ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:1174)? *Options:* **(a)** no — progress resets only the MCP clock, inside the sandbox's bound, and an operator who wants longer calls raises `max_tool_runtime_secs`; **(b)** yes — a changed sandbox contract. *Recommendation:* **(a)**: the per-call timeout is the bound the extension design's drain rests on, and extension tools may never be exempt from it (`tools/registry/mod.rs:570-581`, design §3.2 T3); under (b) a server's notifications would set how long a disable waits. M84's hold while a person answers an elicitation is a separate, person-driven extension, decided with (c)-3 and bounded by the broker's own timeout, not by this sub-question. Not adopted. **(iii)** unchanged — M83 stays design-first; the audit adds a reference shape (ZCode's task registry, completion notification and one read tool, ZCode:docs/task-output-contract.md) and two prerequisites, the MCP stream fix (plan-rev3's N-MCP-2, the audit's A-13) and M15.

**DU-R6 — MCP images, audio and embedded blobs kept as by-products (M85).** Raised in rev 2.3 by the ZCode audit (Z5-12 = Z1-17, its A-33); a new id, an owner question. *Trigger:* a widened component — the MCP bridge starts writing files to the artifact store, which never asks (ST-15). *Facts:* today an image becomes a placeholder sentence and audio or a resource its bracketed kind name (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/mcp/bridge.rs:84-104`), so the model cannot read, keep or `send` what a server returned; M14 covers text, structured content and resource links only. ZCode keeps an image over 200 KiB of base64 as an artifact and never decodes a third party's image (ZCode:docs/mcp-tool-output-budget.md:8-42). *Options:* **(a)** no change — the placeholders stay; **(b)** M85: in the registry's MCP arm, which holds the `ToolContext`, save image, audio and embedded-blob content as a by-product through `put_tool_artifact` within `max_artifact_bytes`, base64-decoded for storage only, and put its id and path in the text, with the plumbing that arm lacks today (no database reaches the registry, `tools/registry/mod.rs:438-449`, and `put_tool_artifact` and `scope_from_context`, which gives the turn's by-product scope, are `pub(super)` in a private module, `tools/builtins/artifact_write.rs:183`, `:61`, `tools/builtins/mod.rs:3`; M85's entry). *Recommendation:* **(b)**. Not adopted; M85 is Conditional on it. The audit's A-42 does not list it for `tasks/pending-decisions.md`; its row there is in §4.3's ZCode table (rev 5), with the three sub-questions above.

**DU-R5 — tower-http 0.7's `CsrfLayer` and `RequestBodyDeadlineLayer` on the daemon's router.** *Trigger:* enforcement — request-rejecting layers. *Facts [C; D: `tower-http-0.7.1/CHANGELOG.md`]:* the daemon binds loopback (`main.rs:210`) and authenticates every `/v1/*` call with a bearer token; `CorsLayer::permissive()` (`router.rs:480`) and `DefaultBodyLimit::max(100 MiB)` (`:153`) are the two sites the new layers map to; 0.7's breaking change (compression wildcard handling) touches nothing used. *Options:* **(a)** no change; **(b)** add either or both. *Recommendation:* **(a)**. *Under (b):* a router commit after P25 with route tests for the rejection paths and a CORS pin (P24) re-run.

**DU-R5, rev 2.0: (a), unchanged — not a compromise.** Neither layer replaces an older form; the daemon authenticates with a bearer token on loopback, so the `Sec-Fetch-Site` scheme defends nothing, and each layer is a new refusal.

### 5.4 Web adoptions — DU-W1…W7

**DU-W1 — the React Compiler.** *Trigger:* widens the build (a compiler pass on every component) and changes bundle bytes. *Facts:* §4.2 — healthcheck 295/295; the oxc host skips 13 locations (15 diagnostics); JS **742,230 → 877,608 B (+135,378 B, +18.2 %, oxc) / 832,166 B (+89,936 B, +12.1 %, Babel)** against today's Vite-8-alone export (rev 1's "775,895 → 877,608, +13 %" used yesterday's P31 + P32 figure as the base); build +0.1–0.3 s (oxc) / +2.4 s (Babel); test wall +3–4 s (oxc), two `findByRole` budgets broken under Babel until `asyncUtilTimeout` ≥ 2 s or `apply: 'build'`; benefit for this app [NE]. *Options:* **(a)** none; **(b)** after P32/P33 the documented Babel host (`@rolldown/plugin-babel` + `reactCompilerPreset()`, `babel-plugin-react-compiler` 1.0.0) with M43 first; **(c)** the native oxc host (`compiler: { reportDiagnostics: true }`, experimental per the plugin's changelog) — cheapest, and it reports skips. *Recommendation:* **(a) now**; revisit after the wave with M43 done and a render-performance question to answer (plan-rev3 G4's protocol is where that question gets a number). *Under (b)/(c):* a config commit after P33 (M43 is Recommended regardless and lands first); the test gate's budget adjusted; dist bytes recorded against P32's 742,230 B.

**DU-W1, rev 2.0: (b) — the Babel host, Babel 7 held.** React Compiler 1.0 is stable with a documented Vite 8 host; "no measured benefit" was a "not now". M69 runs it in every build and test run, `panicThreshold: "none"`, with a CI skip check whose `validate*` flags are on. **The held major, named:** `@babel/core` 7.29.7 — under Babel 8 (8.0.7, a required peer's highest version) compiler 1.0.0 silently skips components with destructured parameter defaults (react#37406; #36868 errors) and ≈ 53 such components and hooks exist here; way out: a compiler release with the fixes (#36956/#37492/#37558) ≥ 7 days old, or the oxc host once it leaves "experimental". §4.2's Babel figures are re-measured in M69's gate.

**DU-W2 — `<Activity>` for the five-view swap.** *Trigger:* reverses accepted rows G1 (cards seeded only from a read made since mount), P2 (run-report and written-file cards die with the view, by decision, the manual says so) and F6 (the session-change reset); needs 19.3's `useSyncExternalStore` fix (P31). *Options:* keep `lazy` + unmount (shipped); Activity after 19.3 with G1, P2 and F6 re-decided. *Recommendation:* **no** — the benefit (kept scroll positions and drafts across view swaps, warmer switches) is unmeasured and three decided rows would have to be reopened.

**DU-W2, rev 2.0: an owner question, recommended yes.** The refusal rested on three decided rows worded around the view *unmounting*. A form keeps their behaviour: G1 — a reveal stamp captured before seeding, so a pre-hide snapshot never seeds a card, and the daemon's-word recorder on an always-mounted listener; P2 — run-report and written-file cards cleared on hide; F6 untouched; open dialogs closed on hide; one `ViewBoundary` per view. But it reads G1's "since mount" as "since shown", and scroll position and DOM survive a swap — a visible change. The owner confirms the reading; M70 is Conditional on it.

**DU-W3 — View Transitions (`<ViewTransition>`, `addTransitionType`) on view and aside swaps.** *Trigger:* user-visible behaviour (animation) and webview support [NE]. *Recommendation:* **no** (cosmetic; WKWebView/WebView2/webkit2gtk support of the View Transition API at this app's floor not established). *Under yes:* a ≈ 10-line commit after P31 with E5's packaged check as its gate.

**DU-W3, rev 2.0: no — the reason corrected.** DESIGN_SPEC §1.7 (`DESIGN_SPEC.md:273`): "no motion beyond oaP is part of the design" — a settled design row, not a webview question (React degrades without the API). Motion would be a DESIGN_SPEC revision first.

**DU-W4 — `useSuspenseQuery`.** *Trigger:* reverses owner decision D-G (the first-run card's derived three-state reading, `views/chat/first-run.ts:26-39`; no `initialData`, so no flash). *Recommendation:* **no**.

**DU-W4, rev 2.0: no — the reason corrected.** `useQuery` + `isLoading` is TanStack v5's primary form, not an older one; and the status poll (`hooks/useConnection.ts:93-99`) is `enabled: !stopped` — D-E's stop intent — which `useSuspenseQuery`, forcing `enabled: true` [D: `@tanstack/react-query@5.102.8` `useSuspenseQuery.js`], cannot express; D-G reads that poll. The one-shot detail readers fail the Suspense criterion too (a live search, a transcript row with a known-from-server short-circuit, a listing) [C].

**DU-W5 — Tailwind 4.3's scrollbar utilities in place of `.sc`.** *Trigger:* a visual change (no px width, no thumb radius) and webview support [NE]. *Recommendation:* **no** until E5's packaged WKWebView check can compare them side by side. *Under yes:* `styles.css:206-215` removed, 15 `className="sc …"` sites rewritten, E5 the gate.

**DU-W5, rev 2.0: keep `.sc` — the reason corrected.** DESIGN_SPEC §1.8 prescribes a 9 px `::-webkit-scrollbar` with a 9 px-radius thumb, which `scrollbar-width`/`scrollbar-color` cannot express; only `.sc`'s CSS form moves to v4's `@utility` (M09).

**DU-W6 — `bunfig.toml` with `[install] minimumReleaseAge = 604800` (+ `minimumReleaseAgeExcludes = []`).** *Trigger:* an enforcement point — it binds the developer's own `bun add` and `bun update` too. *Facts [Docs: bun.com/docs/runtime/bunfig; bun.com/blog/bun-v1.3; M: no `bunfig.toml` exists]:* a **Bun 1.3** feature (release post of 2025-10-10: "In Bun 1.3, you can protect yourself against supply chain attacks by requiring packages to be published for a minimum time before installation"; the bunfig page names no version; fetched today to `SP/logs/fetch/rev11/`), so the owner's own Bun 1.3.13 honours the key [Docs] — rev 1 and the web report labelled it Bun 1.4; the setting makes `bun add x@latest` land on the newest version ≥ 7 days old and refuse a 2-day-old one — plan-rev3 DU-23's 7-day eligibility rule as a tool; Cargo's equivalent (`registry.global-min-publish-age`) arrives with 1.100 (plan-rev3 §9). *Options:* **(a)** none (the 7-day rule stays a review rule); **(b)** the three-line file (M44), with P01/P31; **(c)** a CI-only check (a workflow step, not `bunfig.toml`) if the owner wants their local `bun add` unconstrained. *Recommendation:* **(b)**. *Under (c):* M44 becomes a `ci.yml` step.

**DU-W7 — Trusted Types (DOMPurify `RETURN_TRUSTED_TYPE` + React 19.3's pass-through + CSP `require-trusted-types-for 'script'`).** *Trigger:* a CSP enforcement point on the two `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` sites; WKWebView's enforcement [NE]. *Recommendation:* **no** until the webview floor is known; recorded as the one DOMPurify/React 19.3 pairing worth a later look. *Under yes:* ≈ 6 lines after P31 (`DocumentPreview.tsx:21`, `MediaPreview.tsx:200`, `tauri.conf.json:24`), E5 and E5b the gates.

**DU-W7, rev 2.0: yes.** The floor is not the blocker: WebKit enforces Trusted Types from Safari 26 and older WebKit ignores the unknown directive [Docs: webkit.org/blog/17333; MDN BCD]; DOMPurify's `RETURN_TRUSTED_TYPE` and React 19.3's pass-through are in place after P31. The residual [NE] — whether Tauri's injected scripts reach a sink — is G-bundle's (plan-rev3 §5.2) to decide. M71, after the CSP commits M72 and M73.

**Owner question (c)-5 — window chrome** (DESIGN_SPEC §3.1; `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.3; recorded here in rev 2.2, C-19/E-13 — rev 2.1 wrote the shell commits under one answer without saying so). *Options:* **(a)** keep today's decorated window; **(b)** the overlay title bar. *Recommendation:* **(a)** — no change. Open; nothing that depends on it lands before the answer. **M74, M76 and M78 are written under (a):** M74's capability grants `core:window:allow-is-decorated` and no window-management permission, M76 builds the window from today's configuration, M78 keeps its decorations. Under (b) a follow-up commit adds the overlay title bar and the window permissions it needs (dragging and the window controls), which M74 does not list; until Q07 lands, a browser host draws those controls inert.

## 6. The commit plan

**Conventions.** plan-rev3 §5.2's conventions stand for every commit here: conventional prefixes; a `Verified:` line naming the gate run; the attribution lines; one concern per commit; docs that cite a changed line in the commit that changes it; `CARGO_TERM_COLOR=never`, output to files, orphaned `sleep 3600` children killed after any test run that spawns the daemon's tests; every test that writes, edits, lists or runs a command does so in a temporary project under its crate's home-store guard (CLAUDE.md *Gotchas*); no test reads `~/.openalpaca`. Part III adds three: **never a blanket `cargo clippy --fix`** (P36's rule — it broke the build in September); **a `Display` string is behaviour** — every `thiserror` commit writes a golden test of each variant's text against the manual `impl` *before* the derive and keeps it; and **Part III's source commits land after P38** (Part I's docs commit), because G-lint's triple counts, the fmt hunk counts and P31–P33's CSS-hash and dist-byte anchors are all measured on the source as it is — the exceptions are M04 and M07, which *are* P36, and the three target-directory commits, which touch no source — and, since rev 2.0, M63 (no source; §6.3 places it right after Q11). Rev 2.0 also excepted the shell commits M72–M75 ("they may land after plan-rev3's P31a and Q11") and M79 (after P27a) — **superseded in rev 2.2** (SF-16): §6.3 gives each commit one place, and these sit after P38, in its step 12. Rev 2.0's reason stays as the constraint: no Part I anchor reads the shell's source or `tauri.conf.json`, so an earlier place would break no anchor; none is taken. **Rev 2.0 adds G-bundle** (plan-rev3 §5.2, its rev 3.9) to every shell and CSP commit's gate. Status words as in plan-rev3: **Required** (lands under every answer), **Recommended**, **Optional**, **Conditional** (waits for a DU, the recommended answer assumed in the sequencing), **DU-bound** (lands only under the named answer).

**Rev 2.2 — conventions the 2026-10-17 review added** (`tasks/deps-upgrade/reports/2026-10-17-plan-review.md`):
- **One place per commit.** §6.3 gives every commit one place; an entry's "after X" line is its constraint, not a second place (SF-16).
- **The pinned toolchain** (SF-8; the review's D3, taken 2026-10-17 as (a)). Every gate runs `cargo` under the toolchain `rust-toolchain.toml` pins from P03 on — **1.99.0 from P03, then 1.99.1 from P03b** (`build: Rust 1.99.1`, plan-rev3's one-line commit through P03's gates). DU-23 governs a toolchain as it governs a crate, so 1.99.1 moves in once it is seven days old, and **a toolchain's clock is the later of its publication times** — for Rust, the stable channel manifest on `static.rust-lang.org` and the GitHub release: for 1.99.1, 2026-10-15T17:39:23Z (the manifest's `last-modified`, re-read 2026-10-17T07:52Z) and 2026-10-16T04:47:44Z (the GitHub release: the compatibility report's §2.1 and plan-rev3 §2.2, not re-read here), so P03b may land from **2026-10-23T04:47:44Z** (21:47 PDT on 2026-10-22), after the cut; DU-2's "take an x.y.1 that appears before the cut" yields to that rule. A `cargo +1.99.0` in a Part III entry or gate means the pinned toolchain — 1.99.0, then 1.99.1 from P03b — as plan-rev3 §5.3's preamble (4) reads its own; the literal is not rewritten, and run literally after P03b it would be a second toolchain (M01's line (2)). The `+<toolchain>` spellings in §1 and §2 are dated measurements and stay; where §2.4 names one as a gate (M04's and V2's two clippy runs), it reads as the pinned toolchain too.
- **Build-dir beside target-dir** (MF-5). From M63 on, every build that sets `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` also sets `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` — to the same path or a sibling under the same scratch root, and for the shell always the same path (`target/gui`) — or it writes a `~/.cargo/build/<hash>/` that nothing removes.
- **Freshness and new majors** (MF-2, G-07). A commit that adds or moves a `Cargo.lock` or `bun.lock` entry takes the newest release eligible under DU-23 when it lands, checked against the registries' dates — by plan-rev3's freshness gate for cargo (`lockdates.py --gate`, built in `dd677009`; the review's MF-2) and by `bunfig.toml` for JS — and its body lists the new major versions it pulls in and from where. The version literals in the entries (dynosaur 0.3.1; the floors of M57, M60 and M61; the Babel host's packages; jsonschema, oxlint, oxlint-tsgolint and knip with their registry dates) say what was eligible when written. *Rev 2.4 (the compatibility report's §2.3, C3, C5).* A floor an entry writes is the version the lock holds when the commit lands (MF-2; plan-rev3 §5.3's preamble (3)): at D1 (a)'s cut P04 leaves tokio-util 0.7.20, reqwest 0.13.5 and clap 4.6.7, so M57's and M61's floors stand and M60 writes `0.7.20` on that lock, its `0.7.16` being the API's minimum, named in the body. For JS, `bunfig.toml` counts the seven days from the install, not from the instant DU-23 counts from, so an install after the cut admits a transitive release DU-23 refuses (undici 8.12.2 and @asamuzakjp/css-color 7.1.6 on 2026-10-17; C5): each JS lock write here (M66, M67, M69) runs C5's check — `lockdates.py --pins npm` over every name@version of the new `bun.lock`, with the max date the cargo gate takes for the same commit, the wave's **2026-10-14T19:53:00Z** for every gate (plan-rev3 rev 3.15, §5.2) — and records it in its `Verified:` line; whether the install-time rule should govern transitive JS packages instead is C5's reading of DU-23 for the owner, not adopted. *Verifier pass (rev 2.4).* "The newest release eligible when the commit lands" (the review's SF-9 wording, in M66, M67 and M82) reads as plan-rev3 §5.3's preamble (1) reads a lock move: the newest release eligible at the cut instant, a release that turns eligible later being §5.1 item 5's before-the-merge sweep's, in its own commit with its gate — so M82 takes 0.58.6, and jsonschema 0.59.0, eligible from 2026-10-23T20:30:14Z, is the sweep's, which carries M82's API and feature check.
- **Dependency source at the locked version** (MF-1). A `[D]` citation names the version read (`tauri-2.12.3`, `tauri-build-2.7.2`, `tauri-utils-2.10.1`, …) and stays the dated read it is. The cut is **at or after 2026-10-21T19:53Z (12:53 PDT)** — the review's D1, taken 2026-10-17 as (a), replacing rev 3.9's 2026-10-17T20:08Z (13:08 PDT) — so P31a takes Tauri's 2026-10-14 family whole, with no Tauri step-back: tauri 2.12.4, tauri-build, tauri-codegen and tauri-macros 2.7.3, tauri-runtime and tauri-runtime-wry 2.12.2, tauri-utils 2.10.2, tauri-plugin 2.7.2, tauri-plugin-fs 2.7.1 and `tauri-plugin-dialog = "=2.8.3"`, paired with `@tauri-apps/api` 2.12.4, `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` 2.8.3 and `@tauri-apps/cli` 2.12.3; wry 0.57.0, tao 0.37.1 and swift-rs 1.0.9 as before, and static_vcruntime 3.0.0 new through tauri-build 2.7.3, Windows only [the compatibility report §2.2, §6.1; C2, C9]. Each commit citing a Tauri crate source (M63, M72–M78) re-reads what it cites at the version the lock holds when it lands — tauri-2.12.4, tauri-build-2.7.3, tauri-codegen-2.7.3, tauri-utils-2.10.2 — and corrects a moved line in its own commit; the 2.12.3-era citations are not rewritten to a version nobody opened. Between the two families the dependency lists changed only as the report's §3.5 lists (rust-version 1.90 throughout), and 2.12.4 fixes a panic in the IPC and asset protocol handlers on an empty URI path (the review's MF-1) — the request paths M72 and M77 reason about, so theirs are the first citations to re-read.
- **Gate counts** (D-14). A test-count gate reads "the parent's count, 0 failed"; a figure from a past run is a record, not the gate.
- **Status words** (D-10). **Recommended** in Part III lands under the answers taken (the owner's delegation of 2026-10-11 and the rule of 2026-10-12); only **Optional** (M53) may be dropped, and **Conditional** waits for its owner question.
- **Memory code** (D-13). §3.5 item 1's read-first rule binds every commit whose sites reach `memory_files`, `memory_index`, `memory::service` or `memory::learning` — M05, M06, M08, M21–M24, M29 (`MemoryError`, `memory/service/error.rs`), M41, M45–M53 and M64 among them — and its `Verified:` line names the reading.
- **Shell commits** (C-10). M63 and M71–M79 run G-GUI beside G-bundle; G-bundle's leg A runs under plan-rev3's corrected launcher (the review's MF-4).
- **DU-33** (the owner, 2026-10-12: macOS first; Linux and Windows verification deferred, CI's existing jobs kept; plan-rev3's DU-33) binds Part III too: M17's `LockFileEx` leg and M18's Windows store difference wait with it, and CI's ubuntu rust job is the one non-macOS build a gate here reads (M39, M65).

### 6.1 The commits, in order

**Phase 0 — the target directory (no Rust or TS source; may land before, during or after the wave)**

**M01 — `docs(claude): build hygiene — one invocation set, one toolchain in target/, cargo clean at a threshold`** · Required · now
- **Contents.** Four lines in CLAUDE.md *Build & Development* (after the command block, before the `Toolchain:` line — `:168` in the working tree, `:169` at HEAD `109d2ba0`): (1) build, test and clippy with `--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui`; `-p <crate>` only to run a filtered test — each distinct package/feature selection costs 0.4–1.7 GiB of `target/` per lock state (**this line is DU-M1 (c) as instruction; (b) changes the manifest** — until DU-M1 is answered the Build block's bare `cargo build` line, which the `default-members` comment at `Cargo.toml:15-16` explains, stays as it is); (2) no toolchain but the pin builds into the repository's `target/`; a session that tries another toolchain uses a worktree with its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`; (3) `cargo clean` when `du -sh target` passes a threshold the owner picks (a cold rebuild of the CI set is 144 s, 118 s with incremental off); never a hook, cron or agent rule that deletes `target/` unasked; (4) `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` runs with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui`, as plan-rev3's G-GUI (`:620`) and Q08 (`:576`) already say, and the sidecar build does once Q02 (`:571`) lands — `target/gui` is a second full tower inside `target/` (≥ 2.73 GiB cold for the workspace alone [M: `attr-C-sizes.tsv`]), so the threshold of line (3) counts it. The numbers from §1 in the commit body.
- **Gate.** `git diff --stat` shows CLAUDE.md only. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Action, not a commit (DU-M4 a):** right after P03 lands locally, one `cargo clean` in the repository, `du -sh target` before and after recorded in P03's `Verified:` line or the session's notes. *Rev 2.4:* P03b moves the pin a second time (D3 (a)); the same clean right after it is recommended on DU-M4 (a)'s reasoning and runs only on the owner's yes — presented, not adopted (§5.1's DU-M4); either way P03b's `Verified:` line records `du -sh target`.

**M02 — `build: default-members = every workspace member but the Tauri shell`** · DU-bound (DU-M1 b) · any time
- **Contents.** `Cargo.toml:15-26`: the comment rewritten (a bare `cargo build`/`test`/`clippy` now selects what `--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui` selects; the shell builds when named), `apps/openalpacad` and `apps/openalpaca` added to the array.

- **Contents.** `.claude/settings.json` = `{"env": {"CARGO_INCREMENTAL": "0"}}`; `.gitignore`: line 139 (`.claude/`) removed, line 140 (`.claude/*`) kept, `!.claude/settings.json` added after it (a file under an excluded directory cannot be re-included, so the directory line must go). **M63 — `build: build.build-dir out of target/; the shell's build-dir beside its target-dir`** · Required (DU-M5 (b), rev 2.0) · **new** · after plan-rev3's P03 (its one `cargo clean`), P31a and Q11, before P04 (§6.3)
- **Contents.** Root `.cargo/config.toml` `[build] build-dir = "{cargo-cache-home}/build/{workspace-path-hash}"`; `apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/.cargo/config.toml` adds `build-dir = "../../../target/gui"` (equal to its `target-dir`), because tauri-build finds its target dir by walking `OUT_DIR` up to `build` and copies the staged sidecar there [D: `tauri-build-2.7.2/src/lib.rs:292-301`]; a root `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` sets `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=target/gui` beside `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui` (CLAUDE.md's hygiene line); CI's `Swatinem/rust-cache` step gains `cache-directories` naming the build-dir; M01's threshold line names it beside `target/`. Final artifacts stay in `target/<profile>/` [M: §1.8], so plan-rev3's Q02 (cargo's JSON artifact paths) and `prepare-sidecar.ts` are unaffected; Cargo 1.100's build-dir layout change is internal and nothing here reads it.

So M63 lands only when every recipe that sets `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` also sets `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` — to the same path or a sibling under the same scratch root, for the shell always the same path — a recipe already amended is checked (the G-base driver is: its amendment of 2026-10-17 sets `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` beside `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` in `cargo_cmd` and in G-GUI's and the suite's environments — `tools/gbase/README.md`, *How to run on the cut day*), the rest changed in this commit: the G-base driver (`tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/gbase.sh:541`, the `CARGO` arrays at `:770` and `:956`, `CARGO_GUI` at `:957`, and the call line its README states, `tools/gbase/README.md:30`, `:90`; lines at HEAD `3598a6fb`); E6's builds (`tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/e6/README.md:44`, the `c()` helper — E6's tools sit under the repository and read the root config); plan-rev3's export recipes (G-GUI's own target dir, E5's `tauri build`, E7's pre-wave build, Q02's G2 gate, Q08's and Q11's `target/gui`); CLAUDE.md's *Build hygiene* lines (the second-toolchain worktree and the shell's `target/gui`, `CLAUDE.md:173`, `:175`); and the scratch-build rule any agent brief states. A recipe that cannot set the variable strips the `build-dir` line from the export's `.cargo/config.toml` after `git archive`. If P03 has deleted the root `.cargo/config.toml` (its `[resolver]` table goes, and the review's G-08 asks P03 to settle `[term] color`), M63 creates it.

**M60 — `refactor: CancellationToken::run_until_cancelled at the two-arm races`** · Required (rev 2.0) · **new** · after plan-rev3's P04 and P38
- **Contents.** `tokio-util = { version = "0.7.16", features = ["rt"] }` (`Cargo.toml:127`; the floor for the up-front cancellation check — rev 2.4: written as the version the lock holds when M60 lands, `0.7.20` at D1 (a)'s cut, 0.7.16 named in the body as the API's minimum; §6's *freshness*). The 28 eligible two-arm `tokio::select!` races between a future and `token.cancelled()` → `let Some(x) = token.run_until_cancelled(fut).await else { … };` (or `.is_none()` for a sleep): `apps/openalpacad/src/background.rs:20`, `:45`, `:77`, `:192`, `:302`, `:340`, `:357`, `:377`; `hot_reload.rs:136`, `:676`, `:731`, `:789`; `event_bridge.rs:22`; `main.rs:681`; `notification/mod.rs:69`; `credential_discovery/mod.rs:127`; `memory/task_extraction/mod.rs:303`; `runner/plugin_agent.rs:251`, `:282`; `runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:852`, `:1156`, `:1454`; `prompt_ctx/compaction/graduated.rs:99` (rev 2.2, D-15: rev 2.0 cited a `context_budget/graduated.rs` that does not exist); `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:603`; `discord/connector.rs:301`, `:351`; `imessage/connector.rs:148`, `:190` [M: the Rust group's `selects.py`]. **The bias, read in source** [D: `tokio-util-0.7.19/src/sync/cancellation_token.rs:300-313`, `:457-469`]: one up-front `is_cancelled()` check, then the **future** is polled first — the 26 unbiased sites become deterministically future-first (at most one more event processed at shutdown, none dropped); the two token-first `biased;` sites (`client.rs:603`, `task_extraction/mod.rs:303`) are read one by one and keep `select!` where the token must win. One site changes what a closed channel does: `hot_reload.rs:136`'s arm is refutable (`Some(ev) = wake_rx.recv()`, `:137`), so today a closed channel disables that arm and the loop waits for the token; through `run_until_cancelled` the closed channel is `Some(None)`, and the converted loop **breaks** on it (nothing can arrive after it) — stated in the body (rev 2.2, D-15). Excluded and kept: `apps/openalpacad/src/routes/chat.rs:550` (an already-cancelled token would return `None` before a buffered `done` is read — D-E's `sse_frames` contract) and the three-arm `memory/learning/queue.rs:1715`, `routes/events.rs:96`. Each site's ordering stated in the body.
- **Rev 2.3 — the plugin agent's two RPCs (the ZCode audit's A-35, Z4-12).** `executor.spawn` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/runner/plugin_agent.rs:142-145`) and `executor.step` (`:198`) are awaited without racing the token, bounded only by the plugin channel's 60 s default (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/process_pool.rs:85-90`) — up to 60 s of cancel latency per call, with the permit held. Where a token is present (`cancel_token: Option<&CancellationToken>`, `plugin_agent.rs:139`), both take `run_until_cancelled`, and a cancel ends as the loop's existing arms end it: `executor.stop(instance_id)` and `PluginLoopOutcome::Failed { error: "Cancelled" }` (`:259-263`). Thirty sites with these two. N-LEAD-2 (before the cut, §6.3 step 1a) already acquires its queued permit in this form, which tokio-util 0.7.19, in the lock, carries (`src/sync/cancellation_token.rs:300`); M60 brings the remaining waits to it.
- **Gate.** The touched crates' tests; `cargo test -p openalpacad graceful_shutdown`; G-CI.
- **Back-out.** Revert.

**M15 — `feat(mcp): protocol-level cancellation, the MRTR loop and progress-reset timeouts through send_cancellable_request`** · DU-bound (**DU-R4 (v)**, rev 2.0: **(b)** — the raw path with the MRTR loop; and **DU-R4 (i)**: the progress-reset timeout) · **after P19** (rmcp 3.5.x); the call path is decided, not in the commit
- **Contents (under (a) — not taken; kept as the base the rev 2.0 contents below extend).** `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:558`, `:585-593`: `running.call_tool(params)` under `tokio::time::timeout` → `peer.send_cancellable_request(ClientRequest::CallToolRequest(..), PeerRequestOptions::with_timeout(request_timeout))`, the `RequestHandle` awaited under our `CancellationToken` and `cancel(reason)` sent when the token fires; a `ServerResult::CallToolResult` is the result, a `ServerResult::InputRequiredResult` becomes a tool error whose text says the server asked for input this client does not provide (rmcp's wrapper would have answered it with `create_elicitation`'s `Decline`, up to 10 rounds — DU-R4 (v)), any other variant `UnexpectedResponse`; the "later refinement" comment replaced by the contract. Under (b) the same plus a ≤ 10-round loop that feeds the Decline back, each round through `send_cancellable_request`.
- **Rev 2.0 — the contents under (v) (b) and (i).** The raw path above plus a ≤ `DEFAULT_MRTR_MAX_ROUNDS` (10) loop that answers each `InputRequiredResult` through `ClientHandler` (elicitation's `Decline` until DU-R4 (ii) is answered, then M84's card), each round a `send_cancellable_request` our token can cancel; past the 10th round an error naming the cap. The request carries `PeerRequestOptions { timeout: Some(request_timeout), reset_timeout_on_progress: true, max_total_timeout: Some(max_total_timeout) }` [D: `rmcp-3.5.1/src/service.rs:772-806`], `max_total_timeout` from a new `config/mcp.toml` key `max_total_timeout_secs` (proposed default 300, beside `request_timeout_secs = 30`, `config/mcp.toml:11`); `ClientHandler::on_progress` logged at debug (no new event variant). The docs name the key (`docs/Daemon_Manual.md`'s `mcp.toml` table). Gate addition: a stub reporting progress past `request_timeout` completes; one past `max_total_timeout_secs` is cut with rmcp's `notifications/cancelled`; one input-required round answered and the server's final answer returned. **The rmcp gap, named (rev 2.2, E-15):** rmcp 3.5 has no cancellable `call_tool` that runs the MRTR loop — `Peer::call_tool` loops but takes no `PeerRequestOptions` and hands back no cancel handle, `send_cancellable_request` does both but answers no round, and the loop's helper `prepare_input_required_retry` is private [D: `rmcp-3.5.0/src/service/client.rs:2098-2130`] — so M15 copies the loop (≈ 60 lines). Way out: an rmcp issue asking for the loop on the cancellable path (a `call_tool` that takes `PeerRequestOptions` and returns a cancellable handle); the owner posts it, and M15's copy goes once a release carrying it is eligible.
- **Rev 2.3 — the call bounded where it is enforced (the ZCode audit's A-32; Z5-06, Z5-02).** Two fixes in this commit, one question kept out of it. *(a) The cancel always reaches the server.* Today the registry passes no token (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/registry/mod.rs:852`), the sandbox ends an over-time call by dropping its future (`security/sandbox/mod.rs:542-547`), a cancelled run's loop drops its tool futures from its cancellation arm (`security/sandbox/mod.rs:689-691` says so), and rmcp's `RequestHandle` has no `Drop` (`rmcp-3.5.1/src/service.rs:542-550`), so neither a sandbox cut nor `/cancel` tells the server anything. M15 gives `ToolContext` a cancellation token for the call — a child of the loop's (`runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:434`), cancelled by the sandbox when its deadline passes — which the registry's MCP arm passes to `McpClient::call_tool`, where a cancelled token tells the server. It cannot do so through `cancel(reason)`, as the base above has it: `await_response` takes the handle by value (`rmcp-3.5.1/src/service.rs:555`; so does `cancel`, `:666`), so once the response is awaited no handle is left to cancel, and a future dropped before its token is seen sends nothing at all. The in-flight request therefore holds a guard — a clone of the handle's public `peer` and `id` — which is OpenAlpaca's one sender: the response is awaited in a `select!` against the token, and a token that fires, or a drop before `await_response` returns, sends one `notifications/cancelled` naming that id (with the token's reason, or spawned from the drop). The guard is disarmed whenever the handle is consumed: `await_response` returning, whatever it returns — a response, rmcp's own timeout, which has already sent its notification (`send_timeout_cancel_notification`, `:570`, `:636`, `:646`), or a closed transport — and `cancel` sent, should a path ever send it before awaiting. So rmcp's timeout and the guard never both send, and a request id sees at most one `notifications/cancelled`. A dropped `await_response` also skips rmcp's removal of the call's progress watcher (`:687-698`; registered at `:901-907`), which rmcp then removes only on a later progress notice for that token (`:1018-1022`) or with the connection: one small map entry per cut call, out of the guard's reach (`Peer`'s field is private, `:730`), named beside E-15's gap in the rmcp issue above. The sandbox's bound itself is unchanged: it still drops the call at its deadline. *(b) One bound, the sandbox's.* `max_tool_runtime_secs` is 60 s for an agent and 300 s for the lead (`config/daemon.toml:4`, `:13`), so the proposed `max_total_timeout_secs = 300` would bind only the lead, and race it there. The sandbox passes the call's own bound on the same context, and each request takes `max_total_timeout = min(max_total_timeout_secs, that bound less a margin the body states)`, so rmcp's own clock fires first and sends its `notifications/cancelled` itself; the manuals' `mcp.toml` table says the smaller wins. *Kept out, pending:* whether a server's progress may extend the sandbox's clock — DU-R4 (i)'s rev 2.3 sub-question, recommended no; under that answer progress resets only rmcp's idle clock, inside the bound. *Order and contract.* M15 lands after N-MCP-1 and N-MCP-3 — plan-rev3's commits at Phase G's head, before P19 (§6.3 step 2) — and keeps their contract: a request that may have reached the server is never sent again — each MRTR round is its own request, sent once — a dead connection is reconnected once before sending, inside the call's one deadline, and the request goes out on a `Peer` cloned out of the service lock. Gate addition: a stub holding a call past the sandbox's bound, one ended by a run's `/cancel` and one cut by rmcp's own timeout each record exactly one `notifications/cancelled` naming the request's id, and a call answered records none; an agent-policy call under `max_total_timeout_secs = 300` is cut by rmcp's clock before the sandbox's 60 s.
- **Gate.** P19's real-server round trip; tests with the stdio fixture that a cancelled call emits `notifications/cancelled` with our reason, that a timed-out one emits rmcp's, and that an `InputRequiredResult` round is answered through the handler (the Decline until DU-R4 (ii)) and the round past the 10th reported as the cap's error (rev 2.2, E-15: rev 2.0 kept option (a)'s "reported as the error text, not awaited" here); `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp`; `gen_api_docs.py --check` if a doc comment changed. **Back-out.** Revert (the local bail-out returns).

**M17 — `build: std::fs::File::{lock, try_lock} replace the file-lock crate`** · DU-bound (DU-R2 a) · after P03
- **Contents.** `storage/discovery/mod.rs:187-205` (the singleton lock; `block` → `lock()` vs `try_lock()`), `core/config_io.rs:241-256`, `llm/keys/key_encryption/mod.rs:299-315`: a `struct … (File)` guard whose drop closes the file (std: locks are released when the file is closed); `TryLockError::WouldBlock` mapped where `file-lock`'s `os error 35/11` was; `file-lock` removed from `Cargo.toml:99` and the three crate manifests. The commit body records the primitive change (`fcntl` → `flock`), the mixed-build window and that "the process, then the lock, never the port" is unchanged.
- **Rev 2.2 — exclusion inside one process (D-06).** An `fcntl` record lock never conflicts within one process: a second acquisition there succeeds, and closing either descriptor releases both — as the CLI's `LockHolder` comment says, correctly (`apps/openalpaca/src/test_util.rs:150-156`). A `flock` lock belongs to the open file description, so after M17 two writers of one config file in the same process **exclude each other**. That closes a latent lost-update race (today both "hold" `<path>.lock`, read the old text, and the second write discards the first), and it means a path that takes a lock it already holds now **blocks**. So M17: audits every caller for re-acquisition (`atomic_write_with_backup`'s callers hold `<path>.lock` already; `persist_only` holds it through `acquire_config_write_lock`); rewrites the comments that state the semantics — `crates/openalpaca_core/src/config_io.rs:138-141` and `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/config/settings_service.rs:36-37` ("`file_lock` locks per descriptor, so taking the lock again would deadlock": false under `fcntl`, true under `flock`) and `test_util.rs:150-156` (true under `fcntl`, false under `flock`); adds one test with concurrent in-process writers (every write survives); and states the bound in the body: a blocked writer waits for the holder's one read-modify-write, `lock()` has no timeout, and the test fails on its own timeout rather than hanging.
- **Gate.** The CLI's cross-process `LockHolder` test (`apps/openalpaca/src/test_util.rs:160-245`); `cargo test -p openalpaca_storage -p openalpaca_core -p openalpaca_llm`; a manual second-daemon start against an isolated store (sandboxed, §5.2 of plan-rev3) refused with the new wording; `cargo tree -i file-lock` empty; the `LockFileEx` leg only if Windows is ever built. **Back-out.** Revert and `cargo update -p file-lock --precise 2.1.11`.

The commit body records the Windows difference (known-folder API vs `USERPROFILE`/`GetUserProfileDirectoryW`).
