**M19–M38 — `refactor(<crate>): thiserror derive for <Type>`** ×20 · Recommended · one type per commit, squashable by crate at review; `ProjectError` (M19) and `RevealRefusal` (M20) **after Q06 or before Q05** (their files move)
- **The twenty**, storage then core: **M19** `ProjectError` (`store/projects.rs:342-375`), **M20** `RevealRefusal` (`store/reveal.rs:50-76`), **M21** `SnapshotError` (`memory_files/snapshot.rs`, `:206`), **M22** `MemoryFilesError` (`memory_files/mod.rs:268-315`), **M23** `IndexError` (`memory_index/mod.rs`, `:143`), **M24** `NoteParseError` (`memory_files/note.rs`, `:253`), **M25** `RunsInFlight` (`project_life/delete.rs`, `:98`), **M26** `RepointInterrupted` (`project_life.rs`, `:58`), **M27** `UploadError` (`uploads/mod.rs`, `:138`), **M28** `ArtifactError` (`artifacts/mod.rs:298-333`); **M29** `MemoryError` (`memory/service/error.rs:98-152`), **M30** `SecurityViolation` (`security/capabilities/mod.rs:35-69`), **M31** `WorkingFolderRefusal` (`tools/working_folder.rs:112-143`), **M32** `ConstraintConflict` (`skill/constraints.rs:37-58`), **M33** `AgentParseError` (`agent/template/mod.rs:107-122`), **M34** `UserParseError`, **M35** `SoulParseError`, **M36** `SkillParseError`, **M37** `BootstrapParseError`, **M38** `IdentityParseError` (`middleware/{user,soul,skill,bootstrap,identity}/mod.rs`). M23's `IndexError` sits inside `memory_index/` — the derive changes no query, ranking or chunking code, so §3.5 item 1's `SCHEMA_VERSION` rule is not triggered; the commit body says so. **M21–M24 are memory code** (`memory_files/snapshot.rs`, `memory_files/mod.rs`, `memory_index/mod.rs`, `memory_files/note.rs`): CLAUDE.md's read-first rule applies — 03 §7a/§7c and the 09 revision-log entry of the slice that wrote each type, read before the derive and named in the commit body — and their gates add the file layer's and the index's own suites (`cargo test -p openalpaca_storage memory_files memory_index`, the fixtures `memory_files::fixtures` and `memory_index::fixture_tests` among them) beside the golden test; the error texts the doctor and `openalpaca memory` print are among the refusal sentences the manuals quote. **M29 is memory code too** (`memory/service/error.rs`, in core; rev 2.2, D-13): the same reading, named in its body, and its gate adds `cargo test -p openalpaca_core memory::service` beside the golden tests; `MemoryError` is the wrapper whose `{:#}` text the golden tests below pin.
- **Contents, each.** First a golden test enumerating every variant and asserting its `to_string()` against the manual `impl`'s output (literal strings in the test); then the derive (`#[derive(thiserror::Error)]`, `#[error("…")]` per variant, `#[error(fmt = path)]` where an arm computes its text), the manual `impl fmt::Display` and `impl std::error::Error` deleted. **No `#[source]`** (rev 2.2, SF-25): every variant's `source()` stays `None`, as the manual impls give today, so every text stays byte for byte — the type's own and every wrapper's `{:#}`, which walks `source()` (`MemoryError::Internal` prints `{e:#}`, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/memory/service/error.rs:147`, over an `IndexError` kept whole, `:184-191`; a source on `IndexError::Sqlite` would print "memory index database: X: X"). thiserror adds a source implicitly in two ways, both refused here: `#[from]` — the hand-written `From` impls stay (`memory_files/mod.rs:317`, `:323`; `memory_index/mod.rs:163`, `:169`; `memory/service/error.rs:154`, `:184`, `:205`) — and a field named `source` (none among the twenty [M: grep, 2026-10-17]). Each golden test also asserts `source().is_none()` for every variant. Rev 2.0's "`#[source]` added only where a cause is already carried and swallowed" is withdrawn: exposing a cause changes what a person reads through a wrapper (`openalpaca memory doctor`, the manuals' quoted sentences), a behaviour change to present with its reason, not part of a derive. **Before M21–M23**, golden tests of the `{:#}` text through every wrapper of the memory types — at least `MemoryError::Internal` over `IndexError` (`:184-191`) and over a `MemoryFilesError` (`:179`, `anyhow!("{other}")`) — are written against the unchanged code and kept. The golden test stays.
- **Rev 2.3 — M36 and N-SKILL-2 (the ZCode audit's A-38, Z3-03).** N-SKILL-2 serves `SkillParseError`'s `Display` strings as skill diagnostics (a new `GET /v1/skills/diagnostics`, `openalpaca skills list|info`, a count in Settings → Tools). It lands before M36 (before the cut, §6.3 step 1a; if not ready, in the wave before P30, plan-rev3 §5.4), so M36's golden test pins the strings N-SKILL-2 already serves, and N-SKILL-2's own test of them may be the one M36 keeps rather than a second copy. *Rev 2.4 (D4 evidence; D4 pending):* `InvalidYaml(String)` carries the YAML crate's own message (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/middleware/skill/mod.rs:54`, `:63` at HEAD), which P30 changes from serde_yaml's to serde-saphyr's — and 1.3's text differs from 2.0's, the only difference the report found between the two, 2.0's one line each and cleaner [the compatibility report §5]. M36's golden test builds each variant with a literal payload, so it pins the frame `Invalid YAML: {msg}`, not the crate's text; a test kept from N-SKILL-2 that parses a real malformed file pins that text, and is recorded on the parser P30 took.
- **Gate, each.** The golden test green before and after; the crate's suite; the manuals' quoted refusal sentences unchanged (`git grep -F` of each variant's text under `docs/`); G-lint unchanged. **Back-out.** Revert the one commit.

**M39 — `style: #[expect] for every allow`** · **Required** (rev 2.0; rev 1.1: "where an allow holds under every documented selection") · after M38 (the derives change which allows still hold)
- **Contents (rev 1.1's scope — superseded by the Rev 2.0 bullet below, which converts every allow; its list of selections still names what the gate builds).** Of the 134 `#[allow(…)]`, those whose lint fires under **every documented selection** (§1.5's 13 — feature unification differs per selection, so an `expect` fulfilled under CI's `--all-targets` can be unfulfilled under a `-p` test build and warn in an agent session) become `#[expect(…)]`: the bare `cargo build` (after M02, every member but the shell), CI's `cargo build|test --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui` and `cargo clippy --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets`, `cargo test -p <crate> --no-run` for each of the eight library crates, the daemon and the CLI, `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --features live-eval --test memory_eval --no-run`, and `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` (its own target dir); the rest (e.g. `connectors/lib.rs:71`'s `dead_code`, which holds only with every connector feature on) stay `allow` with a comment naming the selection under which the lint is silent. A `-p` build that warns is **not** accepted: an agent session's `cargo test -p <crate>` must stay warning-free.
- **Rev 2.0.** **Every** `#[allow(lint)]` becomes `#[expect(lint, reason = "…")]` — 135 attributes [M: the Rust verifier: `too_many_arguments` 61, `dead_code` 48, `result_large_err` 14, `type_complexity` 3, `unused_variables` 2 (one the multi-lint allow at `llm/config/llm_config/router_builder.rs:39`), `unused_imports` 2, `unreachable_patterns` 2, `large_enum_variant` 2, `should_implement_trait` 1]; a lint that fires only under some selection takes `#[cfg_attr(<where it fires>, expect(lint, reason = "…"))]` (the connectors' conditions name `target_os = "macos"` beside the features, the iMessage connector being macOS-only), an item read only by tests `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(dead_code, reason = "…"))]` — which removes the unfulfilled-expectation risk that made rev 1.1 keep `allow`; the 23 deserialized-but-unread fields in `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/tail.rs` are removed or read; R79's `created_by: _` (`event_bridge.rs:37`) under `#[expect(clippy::unneeded_wildcard_pattern, reason = "R79")]`. Gate addition: `rg -n '#!?\[allow\(' crates apps` → 0.
- **Rev 2.2 (SF-28, MF-5).** The selections gain the one non-macOS build any gate here reads: CI's rust job on ubuntu-24.04 (`cargo clippy --locked --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets`), read from its log on the draft PR (plan-rev3's push step) **before any allow is converted**. Over 250 platform `cfg` sites (the review counted 254 in 54 files) have never been compiled on Linux, and an `expect` fulfilled only on macOS is `unfulfilled_lint_expectations` there, so a lint that fires on one platform only takes `cfg_attr(target_os = …)` too. A local `cargo clippy --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` would serve only where every C dependency cross-compiles (bundled libsqlite3-sys, aws-lc-rs) [NE]. The shell's selection runs with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=target/gui` (M63).
- **Rev 2.3.** The ZCode audit's fixes in §6.3 steps 1a, 2, 3a, 6b and 8 land before M39, so an allow any of them adds is converted with the rest; N-GUI-1 (step 11) adds no Rust. Keeping a new bare `#[allow]` out after M39 is DU-R3's rev 2.3 sub-question (pending), not this commit.
- **Gate.** 0 `unfulfilled_lint_expectations` under each selection named above, the list in `Verified:`; G-CI. **Back-out.** Revert.

**M42 — `refactor(gui): skipToken for the id-guarded queries`** · Recommended · after P31
- **Contents.** **All 16** queries of §4.1 row 4 (rev 2.0): `queryFn: id === null ? skipToken : ({signal}) => get(id, signal)`, the `enabled:` line and the `id as string` cast removed — `useMemoryNote` and `useWorkspace` included, with **no caller change**: `refetch()` rejects only while the query's *current* `queryFn` is `skipToken` [D: `@tanstack/query-core@5.102.8` `build/modern/utils.js:160-166`], and both hand-refetch callers run with a non-null id (`MemoryDetail.tsx:355` past the `model === undefined` return at `:154-155`; `ProjectsCard.tsx:210` with `project.root`). Rev 1.1's "14 of the 16" rested on the docs' blanket caveat. **Rev 2.2 (SF-27): the `skipToken` condition is the whole former `enabled:` predicate, not its id half.** Six of the sixteen guards are compound, and dropping the other half would fetch what the guard keeps out: `useArtifactDiff` (`hooks/useArtifacts.ts:176`, `from >= 1 && to > from` — an invalid diff), `useArtifactText` (`:189`, `:194`, the caller's `enabled` — its large-file and binary preview policy), `useMemoryRevision` (`hooks/useMemories.ts:208`, `revision !== null`), and `hooks/useTasks.ts:64`, `hooks/useEventHistory.ts:41`, `hooks/useFollowups.ts:35` (each `!== ""`). So each becomes `queryFn: <the whole predicate> ? ({signal}) => … : skipToken`.
- **Gate.** The four bun gates; a test that a disabled query is not fetched and that `refetch()` is never invoked on a skipToken query; for each of the six compound guards, a test that nothing is fetched while any part of the predicate is false (rev 2.2); `bun run check` with no cast left (`grep -c 'as string' src/hooks/*.ts` down by the converted count). **Back-out.** Revert.

**M55 (a–d) — `refactor(<crate>): enum dispatch for <Trait>`** ×4 · **Required** (rev 2.0) · after M13, **before M58** (so these four never take dynosaur)
- **Contents.** One commit per trait: **(a)** `Transport` (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/mod.rs:23`; `TransportKind` is already an enum, so `build_transport`'s `Box<dyn Transport>` at `client.rs:619` becomes an enum-dispatched `connect`, ≈ 30 lines); **(b)** `PersonaHandler` (`core/src/tools/builtins/update_persona/mod.rs:33`; an enum over soul/user/identity replaces `&dyn PersonaHandler` at `:81`, ≈ 20 lines); **(c)** `EventWatcher` (`wake/src/watcher/mod.rs:11`; one impl today, `Vec<Box<dyn EventWatcher>>` at `wake/manager/mod.rs:19`). **M57 — `fix(llm): a typed reqwest error classification — the retry classifier sees real transport failures`** · **Required** (rev 2.0; a latent defect) · after plan-rev3's P04 (reqwest 0.13.5); the first Part III source commit after P38
- **Contents.** The providers map `reqwest::Error` through one function — `is_timeout`, `is_connect`, `is_dns` (0.13.5), `is_request`, `is_body` — into typed `LlmError` variants (the shape below, rev 2.2), at `providers/openai/mod.rs:184`, `:232`, `:314`, `providers/anthropic/mod.rs:126`, `:175`, `:254`, `providers/ollama.rs:129`, `:196`, `:315` (today `LlmError::Http(e.to_string())`); `is_transient_http_error` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/error.rs:89-111`) classifies the typed variants; the CLI's REPL check (`apps/openalpaca/src/repl/mod.rs:88-89`, `is_connect() || is_timeout()`) adds `is_dns()`; floor `reqwest = "0.13.5"`. Why a fix: reqwest's `Display` writes only the kind and the URL, never the source chain [D: `reqwest-0.13.5/src/error.rs:257-305`], so the classifier's substrings ("timed out", "reset by peer", …) never match a real send failure and timeouts and resets are not retried today; the unit tests at `lib.rs:242-277` pass on hand-written strings reqwest never produces [C]. Rev 1.1 cited `lib.rs:88`, which is `chat_with_key`'s default body.
- **Rev 2.2 — the cause's type, the retry table and the bound (SF-24).** *The type.* `LlmError` and `LlmRouterError` derive `Clone` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/error.rs:1`; `routing/router/types.rs:68-69`) and `reqwest::Error` is not `Clone`, so rev 2.0's "keep the error as `#[source]`" does not compile. The variant carries a typed kind and a message and no source: `LlmError::Transport { kind, message }`, `kind` one of connect timeout, request timeout, connect refused, DNS, reset or closed, TLS or other, and `message` reqwest's text followed by its source chain joined with `: ` (the chain its `Display` leaves out). `Clone` holds, and `{e}` and `{e:#}` print the same text once — most callers print with `{e}` (`orchestrator/working_state.rs:512`), where a cause kept only in `source()` would vanish. *The retry table* — what each kind does at each layer; the gate tests every cell:

  | Kind | `complete`'s key layer (`routing/router/retry.rs:203`: `max(pool.len(), max_transient_retries = 3)` attempts, backoff with jitter, then at most 2 cooldown waits of ≤ 30 s) | `complete`'s fallback chain (`completion.rs:189-196`, then the CLI backend) | `complete_streaming` (`completion.rs:18-132`; on its error the loop's backend retries the call through `complete`, `runner/agentic_loop/backend.rs:31-32`, `:159`) | the agentic loop (`runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:1429-1440`: 3 retries, `2^n` s apart, ≤ 30 s, each a round) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | connect timeout | retried | taken | returned at once (`:128`) | retried |
  | request timeout — a non-streaming call's `request_timeout` deadline, or the read gap (`providers/mod.rs:29`) | **the owner's question below**: under (a) not retried, on the same key or another — returned at once, still transient; under (b) retried like a connect timeout | taken | returned at once | retried |
  | reset, closed, incomplete body | retried | taken | returned at once | retried |
  | connect refused, DNS, TLS, other | not retried (the classifier's own rule, `error.rs:86-88`) | not taken | returned at once | not retried |

  *The request-timeout question* — raised by this file in rev 2.2, where the review's SF-24 asked whether a timeout that used the whole `request_timeout` is retried on the same key; not one of the review's D1–D13; **pending**, for the lead to put to the owner. Today one predicate, `LlmError::is_transient()` (`error.rs:102`), decides all three layers — the key ladder (`retry.rs:203`), the fallback chain (`completion.rs:190`) and the loop (`runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:1435`) — and its doc comment (`error.rs:101`) calls a transient error "worth retrying with the same key", with a timeout among the substrings it matches (`:91-92`). **(a)**, recommended: the key layer does not retry a request timeout. A key-layer predicate beside `is_transient()` — false for `Transport { kind: RequestTimeout, .. }`, otherwise `is_transient()` — guards `retry.rs:203`'s arm, so the timeout leaves through the final arm (`:221-225`) as `LlmRouterError::Llm`, which the chain (`completion.rs:190`) and the loop (`agentic_loop/mod.rs:1435`) still take because `is_transient()` stays true for it; the doc comment is narrowed to say so. The reason: a server silent for the whole `request_timeout` is not helped by another key, and `max(pool.len(), 3)` tries would multiply the configured timeout (L7) by three or more before the chain is reached. **(b)**, the documented intent: the key layer retries it like a connect timeout, and one predicate stays. M57 lands under the answer given; the row above, the bound and the gate are written for both.

  *The bound*, against a silent listener (it accepts and never answers), with C the CLI backend's run where one is registered for the provider (`routing/router/fallback.rs:30-89`; 0 otherwise — the gate builds its router with none). Under (a): one `complete` call waits at most `(1 + chain length) × request_timeout + C`; one loop attempt of a streamed turn adds the failed stream first, so at most `(2 + chain length) × request_timeout + C`; the loop's three retries make a turn at most `4 × ((2 + chain length) × request_timeout + C) + 14 s`. Under (b): in those sums each model's term inside a `complete` call is taken `max(pool.len(), 3)` times, for that model's own pool (the failed stream stays one term), plus the key layer's backoff between tries (each wait at most `backoff_cap_ms`, 30 s by default). A direct router caller (`working_state.rs:509-512`) gets one `complete` call's share. *The breaker.* The router has one circuit breaker, owned by `RateLimiterRegistry` (`routing/rate_limiter/mod.rs:456-478`), not one per provider. A failed try on the key ladder reports to it — rate-limited, overloaded, transient and every other error (`retry.rs:147`, `:190`, `:217`, `:224`), a request timeout included under either answer; an authentication error does not, and an empty completion counts as a success (`:86`) — and after `circuit_breaker_threshold` consecutive failures across all providers (default 5, `rate_limiter/mod.rs:430-432`) it opens. While it is open, every `complete` call fails at once at `retry.rs:18`, every fallback model included (each rung is `try_model` → `execute_with_retry`, `completion.rs:221`), and lands on the CLI backend, which the breaker does not guard — so such a call costs only C. `complete_streaming` neither consults it nor reports to it, so a streamed attempt is still made and still waits out its stream. The commit body records the latency change against L7: today a timeout fails once; after M57 it walks the fallback chain and the loop retries it (under (b), the key ladder first).
- **Gate.** The retry table above, under the answer taken, tested with **real** reqwest errors (a refused port, a silent listener past its timeout, an unresolvable host), on the non-streaming and the streaming path; the wall time against a silent listener measured with a short `request_timeout`, a fallback chain and no CLI backend, and held to that answer's bound; the breaker case on the model above — after `circuit_breaker_threshold` consecutive failures a `complete` call fails at once at `retry.rs:18` on every rung of the chain, while a streamed call is still made; the router's retry tests (`routing/router/retry.rs:203`, `completion.rs:190`); the user-visible error text changes recorded in the body. **Back-out.** Revert.

**Added in rev 2.0** (the owner's rule of 2026-10-12)

**M58 (a–j) — `refactor(<crate>): native async fn in trait + dynosaur for <Trait>`** ×10 · Required (DU-R1 (b)) · after plan-rev3's P19 and P38, M13 and M55 (a–d); crate order api → llm → core
- **Contents.** One trait per commit: **(a)** `PluginToolExecutor` — and `dynosaur = "0.3.1"` into `[workspace.dependencies]` and the crate's manifest, the floor the version taken (0.3.1, 2026-07-03, rust-version 1.84, is the newest eligible at D1 (a)'s cut — a registry fact [the compatibility report §2.3]) — **(b)** `ToolCallbackExecutor`, **(c)** `PluginSkillExecutor`, **(d)** `PluginAgentExecutor` (`crates/openalpaca_api/src/plugin_traits.rs:8`, `:31`, `:39`, `:66`), **(e)** `LlmProvider` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/lib.rs:63`), **(f)** `BuiltInTool` (`core/src/tools/registry/mod.rs:318`), **(g)** `MessageHandler` (`core/src/gateway/router/mod.rs:194`), **(h)** `ConnectorSendProvider` (`core/src/orchestrator/mod.rs:85`), **(i)** `ContextSource` (`core/src/prompt_ctx/sources/mod.rs:31`), **(j)** `Summarizer` (`core/src/context_budget/compaction.rs:11`). Each: the methods declared `fn m(&self, …) -> impl Future<Output = T> + Send`, `Future` spelled `std::future::Future` or the prelude's, never `futures::Future`, which dynosaur's macro does not box (rev 2.2, D-12) (explicit `+ Send`: return-type notation is not stable, and a bare `async fn` in a public trait warns `async_fn_in_trait`); `#[dynosaur::dynosaur(pub DynX = dyn(box) X)]` on the trait (the spelling dynosaur 0.3 requires); the 10 default `async fn` bodies (`BuiltInTool` 3; `LlmProvider` 5 — `chat_with_key`, `list_models_with_key`, `discover_models`, `chat_streaming`, `chat_streaming_with_key`, `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/lib.rs:81-133`; `MessageHandler` 1; `ConnectorSendProvider` 1 — rev 2.0 said 9, D-12) as `fn … -> impl Future<Output = T> + Send { async move { … } }`; every impl loses the attribute and keeps its `async fn` body; every `dyn X` becomes `DynX<'_>` / `Arc<DynX<'static>>`, built with `DynX::new_arc` / `from_arc` / `from_ref` [D: `dynosaur_derive` 0.3.1 `lib.rs:508-547`] — so every construction site changes too, not only the ≈ 89 `dyn` uses: each commit counts its trait's construction sites in its body, and a registration API that can take `impl X` and build the `DynX` itself is preferred to a `DynX` at every caller (rev 2.2, D-12); the `use async_trait::async_trait;` lines go. A dynamic call boxes as `async-trait` did; a static call no longer boxes.
- **Rev 2.3 (the ZCode audit's A-34).** (e) converts no plugin implementation: M86, at the head of §6.3's step 7, deletes `PluginLlmProvider` under the owner's D-I (revised), so `LlmProvider`'s impls are the three providers' and the test doubles'. (a)–(d) land after N-PLUG-6 (before the cut, §6.3 step 1a) and N-PLUG-1 (§6.3 step 6b), so the executor traits are converted over a tool bridge that reports `isError` and a manager that removes only what a plugin owns.
- **Gate, each.** `cargo check --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked`; the crate's tests (the workspace's for (e) and (f), whose impls are spread); clippy 0 new; a grep that the trait has no `where Self: Sized` method and no associated const (dynosaur refuses both). (e) and (f) budget for higher-ranked "`Send` is not general enough" errors; (f)'s compile time recorded against its parent.
- **Back-out.** Revert the one commit.

WKDownload needs macOS 11.3, below every floor plan-rev3's DU-31 sub-row offers. Written under owner question (c)-5's answer (a) (§5.4): the window built here keeps today's decorations.

The audit puts the remedy in Q14 (plan-rev3, its A-25); if Q14 has not taken it when M76 lands, M76 does: `on_web_content_process_terminate` on the app's `tauri::Builder` (tauri-2.12.3 `src/app.rs:1910`, macOS) writes a line to `gui.log` and reloads the window — safe, because the state lives in the daemon — and a `std::panic::set_hook` writes to `gui.log`. G-bundle then kills the WebContent process and sees the window reload and reconnect to the same `instance_id`.

**M77 — `feat(gui-shell): external links open in the browser; navigation stays in the app`** · Required · after M76 and M79 (rev 2.2, D-18: the shell has no direct `opener` dependency until M79 adds one — today only the daemon names it, `apps/openalpacad/Cargo.toml:50`)
- **Contents.** On M76's builder: `.on_new_window(…)` handing an http(s) URL to `opener::open_browser` and answering `Deny` — the Extensions row's "Authorize at <url>" link (`views/settings/ExtensionsSection.tsx:419-427`, `target="_blank"`) opens the default browser instead of nothing [D: `wry-0.57.0/src/wkwebview/class/wry_web_view_ui_delegate.rs:173-204`]; `.on_navigation(…)` admitting only the app's own origin (the devUrl in dev) and handing an http(s) URL to `opener::open_browser`, so a link in agent-written markdown (`DocumentPreview.tsx:20-22`, `:139`; DOMPurify keeps `<a href>`) no longer replaces the app in its only window [D: `tauri-2.12.3/src/manager/webview.rs:601-627`]. `opener` is the shell's own dependency from M79 on; no plugin and no capability.
- **Rev 2.3 — the exact entry document, and the preview's own anchors (the ZCode audit's A-39, Z6-03; it completes this entry's stated intent).** "Only the app's own origin" admits too much: wry asks the policy about same-origin navigations too, and Tauri answers an unknown asset path with `index.html` (tauri-2.12.3 `src/manager/mod.rs:388-416`), so a click on `[notes](/Users/me/proj/notes.md)` in agent-written markdown would navigate the app's only page to a fresh copy of itself — a blank window or a cold re-bootstrap [NE until run]. So `on_navigation` admits exactly the entry document (the devUrl's root in dev), refuses every other same-origin path, hands an http(s) URL to `opener::open_browser`, and refuses every other scheme: `file:` is never handed to the operating system, and ST-18's Open by id stays the only way to open a local file (ZCode's `file:` branch opens any file with no check on its kind, ZCode:packages/desktop/src/main/desktopMainIpcHelpers.ts:28-58). `on_new_window` takes the same rule. And DocumentPreview (`components/work/preview/DocumentPreview.tsx:20-21`, `:139`) handles a click on an anchor itself: an http(s) URL goes the browser's way above; a fragment scrolls within the preview and navigates nothing; any other href — an absolute or a relative path, `file:`, `mailto:`, any other scheme — is prevented, nothing navigated. Offering Open or Reveal for such a link is not part of M77 (it would go through the daemon's routes only, ST-18; the review's D8, pending, decides Reveal's argument). Gate addition: G-bundle clicks an absolute path, a relative path, a fragment, a `mailto:` and an http link in a previewed markdown file and finds the app on its page after each, the http one opened in the browser; the four bun gates, with a DocumentPreview test per shape.
- **Gate.** G-bundle: the Authorize link (a fixture MCP server answering 401 with a hint) and a markdown link both open the default browser; the app stays on its page. Both links point at loopback URLs (a local fixture page), so the browser they open reaches nothing beyond the machine (rev 2.2, the review's MF-4).
- **Back-out.** Revert.

The build's commits are sized by the design.

**M84 — `feat(mcp): MCP elicitation answered by a person, as a form, through the ConfirmationBroker`** · **Conditional** (DU-R4 (ii) with T35 — (c)-3 in `tasks/pending-decisions.md`, an owner question, recommended yes in the rev 2.3 shape below) · after M15
- **Contents (rev 2.0; superseded in shape by rev 2.3 below — the broker, the escape, the unattended refusal, the advertised capability and M15's loop stay; the Approve/Deny card and rmcp's `elicitation` feature go).** `ClientHandler::create_elicitation` (default `Decline`, `rmcp-3.5.1/src/handler/client.rs:179-190`) answered through the `ConfirmationBroker`: an approval card whose server-supplied text goes through `utils::text::escape_hidden*` (and `lib/hidden-chars.ts` in the GUI), a person's Approve or Deny, an unattended turn refused at once (= `Decline`); M15's MRTR loop answers input-required rounds through it; the client **advertises** the elicitation capability (a wire change: a spec-following server sends `elicitation/create` only to a client that advertises it); rmcp's `elicitation` feature added.
- **Rev 2.3 — a form, not an Approve/Deny card (the ZCode audit's A-31; Z1-12, Z5-07).** Rev 2.0's card can answer only accept or decline, but a request whose schema names fields expects them back in `ElicitResult.content` (`rmcp-3.5.1/src/model.rs:3910-3923`), which a card cannot supply. The shape put to the owner, ZCode's built design (its elicitation is form-only, attributed to the call in flight and declined otherwise, live-only; ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:1093-1131, :1543 — the adapter forwards a request that arrives with no call in flight without a trace, and ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/bootstrap/src/zcode-protocol/interaction-broker.ts:255-260 declines it) less its one defect:
  - *Form mode only.* The client advertises `elicitation: { form: { schemaValidation: true } }` and never `url` (`ElicitationCapability`, `rmcp-3.5.1/src/model/capabilities.rs:80-133`); a request in any other mode (`ElicitRequestParams::UrlElicitationParams`, `src/model.rs:3870`) is declined, with a warning in the log. An answer is validated against the requested schema before it is sent.
  - *One input per primitive field* — string, number, integer, boolean, enum — the server's message, field titles, descriptions and enum labels escaped under the approval rule (`utils::text::escape_hidden_line` / `escape_hidden`, `lib/hidden-chars.ts` in the GUI, the hidden-character notice beneath). The narrower answer the owner may take instead: accept only a request whose schema asks for no fields — an accept/decline card returning `content: {}` — and decline every other.
  - *Attributed to one call.* An MRTR round (DU-R4 (v)) is attributed exactly, through M15's loop, which knows the call it runs. A request the server sends during a call (`elicitation/create`) carries no related-request id (`RequestContext`, `rmcp-3.5.1/src/service.rs:1206-1215`), so it is attributed only when exactly one call to that server is in flight, and declined otherwise — ZCode's map of calls in flight is keyed by server name, so two concurrent calls overwrite each other (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:1104, :1125, :1133), which this commit does not copy; with N-MCP-3's concurrent calls such requests are declined more often. With no call in flight, declined.
  - *The clocks held while a person answers.* A request sent mid-call arrives while the call's MCP and sandbox clocks run (30 s and 60 s or 300 s today); the broker extends both by at most its own confirmation timeout — a change to the sandbox's per-call bound, taken with (c)-3, never by a server's notifications (DU-R4 (i)'s sub-question) — so the call stays bounded by the sum; where a clock cannot be held, such a request is declined. An MRTR round is answered between two requests, so only the sandbox's clock runs then. Both holds change the bound the extension design rests on — its §3.2 T3 statement that an in-flight extension call ends within the sandbox's per-call timeout (`tasks/extension-enable-design.md:608-623`) and the disable latency of its §3.2 T4 and of §8's disable route (`:662-663`, `:2195-2197`) — so, if (c)-3 is taken in this shape, M84 amends those texts, as N-MCP-3 leaves them (plan-rev3 §5.4 names its amendment of `:2195-2197`), and that design's revision log in the same commit.
  - *Declined at once* on an unattended turn and on a connector lane, as `Decline` — a chat platform's `/yes` cannot fill a form.
  - *Nothing kept.* The answer is never persisted: the confirmation's persistence arm keeps writing ids alone (`apps/openalpacad/src/events/persistence.rs:301`), as it does for `preview` (CLAUDE.md *Gotchas*).
  - *Not added:* rmcp's `elicitation` feature. At 3.5.1 it gates the server side (`src/service/server.rs:3-12`, `:945-1079`) and one client-builder helper, `ClientCapabilitiesBuilder::enable_elicitation_schema_validation` (`src/model/capabilities.rs:513-528`, compiled under `all(feature = "elicitation", any(feature = "server", feature = "macros"))`, which P19's default features would meet if the feature were added); `create_elicitation` (`src/handler/client.rs:179-191`), the request and result types and the capability types compile without it [D]. M84 does not use that helper: it builds the form capability directly, `enable_elicitation_with(ElicitationCapability::new().with_form(FormElicitationCapability::new().with_schema_validation(true)))` — the builder under `any(server, macros)` (`src/model/capabilities.rs:437-454`), the two types' constructors ungated (`:93-102`, `:132-146`). Rev 2.0 named the feature in error.
  - *The surfaces it touches:* the confirmation request and its answer carry the form's schema and values (the `ToolConfirmationRequested` family; `POST /v1/chat/confirmations/{request_id}`, `apps/openalpacad/src/router.rs:401`; `docs/api` regenerated); the GUI card draws the inputs; the CLI's attended prompt asks one line per field or declines [NE: which]; the connectors decline. Size [NE].
- **Gate.** An elicitation stub per case: a field-less request accepted and declined; a typed form with each primitive, an invalid value refused before sending; a `url`-mode request declined with its warning; an MRTR round attributed through M15's loop; a mid-call request with one call in flight attributed, with two declined; an unattended turn and a connector lane declined at once; the clocks held within the broker's bound; the server's text escaped with a U+202E and a U+200B planted (CLAUDE.md's approval rule) and the hidden-chars suites; no form value in `event_log`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.

**Added in rev 2.3** (the ZCode prior-art audit, `tasks/research/zcode-audit-2026-10-17.md`)

**M85 — `feat(core): MCP images, audio and embedded blobs kept as by-products`** · **Conditional** (DU-R6, an owner question raised in rev 2.3, recommended yes) · **new** · after M14 (§6.3 step 8)
- **Contents.** Today an image becomes a placeholder sentence and audio or an embedded resource its bracketed kind name (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/mcp/bridge.rs:84-104`), so the model can neither read, keep nor `send` what a server returned. Under DU-R6 (b): each image, audio and embedded-blob block of a result (rmcp-3.5.1's `ContentBlock::{Image, Audio, Resource}`, `src/model/content.rs:261-263`; a blob is `ResourceContents::BlobResourceContents`, `src/model/resource.rs:182-188`) is base64-decoded to bytes and saved through `put_tool_artifact` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/builtins/artifact_write.rs:183`) as a by-product of the turn's project or of none — never asked (ST-15) — within `[execution.artifacts] max_artifact_bytes`, its kind taken from its MIME type by the store's existing rule; never image-decoded, transcoded or compressed: a third party's bytes are stored as received (ZCode keeps a third party's large image as an artifact without decoding it, ZCode:docs/mcp-tool-output-budget.md:8-42). The block's text names the artifact's id, its absolute path, the MIME type and the size, so the model can `artifact_read` or `send` it; a block over the limit, or not valid base64, says nothing was saved and why; with no `ToolContext`, or no store, the text says nothing was saved (the audit's A-33). An embedded text resource stays text. **The plumbing, which is the widening DU-R6 presents:** the registry holds no database (`ToolRegistry`, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/registry/mod.rs:438-449`) and `serialize_call_result` is a pure function, so M85 gives the registry's MCP arm (`:832-852`, where the `ToolContext` is) a by-product sink the daemon sets at boot — the database and the daemon config `put_tool_artifact` takes (`artifact_write.rs:183-189`) — and one `pub(crate)` wrapper in `tools/builtins/mod.rs` that takes the `ToolContext` and the sink and calls `scope_from_context` (`artifact_write.rs:61`: the turn's project or none, as `artifact_write` takes it) and then `put_tool_artifact` (`:183`). Both stay `pub(super)`: `artifact_write` is a private module of `builtins` (`tools/builtins/mod.rs:3`), so nothing in it is reachable from the registry at any item visibility, and the wrapper is the one entry point.
- **Gate.** A stub server returning an image, an audio clip, an embedded blob, an over-limit image and invalid base64: three by-products whose bytes equal the decoded input byte for byte, two "nothing saved" lines; a call with no context saves nothing and says so; `artifact_read` of a saved id returns it; the Library lists the three and no card was raised; `cargo test -p openalpaca_core tools::mcp`.
- **Back-out.** Revert.

**M86 — `refactor(llm,plugins): the plugin LLM provider removed, as D-I (revised) rules`** · Required (the owner's D-I (revised), taken 2026-09-23 with the LLM design's D-H…D-N, `tasks/llm-simplify/design.md:18`, an untracked file; recorded in `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §3.7's plugin-provider line and §4.1) · **new** · after N-PLUG-2 and P38, at the head of §6.3's step 7, before M58 (e)
- **Why here (the ZCode audit's A-34, Z2-12).** M58 (e) would otherwise convert `PluginLlmProvider` to dynosaur — work on code a taken decision deletes. D-I was never scheduled: it sat in the paused LLM design (`tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.1).
- **Contents.** Deleted: `PluginLlmProvider` (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/bridge/provider_bridge.rs`, 255 lines, with its re-exports `bridge/mod.rs:11` and `lib.rs:13`), `PluginManager::get_plugin_provider` (`manager.rs:2439-2459`), the `provider/info` probe (`manager.rs:1263-1294`) and the state only it fills (`registered_provider`, `registered_models`), `ProviderType::Plugin(String)` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/key_pool/mod.rs:16`, its `Display` arm `:48`) and the two `plugin:` parsers (`config/llm_config/mod.rs:29-31`, `config/settings_service.rs:1284-1286`). Kept: the manifest's `types.provider` key — a plugin that sets it installs and approves, its tools, skills and agents work, and it gets D-I's one warning in the validate dry run, the install preview and the log. N-PLUG-2 (before the cut, §6.3 step 1a) builds that warning, so M86 lands after it and rewrites its string: N-PLUG-2 says the provider bridge is not wired, which stops being true once the bridge is deleted. M86's sentence is true when it lands: the plugin provider type is removed, and LLM providers are the built-in ones, configured in `llm.toml` or in Settings → Models & keys. D-I's own wording — LLM providers are added as compatible services in Settings — names D-J/D-K's surface, which does not exist (the LLM design is paused in section 1 of 5 with nothing implemented, `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.1); the commit that builds D-K's surface changes the sentence to it. The extension row's `provider` field (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/extensions/ledger.rs:182`, filled at `manager.rs:1799`) then reads `null` on every row; it stays in the wire shape beside its pair `connector` until Z2-12's connector half is answered, and under deletion both leave `GET /v1/extensions` in one change, presented there. **One behaviour D-I does not spell — the plan's reading, an owner question of M86's own, pending and written for both answers:** a `[providers."plugin:<name>"]` section in a hand-written `llm.toml` — accepted today and never callable — would after the deletion meet `LlmError::UnknownProvider` (`config/llm_config/router_builder.rs:61-62`) and, unless it says `enabled = false` (`:57-59`), stop the router building. D-I says to delete the `plugin:` parsing and says nothing of `llm.toml`; its "not refused" is about the plugin's install. *Options:* **(a)** M86 skips such a section with one warning, every other unknown name keeping its refusal — a recognizer of the prefix kept for the warning alone; **(b)** D-I's letter — no recognizer, the section refused like any unknown name, so the router does not build until the user deletes it. *Recommendation:* **(a)**: a section that never did anything should not stop every model at the next start. A test pins the answer taken: under (a) the warn-and-skip and `[providers.bogus]`'s refusal, under (b) both refusals. The commit body states that this is D-I applied, and names every place a `ProviderType` is written (it derives `Serialize`), showing none holds a plugin variant.
- **Gate.** `rg -n 'PluginLlmProvider|get_plugin_provider|ProviderType::Plugin|registered_provider' crates apps` → 0, and `"plugin:"` left only in the warn-and-skip and its test under (a), and nowhere in `crates/openalpaca_llm` under (b) (its three sites today — the two parsers and the `Display` arm — all go); a fixture plugin with `types.provider = true` installs, approves and registers its tools, with M86's sentence in the three places and N-PLUG-2's "not wired" in none; an `llm.toml` with an enabled `plugin:` section builds the router with one warning under (a), or is refused naming the section under (b), and one with `[providers.bogus]` still refuses; `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins -p openalpaca_llm`; `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check` (regenerated if a public item's doc changed); CLAUDE.md's *Extensibility* sentence on the unwired provider bridge updated in the same commit (docs that cite a changed line change with it), its connector half left to Z2-12's pending row.
- **Back-out.** Revert.

**Phase 4 — the record**

### 6.3 Interleaving with Part I and Part II

**Rev 2.2 — one order (SF-16).** Rev 2.0 gave the interlocks pairwise (M18 before Q05; M19/M20 after Q06; Q11 before M74; Q13 before M76; Q18 before M68) and listed Part II and Part III as separate sequences. The review found what that left open: M64 was placed by Part I and Part III anchors alone while Part II's Q04, Q06 and Q11–Q14 cite `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` ranges; M65's warnings gate binds every later commit; E5 is split by M76's Export click; Q11 was placed three ways across the documents; M77 needs the `opener` dependency M79 adds. Below is the one order. `tasks/todo.md` Chunk 25 carries it, copied from this rev; plan-rev3 §7.7 (rev 3.13) names this list the order of record, gives Part II's places in it and keeps no second copy (SF-16). Part I's own commits keep plan-rev3 §5.3's phase order; only the places of Part II's and Part III's commits in it are shown.

*Inside Part I, before P38*

1. M01, M02 (`f54dfac1`), M03 (`a3d3d5ae`) and M44 (`1641ced0`) — landed.
1a. **Before the cut** (rev 2.4: the cut is at or after 2026-10-21T19:53Z, 12:53 PDT — D1 (a); rev 2.3; plan-rev3 rev 3.14's §5.4, its first row — the ZCode audit's fixes that touch code a later commit rewrites, most of them files P36's prep `ac6db26c` edits, and N-MCP-2, which P19's and P19b's gates need; none moves a version, so DU-23 permits them as it did Phase 0, and the cut waits for none of them): **N-MCP-2** (the audit's A-13: the MCP HTTP client with a connect bound and an idle read bound and no total timeout, which today cuts every streamed body at 30 s, `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/http.rs:96-98`; and a refused redirect, always, as its own typed, non-retriable error naming the target origin — under D6's no-change answer the only refused redirect is a chain past ten hops. Which redirects are refused — cross-origin ones, or every redirect for a server with auth or `extra_headers` — is the review's D6, pending; its policy rides in this commit if it is answered first, since both edit `http.rs`); **N-LEAD-1** first of the lead set (a lead's run cancels the subagents it leaves running — today the run's token is removed without being cancelled, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/orchestrator/dispatcher/lead_agent.rs:1068`, and orphaned subagents keep spending outside every cap, writing and prompting), then **N-LEAD-2** (a spawn queues under the lead's bound; its permit wait in M60's `run_until_cancelled` form, which tokio-util 0.7.19 in the lock already has), N-LEAD-3, N-LEAD-4, N-LEAD-5; N-TOOL-1, N-TOOL-2, N-TOOL-4 (after N-TOOL-1), N-TOOL-5, N-TOOL-7; **N-SKILL-1** (before P36 = M07, which edits `skill/catalog/mod.rs`), **N-SKILL-2** (before P30, whose gate then adds "no new skill diagnostic over the E6 corpus", the audit's A-07, and before M36), N-SKILL-4; **N-PLUG-2** (D-I (revised)'s warning half, so before M86), N-PLUG-4, **N-PLUG-6** (before any plugin-surface refactor — M86 and M58 (a–d) here; the skip rule itself is the audit's B-05, pending); N-DAEMON-1 (`main.rs` is in P41's prep); N-STORE-1 (`files_types.rs` is in P17's prep, and Q05 later moves the store's path code). None rewrites a region the G-base driver's patches edit; one that only shifts lines in a patched file re-runs `gbase.sh --dry-run` green before the run of record (plan-rev3 §5.4, rule (b)). One not ready by the cut lands in the wave before the commit its place names (P36 for most, P30 for N-SKILL-2 and N-SKILL-4, P41 for N-DAEMON-1, P17 for N-STORE-1) — a plan-rev3 commit then, measured by its gates (§3.5 item 14) — never as a reason to delay the cut.

**Why these places**, beyond rev 2.0's interlocks (all kept): Part III's source commits follow P38 so that G-lint's triple counts, the fmt hunk counts and P31–P33's CSS-hash and dist-byte anchors stay as measured (M04/M07 are P36 itself; M63 touches no source); M57 first because it is a defect; Part II's shell work early, where plan-rev3 §7.7 puts it, so that M74's command list and M76's typed errors are written on the final shell; M18 before Q05, which moves `store/mod.rs`'s path code; M19/M20 after Q06, which moves `store/projects.rs`'s reader and `store/reveal.rs`; Q07 before the GUI commits, so their tests run on `FixtureHost`; M68 leaves the ES year to Q18b (M68's entry, C-19/E-13); Q11 before M74 (the command tests; the capability's refusal is G-bundle leg A's); Q13 before M76 (whose `Finished` event the TS side reads); M79 before M77 (the shell's `opener`, D-18); M64 after every anchored commit and before M45–M53 (D-08); M65 after M07, M08, M39, M45–M52 and M64, its gate including CI's ubuntu job (SF-28).

**Rev 2.3 — why the audit's places.** The thirty fixes are plan-rev3's commits, not Part III's: their entries are its §5.4 (rev 3.14), with a test that fails on the parent and a place line each, and this list gives them those places because it is the order of record (plan-rev3 §7.7). The table §5.4 derives is taken whole. **(a)** A fix that touches code a later commit rewrites lands before that commit, which is then written over it — for most, a file P36's prep edits (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs`, `tools/registry/mod.rs`, `orchestrator/skill/catalog/mod.rs`, `providers/anthropic/request.rs`, `session_log/record.rs`, `query_handler/simple_query_handler.rs`, `openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs`), so before the cut (step 1a), and P36's prep is rewritten onto the tip in any case (plan-rev3 §5.1 item 10). **(b)** A fix that rewrites a region a G-base patch edits — N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3, N-MCP-4 in `client.rs` and `bridge.rs` — lands after the driver's run of record, at Phase G's head before P19 (step 2), so the run of record replays the measured wave unchanged. **(c)** A fix that needs an earlier commit follows it (N-MCP-5 and N-PLUG-5 need P19's rmcp 3.5, N-GUI-1 needs Q07), and one in no commit's way lands after P38 (steps 3a, 6b, 8 and 11). **(d)** The audit's orderings against Part III hold in it: N-SKILL-1 before M07, N-SKILL-2 before P30 and M36, N-PLUG-1 before M58 (a), N-PLUG-6 before M86 and M58 (a–d), N-PLUG-2 before M86. M86 heads the `async-trait` series so that no trait is converted over an implementation D-I deletes; M85 follows M14. The pending halves — D6 at N-MCP-2, B-05 at N-PLUG-6, B-11 at N-TOOL-1, B-15 at N-LEAD-1, B-14 at N-DAEMON-1 — block no place: each fix lands its own half.

- **Decisions.** None of the re-answered DUs blocks a commit. M70, M83, M84, Q18a and Q18b wait for the owner's answers to DU-W2, DU-R4 (iii), DU-R4 (ii) with T35, and (c)-2; owner question (c)-5 is open and M74, M76 and M78 are written under its (a) (§5.4). The cut instant and Rust 1.99.1 — the review's D1 and D3, both taken 2026-10-17 as (a) (rev 2.4) — decide Part I's dates and the pinned toolchain, not this order; P03b's place is Part I's (step 2), and the second `cargo clean` after it, presented to the owner (§5.1's DU-M4), moves no commit. M57's own question — whether the key layer retries a request timeout, pending (M57's entry) — decides one row of its retry table and its bound, not its place; M57 lands once it is answered. **Rev 2.3:** M85 waits for DU-R6, and M84 for DU-R4 (ii) with T35 in its rev 2.3 shape; DU-R4 (i)'s, DU-R3's and DU-D3's sub-questions move no commit — answered before M15, M65 or the audit's A-12, each rides in that commit.
- **What rev 2.2 replaced.** Rev 2.1's bullets here — before or beside Part I, inside Part I, after P38, against Part II, and rev 2.0's places for the new commits — are replaced by the list above; every interlock they named holds in it, and git keeps their text. One stale word in them: P36 (M04 + M07) is **Required** since plan-rev3 rev 3.9, where rev 2.1 still called M07 "Recommended, work" (D-14).

## 7. Verification

**Per commit** — the gate in its §6.1 entry, run before the `Verified:` line is written.

**At the end of each phase, and at the end of the programme** (every live daemon under `scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb` from its first launch, on an isolated store; no run against `~/.openalpaca`):

- **V1 — G-CI** (plan-rev3 §5.2): `cargo build --locked`, `cargo test --locked --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui` (0 failed; the `managers::mcp` flake only if the module passes on rerun; `sleep 3600` orphans killed), `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets`; the four bun gates; `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check`.
- **V2 — G-lint by the corrected method** (every primary span under `crates/` or `apps/`; `SP/tools/count_clippy_fixed.py` or `recount_indep.py`): **0** after M07 (rev 2.1, measured in prep; rev 2.0's 18 / 17 counted the allows and judgement items M07 takes); 0 under `-W clippy::manual_is_variant_and` after M06, `-W clippy::manual_let_else` after M08, `-W clippy::uninlined_format_args` and `-W clippy::redundant_closure_for_method_calls` per crate after M45–M53; pedantic `map_unwrap_or` ≈ 181 after M06; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` adds no hunk at any commit, compared hunk by hunk against the parent (plan-rev3 G-lint's rule, its rev 3.12); 0 `unfulfilled_lint_expectations` under every selection M39's gate names after M39; **and the two compiles today's 1.99 runs left out** (§2.4), both 0 `deprecated` and 0 new triples, under the pinned toolchain: `cargo clippy -p openalpaca_core --features live-eval --all-targets --locked` and, with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=target/gui` after `bun run build`, `cargo clippy -p openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked`.
- **V3 — the GUI**: the parent's test count green, 0 failed (121 files / 1,551 tests at `1379b184`); knip "Unused files" 0 after M10; the test wall recorded before and after M11; `grep -c 'as string' src/hooks/*.ts` down by M42's count; after M43 the oxc compiler's `reportDiagnostics` 0 on a worktree export with P33 (`react({ compiler: { reportDiagnostics: true } })`, the export discarded) and `approval-card-returns.test.tsx` and the `useChat` suites green — under DU-D1 (c) the lint's `refs`/`set-state-in-effect`/`exhaustive-deps` 0 as well.
- **V4 — the target directory and the build-dir, re-measured once** after M01–M03 and M63 are in force (rev 2.2, MF-5: with `build-dir` configured, `du -sh target` sees only the final artifacts, so the two are measured apart). In a detached worktree of the tip with a fresh `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` **and** `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` of its own (two sibling directories under the worktree's scratch root), run steps 1–3 and 11 of §1.2 with the session's environment (incremental off), recording after each step `du -sk` and the file count of each directory alone and of both in one `du -sk <target-dir> <build-dir>` call (cargo hard-links a final artifact from the build-dir, so two separate sums count it twice) — expect the combined step-3 state ≈ B's 4.2 GiB / 5,391 paths, almost all of it in the build-dir, and step 11's edit to add 0 paths (B: 12,365 → 12,365) [M: `attr-B-sizes.tsv`]; then step 5 (`cargo check -p openalpaca_gui`) with both variables at `<worktree>/target/gui` (M63's pairing for the shell), its tower recorded apart (expect ≥ 2.73 GiB once Q02's sidecar build also lands there); `$CARGO_HOME/build` listed before and after — unchanged (absent on this machine on 2026-10-17 [M]); the worktree and both directories deleted afterwards. On the owner's machine M01's threshold watches both `target/` and the repository's build-dir, which `cargo metadata --format-version 1 --no-deps` reports as `build_directory` [M: cargo 1.93.0]. A re-measure of the owner's 147.6 GiB is not possible (it is gone).
- **V5 — behaviour pins**: every `thiserror` golden test (M19–M38) green; the refusal sentences quoted in `docs/*.md` unchanged (`git grep -F` per variant text); `a_by_product_result_is_pinned_whole_in_five_parts`, `every_builtin_goes_to_openai_with_strict_only_where_strict_mode_accepts_it`, `needs_escape_is_the_gui_table` and the hidden-chars suites green (the settled rules §3.5 names).
- **V6 — M17 by hand** (if taken): a second `openalpacad` started against a store whose first daemon holds the lock is refused with the new wording and exits; `openalpaca config reset --factory`'s `claim_store` refuses while a daemon runs; the CLI's cross-process `LockHolder` test green.
- **V7 — M14/M15 against a real MCP server** (P19's round trip, extended): a tool whose result carries only `structured_content` reaches the model as its JSON; a cancelled call shows `notifications/cancelled` in the server's log; a timed-out call likewise. **Rev 2.3:** a call cut at the sandbox's deadline, one ended by a run's `/cancel` and one cut by rmcp's own timeout each show exactly one `notifications/cancelled` for its request id; under DU-R6 (b), an image a tool returns becomes a by-product the model reads back with `artifact_read`.

## Appendix B — Open questions nobody could answer

1. Does the owner (or an editor on another machine) run rust-analyzer in this repository? On this machine no rust-analyzer component or editor extension exists, so step 10's +1.33 GiB per all-targets check is probably not a driver here (§1.11).
2. Do agent sessions build in git worktrees of this repository, and with which toolchains and feature sets? Decides whether a shared build-dir (DU-M5 c) would deduplicate anything.
3. How many `Cargo.lock` states and toolchains had the owner's 147.6 GiB target seen, and when was it last cleaned? "About six documented sets" is a ratio, not a history.
4. Which agent runtimes besides Claude Code are used here, and do they read `.claude/settings.json`'s `env` block (DU-M2 a)? Does the owner's installed Claude Code build match the page fetched today?
5. `codegen-units = 16`'s effect on `openalpaca_core`'s and the daemon's edit-rebuild time (DU-M6 d; measured ≤ 1 s on storage only).
6. **Partly closed in rev 1.1 by reading** [D: `rmcp-3.5.0/src/service/client.rs:1982-2004`, `:2098-2130`]: `call_tool_with_mrtr_max_rounds` answers each `InputRequiredResult` by dispatching every `InputRequest` to the local handler — an `Elicitation` to `create_elicitation`, whose default `ElicitResult { action: Decline }` is serialized and sent back to the server as that round's input response — and retries, up to 10 rounds, after which it returns `InputRequiredRoundsExceeded`; a result with neither input requests nor `request_state` is `UnexpectedResponse`. What remains [NE] is the **server's** side: whether a server completes, errors or asks again after a Decline — P19's real-server round trip answers it for the servers in use. The choice between the raw path and the loop is DU-R4 (v).
7. Do `flock` and `fcntl` locks interact on macOS? The local `man 2 flock` is silent; Linux's page says they do not. Relevant to M17's mixed-build window only across the "keep the builds apart" line.
8. Which dependency parent brings `proc-macro-error2 2.0.1` (the E0365 future-incompat note), and does the wave's lock drop it? `cargo tree -i` was not run by either Rust group; plan-rev3's re-cut audit still lists it as a warning, so probably not.
9. Does the `pool: 'threads'` gain (M11) hold on `ubuntu-latest`, where the CI gate runs? Measured on macOS only (Node 24.15.0, load 10–21).
10. **Rev 2.0: no longer decides DU-W1** (re-answered (b) under the owner's rule; plan-rev3's G4 measures on the compiled build). Is there any user-visible render slowness in the app today? If not, DU-W1 has no benefit to weigh; M43 lands anyway as Rules-of-React hygiene (rev 1.1).
11. **Answered by the owner's rule (rev 2.0):** yes — oxlint + oxlint-tsgolint (M66) and knip (M67), with the Rust gate (M65). Does the owner want JS gates at all (ESLint with `recommended-latest`, knip)? One yes/no decides DU-D1 and DU-D2 together — and whether the clippy gate half of DU-17 (DU-R3) reopens with them; M43 no longer hangs on it.
12. For DU-W6: should `minimumReleaseAge` bind the developer's own `bun add` (it does, in `bunfig.toml`; the key is Bun 1.3's, so the owner's 1.3.13 honours it [Docs: the 1.3 release post]) or only CI (then a workflow step)?
13. The three `outline-none` sites (`CommandPalette.tsx:178`, `Resizer.tsx:125`, `Composer.tsx:452`): in Tailwind 4 they remove the outline where v3's `outline-none` (now `outline-hidden`) kept a forced-colors ring — an accessibility intent to confirm with the GUI owner (§4.4).
14. Does the MCP specification ask every server to mirror `structured_content` into a text block (the compatibility practice rmcp's own `CallToolResult::structured` follows), and as a MUST or a SHOULD? [NE] from the spec, [D] from rmcp. Since rev 1.1 it no longer decides M14's rule — "append unless a text block already parses to the same JSON" is right under either answer — but it is read before M14 so the commit body can cite the clause.
15. The writer of the single `~/.bun/install/cache` manifest stamped 01:22:46 local (no logged install header matches it); and whether the owner wants the 4,869 files the deprecated-inventory group's `bun add`/`bun x` left in `~/.bun/install/cache` removed (nobody did, by rule).
16. **Rev 2.0: the `if let` half is M62's survey (50 candidate arms, not 26).** Manual `Eq` impls against the 1.95 `assert_receiver_is_total_eq` deprecation (not checked); the 24 unread `if let`-opening match arms (likely not guards).
17. **Rev 2.0: `defer` is M61 (its gate the byte-identical `--help`); `NoContent` is not an older form (one site, the same response).** `clap` 4.6.7's `#[command(defer = true)]` startup benefit for the CLI (unmeasured); axum `NoContent` candidates among the routes (not counted).
18. Whether `break-words` disappears in a later Tailwind minor (only its docs removal and the 4.1.15 migration entry were read, the latter through a mirrored PR).
19. **Rev 2.0: M69 re-measures on `@babel/core` 7.29.7 with the logger on; the run's Babel version was unrecorded and likely 8.0.7, under which compiler 1.0.0 silently skips destructured-default components.** The exact decomposition of the Babel host's skip set (its preset prints no diagnostics) and why its JS is 832,166 B against the oxc host's 877,608 B (both against the same base, today's Vite-8-alone 742,230 B).
20. Whether any package in `bun.lock` is marked deprecated in the npm registry (the npm analogue of `serde_yaml 0.9.34+deprecated`): nobody established that Bun 1.4.2 prints the registry flag at install, and the lock carries no such field, so "0 `deprecat` lines in 45 install logs" proves nothing; a one-off `npm view <name>@<version> deprecated` (or `bun pm view`) over the lock's packages closes it — offered, not a gate (§2.4).
21. **Answered (rev 2.0):** no — `Swatinem/rust-cache` at `6323deb1` caches the workspaces' `target/` only (`src/config.ts:142-150`, `:274-276` [D]); M63 names the build-dir in `cache-directories`. Whether `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2` (`ci.yml:26`) follows a `build.build-dir` outside `target/`; decides DU-M5 (b)'s CI cost (§1.10).
22. When will a `babel-plugin-react-compiler` release carry the Babel 8 fixes (#36956/#37492/#37558), so that M69's held `@babel/core` 7.29.7 can move?
23. How large is `jsonschema`'s graph with default features off, and what are its feature names (M82)? *Rev 2.4, partly:* at D1 (a)'s cut 0.58.6's required dependencies resolve to eligible releases with no RustSec match, one level deep; the transitive graph (strum_macros, the uuid-simd and vsimd crates among it) and the feature names stay open [the compatibility report §2.3, §7].
24. Which MCP servers the owner uses would exercise M15's MRTR loop, M84's elicitation or M83's tasks at all?
25. Does dynosaur's expansion measurably slow `BuiltInTool`'s 66 impls to compile (M58 (f))?
26. (rev 2.3) Does `clippy::allow_attributes_without_reason` also read `#[expect]` at the pinned toolchain, so that DU-R3's ratchet, if taken, holds M39's reasons as well as keeping bare `#[allow]` out?
27. (rev 2.3) How does the CLI answer M84's form on an attended turn — one prompt line per field, or a decline with a pointer to the app? Decides M84's size, not its shape.
28. (rev 2.3) cargo-nextest (the ZCode audit's Z7-05, A-45 — recorded, not scheduled; plan-rev3 §5.1 item 6b names it the later structural fix for G-base's suite verdict): do its process-per-test runs, test groups and leak detection hold on the version that would be pinned? None of it is verified (the audit's §7).
